  - **Context:** Play test 2026-10-21: "to Everus Harbor aboy" (47) and "… abov" (48), the line broke inside "above"; "Max size willb 1 SCU" missed the size phrase; capture 48 has no drop-off list, but its prose says "taken to a freight elevator at Everus Harbor above Hurston".
  - **Decision:** (1) A destination without a name end loses a last word that is the start of "above" from two characters on, or from four characters on with one misread character ("aboy"); the name then counts as complete. Shorter words with a misread character stay ("St"). (2) Completion gets a second source: "**to** a freight elevator at *name*" in the prose, any verb ("Deliver to", "taken to"), the name ending before " at", " above" or ".". "**from** a freight elevator" is a pickup and never completes. Order: drop-off list, then prose, then the other deliveries of the capture. This revises "prose no longer completes" above. (3) The size phrase allows up to two words of any spelling after "Max size" and needs only "bigger than" of the second phrase; in its number, O counts as 0 and I, L, | as 1 (the game prints it in blue, where OCR misses digits). A corrected number that is no crate size counts as missing.
  - **Counter-argument:** A station whose name ends in "Ab…" or "Abo…" would lose its last word. "to a freight elevator at" could appear in prose for a place that is not a destination; completion still needs a fragment that is a character prefix of exactly one such name. The digit correction can turn noise into a valid size: only behind the two phrases, and only to 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 24, 32.
  - **Consequence:** 47 and 48 both become "Everus Harbor"; the Silicon capture reads "max 1 SCU". Our own overlay in ContractTracker's capture region ("MISC Hull A", "No plan. Open t…") gives no commodity or destination, because only "Deliver …/N SCU of … to …" lines do; the README asks to keep the overlay out of the region anyway.
- **Addendum (B1): one place, one stop.** Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Context:** Play test 2026-10-24 (Covalex, Hull A): "Thundering Express" and "Thundering Express Station", "Melodic Fields" and "Melodic Fields Station" were stops of their own, with wrong "shared by" hints. Diagnosis on capture 9–12 (one capture, four objectives): both names come from the same raw text, not from two fields. Every delivery line is cut before "Station"; "to Melodic Fieids" (l read as i) is no character prefix of the list's "Melodic Fields Station", and the list entry "Freight alevator at Thundering Express Station" didn't count as one (`listedDestinations` 2 of 3). Across captures, nothing set a fragment equal to its full name (the counter-argument above: "a later complete capture … does not match it").
  - **Decision:** (1) **Parser:** completion compares in the anchor form (`ContractText.AnchorForm`, moved from the parser: no spaces, upper case, I/L/1/| and O/0 alike) and takes the full name's spelling; the first letter of "elevator" may be misread or lost in list entries and prose. (2) **Names of one place** (`DestinationNames` in `CargoPlan.Import`): equal in the anchor form after dropping a last word that reads "Station"; the stop keeps the longer name, on a tie the first. Applied where names enter the plan: the reader's stops, the sync (`Unify` before `ContractSync.Apply`: the plan's stop is renamed when a capture brings the longer name, a capture takes the plan's name otherwise, so new crates join the rack of their place), the editor's rows, and every plan the overlay shows (`ShowPlan`: a plan from before B1 is merged once, `PlanSaver` writes it back). A renamed stop gives the notice "Stops merged: A → B" (`NoticeSource.Stops`), until the next plan or the next sync that takes something in without a merge; a read without news leaves it, or the start's first read would take the loading notice away. (3) **Core renames, it doesn't judge:** `LoadPlanner.RenameStop(saved, ship, from, to)` renames missions and crates together, a mission with both names keeps one stop; nothing moves, ticks stay, hints are derived anew. Behind the entry points, names compare as before (ordinal, ignoring case): "the comparison doesn't normalise" still holds there. The child rows were considered as a fourth completion source and rejected: in 9–12 they hold OCR noise that starts with the fragment ("Thundering Express Lagrange point …"), in 28/29 two candidates.
  - **Counter-argument:** Two rules instead of one, and the second is a heuristic. The i/l and o/0 folding applies to typed names too: two real stations differing only there would become one (none known). "X" and "X Station" are taken as one place everywhere, also where they might not be. `Import` was the ContractTracker reader; now it also holds a rule the editor uses. A renamed stop changes its legend line and possibly its colour.
  - **Consequence:** Capture 9–12 gives 3 stops (CLI `contracts`); the plan of 23 October loads with "Melodic Fieids" merged into "Melodic Fields Station". Fragments without any full name in the text stay ("Thundering Express" in 28/29) until a capture or a typed row brings the full name. Mission ids, the watermark and the duplicate rule are untouched: missions come and go by ContractTracker ids, and after `Unify` the duplicate key sees one name per place.

## D41 – Incremental placement: new missions into free cells, existing crates fixed

- **Context:** Contracts are accepted one by one (F3 per contract, D39 addendum); planning again lays out every crate anew and clears ticks (T3.8b). Spike T5.4: `LoadPlanner.Plan` always starts from empty racks and chooses racks globally (a DP over all stops), so a fifth stop moves the second into rack 1. `CrateLayout.Place` already lays crates into a rack's free cells around fixed ones, with fill order and support rule (used by `MoveStop`). From the game (Martin): at the pickup elevator the player selects whole missions to load; delivered crates are off the ship; a crate with another on top along the stack axis can't be taken away; ContractTracker does nothing on Abandon.
- **Options:** (a) `PlaceInto` in Core: append the new crates, existing ones untouched by construction; rack choice: first the racks of a stop with the same destination (one handover, D42), then the first empty rack in rack order, then the first rack with room, then crate by crate in rack order; layout by `CrateLayout.Place`. (b) Re-plan with frozen crates as a constraint: the DP cuts one crate line into contiguous SCU segments per rack; frozen crates break that (partial racks, free space is a shape, not a number), which makes it a second planner, and it gains nothing where fixed crates block the shape. (c) Full re-plan: ruled out (T3.8b, loses positions and ticks).
- **Decision:** (a). **Cells of delivered crates are free** (D24 addendum). **New crates never lie on top of an earlier stop's crate along the stack axis,** enforced by `CrateLayout.Place` (D24 addendum, T5.4a). ContractTracker missions bring their order from the contract text, first appearance as in the editor (D19); a contract that says "ANY ORDER" (Martin) leaves the route to the player in the editor. A new mission joins the route at its end, so its crates rest on nothing of another destination: that follows from the rule, it is no rule of its own. **Doesn't fit → the mission enters the plan without crates**, the legend says "⚠ doesn't fit (16 SCU, 8 free) – tick to drop" (or names the shape), and the watermark rises; ticking drops the mission from the plan, deleting it in the editor does too. No partial placement: the elevator hands out whole missions, half a mission is no action in the game. A notice alone would lose the contract once the watermark passes it, or repeat forever, since ContractTracker keeps an abandoned contract. Spike T5.4, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** Greedy rack choice fragments: four stops of 12 SCU (max 4) leave 4 SCU free in every rack, 16 SCU in all, yet no 8 SCU crate fits; a full re-plan fits a 16 SCU mission in 8 SCU crates by moving stops into shared racks. "First stop in rack 1" erodes with every capture, and shared/spans hints become more common. A second rack-choice rule lives next to `Partition`. An unplaced mission is a new plan state. **The tick gets a second meaning:** on a placed stop it says "delivered", on an unplaced mission "abandoned, out of the plan"; without "tick to drop" in the legend the player doesn't find the gesture, or ticks out of habit and loses the mission. Ticking back stops being an undo once a delivered stop's cells are reused.
- **Consequence:** Needs the mission identity of D42 first (plan schema 3), which also carries the unplaced state; dropping an unplaced mission is per mission, not per stop. T5.4a (stacking rule) comes before T5.4. When an unplaced mission is placed after space frees up is part of T5.4's planning. Re-planning in the editor stays the explicit way out when nothing is loaded yet or the route changes.

- **Addendum (T5.4): when, where, what "deleted" means.** Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Retry at every sync, not after a tick.** Waiting missions are tried first, in mission order, whenever a sync runs (watcher or button); a tick alone changes nothing, because loading into freed cells is the player's decision (D24 addendum). The legend's "doesn't fit" text is the same check as the placement (`LoadPlanner.PlaceProblem`, the D25 pattern).
  - **Rack choice looks at cargo on board:** "racks of the same destination" are racks with crates of it still on board. New crates for such a destination go right behind its crates in the crate list (one handover, its place in the route stays); others are appended, a new visit at the end.
  - **Unload order.** The legend and the stacking rule follow `LoadPlan.UnloadOrder`: a stop ranks by its first crate on board, a delivered one by the start of its last run of crates. `Route` (first appearance) only keeps a stop's colour. So a new contract for a delivered stop comes last in legend and stacking, and no tick moves a line. Correction at approval: the legend follows the unload order, not the route, because the hauler reads it as the route.
  - **Deleted in ContractTracker = every id of the capture gone.** A 2 + 1 split with one row deleted stays. A deleted mission leaves when nothing of it is on board (all ticked, or never placed); otherwise a notice, the plan stays. "Not read" (missing file, busy, other schema) is never "deleted".
  - **Counter-argument:** Space freed by a tick stays unused until the next capture or a click on Sync. Two orders in one plan: a hand-edited file that reorders crates changes the route. A destination visited twice with a removed mission in between merges into one run of crates; fully ticked, its line moves up among the delivered ones.
- **Addendum (B2): no unload order (D64).** `LoadPlan.UnloadOrder` is gone; legend and colours follow the order of first appearance (`Route`), display only. New crates go right behind their destination's crates, also for a ticked destination (no "new visit at the end"), so ticking and new contracts never move a legend line. `PlaceInto` places a mission's stops largest first and takes, of the candidate racks in the order above, the first where no new crate sits on other cargo, else the first that takes it. Its result depends on the order captures come in, by design: a re-plan (B4) gives the order-free plan.
- **Addendum (B3): candidates for moved cargo (D65).** Stage 3b places displaced crates and stage 4 the dragged ones in this order (racks with the destination on board, the first rack with nothing on board, rack order), around the cargo there, per destination first whole into one rack; of these the first without a crate on other cargo wins, as in `PlaceInto`. The target rack is left out; in stage 4 the dragged crate's own rack comes last, so it goes home only when no other rack takes it. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Addendum (K2.2): a ticked stop with new cargo on another ship (D75).** The tick stays with the delivery, a mission's crates for a destination: after a ship change the old visit's cargo stays ticked and off the ship, the new mission's cargo is planned on board. Off-ship cargo ticked back is laid in by this entry's rule (`LayStop`).
- **Addendum (K6.4, D79):** loaded crates are on board like any crate: `PlaceInto` and `RetryUnplaced` lay new missions around them. A mission gone from ContractTracker leaves the plan when nothing of it is planned on board (everything loaded or delivered, or never placed): the loaded cargo of a contract handed in was handed over in the game, and what lay on it settles (D24 addendum). Planned cargo on board keeps the conflict notice. Plan and Re-plan with loaded cargo lay the rest in by this rule, the rest of missions loaded in part first (E1), then missions by SCU, first destination and id. Re-plan keeps the plan as it is when the result would leave more SCU waiting (E2: "Re-plan found no better layout").
  - **Counter-argument:** This rule is history-dependent and fragments sooner than the exact assignment of D64; with loaded cargo it is the only one. Backlog: `RackAssignment` with obstacles (trigger: a hauler's report of fragmentation with loaded cargo).
- **Addendum (D81): the gone part is superseded.** A mission ContractTracker no longer lists stays in the plan marked gone; with `contractTracker.onContractGone` `"remove"` its planned and delivered deliveries leave when it goes, loaded ones never, and later only a mission with nothing planned or loaded leaves. The rule "a deleted mission leaves when nothing of it is on board" (addenda T5.4 and K6.4) no longer holds.

## D42 – Mission identity under the stop (plan schema 3)

- **Context:** T5.4 adds every ContractTracker capture as a mission of its own (D39 addendum); a capture has one or more destinations. The Core's unit is the destination: one `MissionStop` per destination (planner, mission file), ticks per stop (D16), crates without a mission. Removing a mission, marking duplicates, keeping ContractTracker ids and an unplaced mission (D41) need to know which crates belong to which mission. From the game (Martin): the destination elevator takes crates of several missions to the same destination in one handover; the kiosk splits crates within a mission; crates of two missions come up the pickup elevator together, told apart by colour at most.
- **Options:** (a) A mission id on every item and crate; stops stay one per destination; the plan keeps a list of missions (id, ContractTracker ids, hints, unplaced). (b) Stop = mission + destination: a destination may occur twice, in planner, mission file and legend. (c) No identity, removal by matching contents: ruled out, equal crates of two missions can't be told apart (D38: no content key).
- **Decision:** (a). Reason from the game: at the elevator the destination is the unit (one handover, so rack, tick and hints stay per destination); the contract is the unit of the crate mix, of duplicates and of removal. (b) would split one handover over two stops and racks. Spike T5.4, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** `Crate`, the derived placements, the plan file and the editor all grow by a field, and every crate carries an id the player rarely sees. The editor has no missions today: rows entered by hand need a mission too. Ticks stay per stop, so a mission abandoned at a destination another mission still goes to is removed in the editor, not ticked.
- **Consequence:** Plan schema 3 (`missions` with id, `crates` with `mission`); schema 2 is migrated on reading, one mission per stop. Merge refuses crates of different missions, split keeps the mission (D27). How the editor shows and deletes missions is part of T5.4's planning.

- **Addendum (T5.4): the shape of schema 3.** Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Stops under the mission, not an id on every item.** A mission holds its own stops and items (`Mission(Id, Stops, Contract, Unplaced)`); every crate names its mission. Same information as option (a), stored differently: the items of an unplaced mission have no crates, and in one shared stop list they would break the check "crates = items". The one-stop-per-destination view that planner, legend and editor work on is derived (`SavedPlan.Stops`), so (a)'s point stands: one stop, one handover. The embedded mission file of schema 2 goes; the editor's Export stays the way to a mission file.
  - **The watermark is part of the plan** (`contractTrackerWatermark`), held and saved with it, raised only by the sync.
  - **Typed missions are the typed rows of one destination**, as a migrated schema 2 plan has one mission per stop. The editor's Plan keeps ids, captures and the watermark: crates are assigned to the missions holding their kind after planning, since equal crates of two missions are the same crate in the game (D38).
  - **Counter-argument:** Two views of the same items, one stored and one derived, and a route that lives in the crate order rather than in a field. A mission file can no longer be copied out of plan.json.
- **Addendum (T7.4d):** Crates name their item as well as their mission (`Crate.Item`, plan schema 4, D68), so two items of one kind in one mission stay two. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.

## D43 – ContractTracker sync in the overlay

- **Context:** T5.4 takes ContractTracker captures into the plan without a click (D39 addendum). The database changes while ContractTracker writes, checkpoints, starts and exits, and also when we read it ourselves (D38: our read-only connection leaves `-wal` and `-shm`). Who holds the watermark, how a read reaches the plan, and what counts as news needed one answer each.
- **Decision:** **One state, one saver:** the overlay holds one `SavedPlan` (missions, crates, watermark); `Plan` and `Missions` are read from it, and `PlanSaver` saves on its change, so a sync that only adds a mission that didn't fit is saved too. **One handover:** `ContractTrackerSync` watches the database folder (database and `-wal`, created, changed, deleted, renamed; `-shm` ignored), debounces 500 ms, reads on the thread pool (`Task.Run`) and hands the result back on the UI thread to `OverlayViewModel.ApplyContractTracker`, the only way a read reaches the plan; one read at a time, a request during a read runs one more afterwards. The watcher, the editor's **Sync from ContractTracker** and a later hotkey (T5.5) all call the same method. **Diff on two levels:** the file state (existence, length, last write of database and `-wal`) after our last read is kept; an event that leaves it so reads nothing, which drops our own `-wal`/`-shm` events and empty checkpoints. A read without new captures and without a missing id changes nothing (no new scene, which would end a drag, no save); the button always syncs, which also places waiting missions. **Start:** one read after the debounce, so captures made while the overlay was closed come in. **Failures:** a missing database says nothing (T5.5's notice); a file that is no database gives a notice; a plan file that couldn't be loaded pauses the sync until the player plans in the editor, so it isn't overwritten. Deleted contracts with crates on board are named in a notice derived again on every change, so it goes with the tick that settles it. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** Comparing length and write time can miss a change within the file system's time resolution that keeps the length; the next capture catches up, because the watermark hasn't moved. A missing folder at start means no watcher until a restart (the button still reads). The editor reloads its rows when the plan's missions change while its draft is clean; with a changed draft it keeps the edits, adds the rows of new missions (else a Plan would drop a capture the watermark has passed) and says so; a mission dropped meanwhile keeps its rows there and comes back typed with the next Plan.
- **Consequence:** T5.5 adds the path setting and auto-sync off; both go through `ContractTrackerSync` without a second way into the plan.
- **Addendum (D81):** "Deleted contracts with crates on board are named in a notice derived again on every change" is gone: the legend header and the editor say "gone", the sync's notice says once what it did. A read is quiet by `ContractSync.Quiet`, which knows gone and back.

## D44 – ContractTracker settings: path, auto-sync, hotkey; a new database starts over

- **Context:** T5.4 read ContractTracker at its one path and always took captures in (D43). Not every installation keeps the database there, not every hauler wants captures in the plan without a step, and players without ContractTracker should learn where to get it. A database that starts over (reinstall, an update that recreates the file, another path) gives ids from 1 again, below the plan's watermark: without a guard no capture would ever come in again, and nothing would say so.
- **Decision:** settings.json gets an optional `contractTracker` section, `{ "path": null, "autoSync": true }`, and `hotkeys.syncContractTracker`; **schema stays 2** like `legend` and `toolWindowTheme` (D31, D33). `path: null` detects `%AppData%\SCContractTracker\contracts.db`; a path that isn't a full path is corrected to `null` with a notice, the rest applies (D13); a missing file is kept (ContractTracker may come later). The settings window shows the path read-only with **Browse…** (file dialog) and **Detect automatically**, its status ("Found" or "ContractTracker not found – set path or install" with a link to the Nexus page), checked on opening and on change, and **Auto-sync**. Every change goes through `SettingsSession` (saved debounced) and `ContractTrackerSync.Configure` (a new watcher for a new path, a read after the debounce, which also takes in what waited when auto-sync goes on). **Auto-sync off:** the watcher still reads but never changes the plan, deleted contracts included; the notice says "N new contracts in ContractTracker"; the editor's button and the hotkey sync as before. **The hotkey** runs the same manual sync without opening the editor; no default (D17). **Missing database:** the watcher stays silent (players without ContractTracker), the button and the hotkey say where they looked. **New database:** the reader compares the highest id the table ever gave (`sqlite_sequence`, never lowered by deleting rows, also not by F5) with the watermark; below it, it reads from 0 and counts none of the plan's ids as present; Core sets the watermark back to 0 before taking the captures in (`ContractSync.Apply`, `databaseIsNew`), the notice says "ContractTracker database looks new – all contracts imported". Not `MAX(Id)`: it drops when the newest contract is deleted in ContractTracker, and every older one would come in twice. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** An older overlay reads a file with `contractTracker` as broken (strict options); there is no release yet. The status in the window is not live: ContractTracker installed while the window is open shows after reopening it. The settings-to-sync wiring sits in the composition root, a second place next to the session. With auto-sync off, deleted contracts stay in the plan until a manual sync. If an old plan id equals an id of the new database, that old mission counts as present later and gets no deletion notice; the editor deletes it. Without AUTOINCREMENT (a ContractTracker update) a new database is not detected and reads as before. `sqlite_sequence` is SQLite's, not ContractTracker's schema, and is not in the fingerprint.
- **Consequence:** D43's "a missing database says nothing (T5.5's notice)" now means: the section and the manual sync say it, the watcher doesn't. `AppearanceRules` also checks the ContractTracker path, next to the tool window theme; the class name fits less, a rename is its own task. contracttracker-schema.md names `sqlite_sequence`; settings-format.md and the README describe the section.
- **Addendum (K2.3): the player starts over too (D73).** Besides a new database, Discard plan sets the watermark back to 0, so the next sync takes in everything ContractTracker holds.
- **Addendum (K6.5): rows added or changed by hand in a ContractTracker mission.** ContractTracker sometimes misses a destination; added as a typed row it became a mission of its own without the contract's id. The editor's **Add to mission** (Martin, over Add inheriting the selected row's mission or a mission column to pick from) puts a row into the selected row's capture, right below it; a capture's row changed by hand (any input) becomes the player's too. Both are **manual**: `MissionItem.Manual`, `missions[].stops[].items[].manual` in plan schema 7 (no second bump after K6.4), written only as true, an error in schema 6 and in any mission file (the item DTO is shared; a mission file says nothing about ContractTracker, and the editor's exports are all handwork). The Mission column shows "#37–38 ✎"; the legend shows the row under the mission's header as any other. **The sync stays as it is** (Martin): it reads a capture once, so it never overwrote a row anyway; a mission leaves with its manual rows when ContractTracker no longer lists it (and nothing of it is planned on board, D79), and the notice says "Gone from ContractTracker: #37, with 1 row added by hand". A recapture (F4) stays a mission of its own marked "⚠ duplicate?" (D39 addendum); the brief's rule "a manual row ContractTracker later delivers stays manual, notice … now also in ContractTracker" is dropped, since nothing delivers a row of a capture already in the plan. Names stay unified by B1 (`DestinationNames.Unify`, folded, "A = B + Station"): a manual "Melodic Fields" becomes "Melodic Fields Station" when a capture brings the longer name, stays manual, and its mission gets no second stop for the place (tested). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** Since the sync never overwrites, manual is mostly a mark and a format field; what it guards against comes only if the sync ever re-reads captures. A row changed and changed back stays manual: the mark follows the edit, not a comparison. A destination added to a loaded mission is that mission's unloaded rest: without room it makes Plan refuse (D79, E1), the backlog's trigger for the delivery as waiting unit.
- **Addendum (D81):** the section gets `onContractGone` (`"keep"` default, `"remove"`), no schema bump, and a reset for auto-sync and gone contracts. A new database no longer makes every old mission leave: they stay marked gone and detached, so an old plan id equal to a new database's id no longer counts as present (the counter-argument above).
- **Addendum (D82):** the button and the hotkey run the guarded sync too: an unexpected failure gives a notice and a log entry instead of ending the overlay, and a successful one lifts a paused sync.

## D45 – Drags polled from the cursor while captured

- **Context:** In the game, orbit, pan, crate and legend drags froze and jumped on release. Measured with a scratch probe (HwndSource hook): while Star Citizen is the foreground window, no other window gets WM_MOUSEMOVE (0/s, with every window style, ours included), while `GetCursorPos` sees every move at frame rate (≈55/s at 55 fps) and WM_LBUTTONDOWN/UP arrive as usual; with Star Citizen in the background 125 WM_MOUSEMOVE/s. Star Citizen reads the mouse as raw input; window styles play no part. The earlier "1–7 moves/s" (backlog, 2026-10-21) counted WPF's MouseMove events in `OnMouseMove`, not window messages.
- **Decision (A):** While the ship view holds the mouse capture, `CompositionTarget.Rendering` drives the drag once per frame (subscribed on `CaptureMouse`, removed in `OnLostMouseCapture`, duplicates within a frame dropped by `RenderingTime`). The position comes from `GetCursorPos` and `PointFromScreen`, not `Mouse.GetPosition`, which converts WPF's `_lastPosition` from the last mouse message. Mouse moves and the poll go through one `UpdateGesture`, which returns where the position hasn't changed; MouseMove stays for the desktop. A lost up: the poll reads the drag's button with `GetAsyncKeyState` (mapped through `SM_SWAPBUTTON`, it reads physical buttons) and ends the drag like the up. Not `Mouse.LeftButton`: it reads `GetKeyState`, tied to our message queue, and says Released while WPF's mouse device is inactive. Only "armed": `GetAsyncKeyState` returns 0 on failure too (UIPI), so "not down" counts only if the button read as down when the drag started. `SaturationValuePad` keeps plain capture: clicking it activates the settings window, Star Citizen goes behind, messages flow. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** Three P/Invokes more, and up to ~60 polls per second with a render pass every frame – only during a drag. The drag follows the overlay's frame rate, not the mouse. If Star Citizen runs elevated and we don't, the up guard stays off (never armed); the poll still runs.
- **Alternative B, raw input** (`RegisterRawInputDevices` with `RIDEV_INPUTSINK`): rejected as not needed. `GetCursorPos` gives the screen position we need; raw input gives relative deltas we would have to turn into positions, and a second input path next to WPF's.
- **Consequence:** The README's "Known issues" and the backlog item on in-game drag lag go.

## D46 – Release artifact: self-contained win-x64 folder in one ZIP

- **Context:** D4 planned a self-contained single-file EXE plus a framework-dependent one. Measured for the release: framework-dependent 1.8 MB, self-contained 61.9 MB. The overlay and the CLI both ship.
- **Decision:** One publish, self-contained, `win-x64`, no `PublishSingleFile`; App and Cli in the same folder, sharing one runtime, zipped as the only artifact. Self-contained despite the size: our haulers have .NET 8 (ContractTracker), not .NET 10, so the runtime download happens either way – this way without an error dialog. No trimming (WPF can't be trimmed), no ReadyToRun without a startup-time measurement. Only `win-x64`.
- **Counter-argument:** A download 34 times larger than framework-dependent. Runtime patches arrive only with a new release of ours; accepted, the app has no network access. A folder of many files instead of one EXE, where a user can start the wrong one. Windows on ARM runs the x64 build under emulation.
- **Consequence:** D4 is superseded: no second profile, no single file (T6.2). The version comes from the tag (T6.3) and shows in the settings window and `CargoPlan.Cli --version`. Auto-update stays T7.2.

## D47 – settings.json backed up once per run, before the first write

- **Context:** The first save overwrites a broken or hand-edited settings.json (D13, D34) with defaults plus the change; without a copy, a typo costs the player their hotkeys and look. Release precondition (T6.1).
- **Decision:** Before the first write of a run, `SettingsStore.BackUp` copies settings.json byte for byte to `settings.json.bak` (`File.Copy`, overwrite), whether it is JSON or not; no file, no backup. "Once" is a flag in `SettingsSession`, the one owner of the settings per run (D34), not a static: it is set only after the copy succeeded. A failed copy writes nothing, shows "Settings not saved", and the next save tries again – the original is never overwritten without its backup. The load notice for a broken file says "The next save overwrites …, keeping the old file as settings.json.bak". Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** One backup only: the next run's first save replaces it, so a broken file the player ignores for two starts is gone. The guarantee sits in the session, not the store; a second caller of `SettingsStore.Save` would get no backup. A locked `.bak` (an editor holding it) blocks saving until it is released.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `File.Replace` with a backup name in `AtomicFile` – backs up on every save, so the second save of a run loses the original. Backup on loading – writes at every start without a change, against D31. Timestamped backups – the folder grows without limit.
- **Consequence:** Only settings.json; plan.json is out of T6.1's scope. README and settings-format.md name the `.bak`.

## D48 – Publish through build/publish.ps1, no publish profile

- **Context:** D46 wants App and Cli in one self-contained folder. A publish profile (`.pubxml`) belongs to one project, so two projects take two `dotnet publish` calls whatever the profiles say, and a script or two CI steps anyway; a profile of the App can't be used by the Cli (the SDK imports only the project's own `Properties/PublishProfiles/<name>.pubxml`). The `.gitignore` template also ignores `*.pubxml`. Measured: both publishes write 196 same-named files, two of them differ – `WindowsBase.dll` (WPF's in the App, a 16 KB facade in the Cli's runtime) and `SQLitePCLRaw.provider.e_sqlite3.dll` (same version, built for net6.0-windows in the App).
- **Decision:** `build/publish.ps1` is the only place for the publish settings: one argument list (`-c Release -r win-x64 --self-contained true -o out/publish`) for both projects, Cli first, App last so the App's files win; the folder is emptied first, `LICENSE` is copied in. PDBs stay in the folder (no `DebugType=none`): with them an exception in a bug report carries line numbers. `Directory.Build.props` sets `Version` 0.0.0-dev, which the SDK extends with the full commit hash (`0.0.0-dev+<sha>`); the settings footer ("SC-CargoPlan " + it) and `CargoPlan.Cli --version` (it alone) read it from the entry assembly's `AssemblyInformationalVersion`. No `PackageLicenseExpression`: it only acts on `dotnet pack`. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** No "Publish" button in Visual Studio, and a calling order that matters: a plain `dotnet publish` of the Cli after the App breaks the overlay. PowerShell is one more language in the repo. The folder is 143 MB in 416 files (260 at the root, 13 satellite language folders), the ZIP 62 MB.
- **Consequence:** T6.3 calls the script and adds the version parameter; whether the ZIP gets a root folder is T6.3's question. The App icon is in the backlog until there is an `.ico`.
- **Addendum (K5.4): the App icon.** `src/CargoPlan.App/cargoplan.ico` (16 to 256 px) is the App's `<ApplicationIcon>`, a Win32 resource of the exe, so `publish.ps1` has no file to check. No window sets `Icon`: WPF then shows the assembly icon in title bar, taskbar and Alt+Tab ([Window.Icon](https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/api/system.windows.window.icon#remarks)). The source is `docs/images/icon.svg`, a 256 px PNG next to it serves README and Nexus. The logo is Martin's own work and under the repo's MIT licence. The Cli keeps the default icon (a console tool). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** a window that once sets its own `Icon` silently drops out of the shared one; changing the logo means regenerating the `.ico` by hand from the SVG, nothing in the build does it.

## D49 – Release from a tag

- **Context:** A release should need nothing but a tag (T6.3): build, test, ZIP, upload. D48 left the version parameter and the ZIP's root folder open. No MinVer/GitVersion: one more package for what a tag already says.
- **Decision:** `.github/workflows/release.yml` runs on `push` of tags `v*`, on `windows-latest`, with `contents: write`. Its first step strips the "v", checks the rest against the SemVer 2.0 regex of semver.org **without build metadata** (the SDK appends `+<commit>` itself) and requires `docs/release-notes/v<version>.md`, hand-written and committed before the tag; either missing → error, nothing built, no release. A hyphen (prerelease suffix) marks the release as prerelease. Build gets `-p:Version` already, test runs on that build, `./build/publish.ps1 -Version` publishes. The script writes `out/publish/SC-CargoPlan/` and `SC-CargoPlan-v<version>-win-x64.zip` with the root folder `SC-CargoPlan/` (260 loose files would bury the EXE), entries with forward slashes written one by one (Windows PowerShell 5.1's `Compress-Archive` and `ZipFile.CreateFromDirectory` write backslashes, pwsh 7 doesn't), plus a `.sha256` in `sha256sum` format. ZIP and hash in the script, not the workflow: a local run gives the released artifact's content and layout, checkable before the tag; not byte-equal, the compression differs (rc.1: 60.7 MB in CI, 58.7 MB locally, same 260 files; likely the runner's .NET against Windows PowerShell 5.1's .NET Framework, not investigated). Without `-Version` the ZIP is named after MSBuild's `Version` (0.0.0-dev). `softprops/action-gh-release` v3.0.3 is pinned to its commit, with `body_path` and `fail_on_unmatched_files`. The first tag is `v0.1.0-rc.1`; it stays as a prerelease until `v0.1.0` (after T6.5, which checks the README against that build), then release and tag go. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** A valid SemVer tag with build metadata is refused. The tested build isn't byte-equal to the shipped one: `publish` builds for `win-x64` again, from the same commit and version. `publish.ps1` now zips too, every local run writes ~60 MB more, and the App folder moved from `out/publish` to `out/publish/SC-CargoPlan`. A pinned action gets no fixes by itself. The regex lives only in the workflow; `publish.ps1 -Version 0.1` runs locally unchecked. A deleted rc tag lives on in clones that fetched it.
- **Consequence:** `ci.yml` is unchanged (it never runs for tags). Release notes are a file per version in `docs/release-notes/`. The README's download section is T6.5.
- **Addendum (T6.4):** "A pinned action gets no fixes by itself" is too strong: Dependabot's github-actions updater also scans `release.yml` (the grouped PR #17 bumped checkout and setup-dotnet there) and, according to community sources, proposes a new commit with the version comment for an action pinned to a SHA. Not yet observed here: v3.0.3 is still softprops' latest release. Changes to `release.yml` are not tested by CI; the next tag runs them first.

## D50 – English-only satellite resources

- **Context:** The self-contained publish carried 13 language folders (cs … zh-Hant) of 12 WPF resource assemblies each, 156 of 416 files, from the WPF runtime pack. Our UI is English.
- **Decision:** `Directory.Build.props` sets `SatelliteResourceLanguages` to `en`. The docs name NuGet packages only, but the SDK's `ResolveRuntimePackAssets` filters runtime pack resources by the same list; the runtime pack has no `en`, so all 13 folders go. Measured: 260 files, no subfolders, 134.9 MB (was 143.2 MB), ZIP 58.7 MB (was 62.0 MB). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** On a German Windows, WPF's own texts (a TextBox's Copy/Paste menu, framework exception messages) are English instead of German – consistent with our UI, but not what the player's other apps show. It applies to every project, tests included.
- **Consequence:** Localization (T7.5) has to add its language to the list, or WPF's own texts stay English.

## D51 – Dependabot: one grouped pull request per ecosystem, monthly

- **Context:** T0.2 set up Dependabot weekly and ungrouped. By T6.4, 11 pull requests were open for five updates: one per package and test project, several of them duplicates. A contributor or a maintainer with little time can't tell which of them matter.
- **Decision:** `.github/dependabot.yml` keeps the two ecosystems (`nuget`, `github-actions`, both `directory: /`), each with one group matching `*`, and runs monthly; both use the commit prefix `chore(deps)`. A grouped pull request is merged only if CI is green on both runners and the test counts in the CI log equal main's (a runner that finds no tests stays green). Squash-merged with the task prefix of the current task. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** One broken package holds back the whole group; then `@dependabot ignore <dependency> major` or a second group. Several majors in one PR are harder to bisect. Monthly delays fixes by up to a month; security fixes come through Dependabot alerts and security updates, which are repository settings, not this file. `release.yml` changes are not tested by CI (D49 addendum).
- **Consequence:** At most two dependency pull requests a month. T0.2's "weekly" is superseded. Pushing a change to `dependabot.yml` starts an update run at once.

## D52 – Bow wedge: a nose beyond the frame marks the bow
- **Context:** The ship view draws the racks and a dashed frame around them; the frame is `Ship.Bounds`, the union of the rack boxes, not a hull. Seen as a symmetric box, Rack 1 (port upper) can't be told from Rack 4 (starboard lower) once the player has orbited; only the README says where the bow is. Release blocker, found in the T6.5 planning (T6.4b).
- **Decision:** The frame gets a wedge at the bow end: from the bow face of `Ship.Bounds` a nose of a tenth of the length, at least one cell, narrowing to half the width (centred) at the full height. Drawn like the frame, dashed in `MissionPalette.Frame`, not filled, so it needs no place in the painter (D21), which orders axis-aligned boxes only; hit test and drop target ignore it like the frame. The nose lies beyond the frame because the racks reach the bow edge: a taper within the last tenth would cut through them. `SceneProjector.BowWedge` gives its 8 edges in ship coordinates. No ship format change, no setting, Core unchanged. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** Blunt ships get a nose: it marks the direction, not the hull's shape, and a player may read it as the hull. The extent grows with it, so views other than the default one zoom out a little (in the default view the Hull A nose stays inside the old extent). Dashed at alpha 0x60 it may be too pale over a bright hangar; an alternative was a chevron on the floor (a line seen edge-on at pitch 0) or a "Bow" label (text, behind the ship seen from aft).
- **Consequence:** Every ship file gets the wedge from its rack bounds, without data. A real hull outline stays backlog (D14). The README names the wedge in "Reading the ship view".
- **Addendum (T6.4b, in-game check):** Lines were not enough: in the game Martin could hardly see the dashed nose (2026-10-22), it had the frame's style (dashed, alpha 0x60) and no area. The wedge is now **5 filled faces** (tip, top, bottom, two flanks; the base is the box's bow face), filled faintly in `MissionPalette.FreeEdge` with solid rack edges (`RackPen`), the faces turned towards the camera found by their normals like a box's (D15); the flanks' normals are not along an axis. Painter: every rack ends at or before `Bounds.Max.X`, where the wedge starts, so the x axis always separates them; `ViewDirection.X = −cos pitch · cos yaw` with cos pitch > 0 (±85°), so the yaw alone decides: seen from the bow side (cos yaw > 0) the wedge is drawn after the crates and before the red and cyan marks, otherwise before the rack edges; at yaw 90°/270° they lie side by side on the screen. Convex, so its own faces need no order. Still no place in `DrawOrder`, no hit test. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** A filled face in front of the racks dims the crates behind it when seen from the bow (alpha 0x50 over the stop colour). The free cells' edge colour as a fill ties two meanings to one colour.
- **Addendum 2 (T6.4b, in-game check):** At alpha 0x50 the faces dimmed the crates behind them (Martin): the fill is now `MissionPalette.BowWedgeFill`, alpha 0x18, its own colour instead of the free cells' edge colour. A label says "Bow" (UI text in English), laid flat on the wedge's floor like paint, as Martin sketched it: the line runs across the ship, the letters' tops point aft, so it reads from in front of the bow; it turns and tilts with the ship, reads upside down from aft and mirrored from below. `FloorLabel.Lay` maps the glyphs onto the projected floor with one affine matrix, centres their ink, and flattens the result into screen pixels, so the outline pen strokes in pixels; whether WPF scales a pen with `Geometry.Transform` is not documented (Direct2D's transformed geometry doesn't, a WPF forum says WPF does), flattening makes it moot. It is drawn with the faces, so it takes their place in the draw order. The rack number settings apply to it: colour and outline as they are; `crates.rackNumberFontSize` as a share of the default (27 fills 70 % of the floor's length and of the tip's width, 13.5 half of that), capped at the default so the label never leaves the floor; the settings window says "Rack numbers and bow label". Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** A floor label is less legible than an upright one: foreshortened from a low pitch, upside down from aft. Above 27 the size setting changes the rack numbers but not the label, a setting that acts on only one of the two things it names.
- **Addendum 3 (T6.4b, in-game check):** The label came out larger than the rack numbers (Martin): its size was a share of the floor's room, not of the screen. Now its capitals stand as tall on the screen as a rack number's digits: along the letters' upright axis (the ship's x on the floor) one em is the current rack number size, `RackNumberSize.For` (D32, its shrinking for small racks included), computed once per frame for both. In ship coordinates that is the pixel size divided by the projected length of one cell of x at the current camera and zoom (`FloorLabel.Placement`); the floor's room stays the upper bound. "Same height" means the capital B against the digit 1 at the same em size and typeface, not a capital of the em size in pixels (that would be about 1.4 × the digits). No setting of its own; "Rack numbers and bow label" now holds word for word. Replaces addendum 2's share of the default 27. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** The label's size on the floor changes as the camera turns: seen along the ship (x foreshortened) it grows across the floor until the room stops it, so its width varies more than a rack number's. "As tall" holds for the upright axis only; the floor's skew can make it look wider or narrower than an upright word.
- **Addendum 4 (T7.3):** "The floor's room stays the upper bound" holds only down to the smallest rack number (D32, 12 px): on narrow wedges, as on the Caterpillar, the label then reaches beyond the floor instead of shrinking to a few pixels (D61). Geometry of the wedge unchanged.
- **Addendum 5 (T7.4):** The wedge and its "Bow" label are now the fallback for a ship without an outline (D62); with an outline neither is drawn, and the setting `none` draws neither either. Wedge and label are unchanged where they are drawn.
- **Addendum (T8.1, D83):** the wedge is a mesh in the 3D scene, from the same five faces (`SceneProjector.BowWedge`), in `BowWedgeFill` on both sides; the depth buffer replaces "behind or in front of the racks". The "Bow" label comes back with the 2D overlay of the rack numbers (T8.2); until then the wedge stands without it.
- **Addendum (T8.2, D83):** the label is back in the overlay. `FloorLabel.Lay` is unchanged; it gets the wedge's floor linearised at its middle through `CameraMath` (`SceneProjector.FloorOf`: middle, across and bow-wards as screen vectors per cell), so the label lies on the floor as before and shrinks with distance only as the floor does. Its size is a rack number's (Addendum 3); it hides when a rack covers the floor's middle, like a number.

## D53 – Ships before Nexus

- **Context:** After T6.5 the launch was stopped: a hauler who doesn't find their ship closes the tool. Erec's Cargo Grid Reference Guide (ITG D4.6.0-2) lists 73 ships plus 5 cargo elevators.
- **Decision:** All 73 ships come from the guide as **drafts**, before T6.6 Nexus; the cargo elevators stay out. No `verified` flag, corrections come through bug reports. The README's known issues say once that all data except the Hull A comes from the guide and is not checked in the game. The UI stays English. Multiple languages are backlog and start at the parser (German CT captures as fixtures), not at `.resx`. No ship outline, the bow wedge stays (D52).
- **Counter-argument:** The data is unchecked, the screenshots have to be replaced again, and the launch slips. S7.0 run 4 also shows that the planner rejects solvable loads on grids that are not a multiple of the crate dimensions (D9 holds for the Hull A only). That needs T7.0 before the ships.
- **Consequence:** Phase 7 is reordered (see TASKS.md), the old expansion tasks move to Phase 8. The launch version is `v0.1.0` (so far only `v0.1.0-rc.3` as a pre-release). Task numbers in earlier entries use the old Phase 7 numbering: T7.1 more ships → T7.1 (ship drafts) and T7.2 (ship selection), T7.2 auto-update → T8.1, T7.3 winget → T8.2, T7.4 OCR → T8.3, T7.5 localization → backlog.
- **Addendum (T7.5):** the "no ship outline" line is superseded by D55. Traced silhouettes for every ship exist, so the trigger named here (image field, licence checked) has been met.
- **Addendum (T7.1b):** the grids come from Fleetyards (`/v1/models`, CIG's ship data), not from the guide: 96 of 117 ships with cargo have a cargo grid there, every hold a whole number of 1.25 m cells, capacities equal cells in 288 of 288 holds. `tools/import-fleetyards.ps1` converts them; the guide was meant as a cross-check, which was dropped for now because no machine-readable copy of it exists (only the PDF). Fleetyards' offsets are module-local for the Carrack, Caterpillar and Hull C (racks of identical offset and size, overlapping under a corner or a centre reading), so only 5 ships keep them; the others get a generated row and no `position` (D56 addendum). Racks wider than long are turned so `length` is the longer side, since crates don't turn (D9). The README credit line is T7.4. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** Fleetyards is an aggregator; when it lags behind a patch the drafts are wrong, and with the guide check dropped nothing catches it. The rack arrangement of 90 ships is invented. Fleetyards gives the 32 SCU crate as 8×2×2, `CrateSize` as 4×4×2 (T7.1c): until that's settled, 26 racks in 6 ships take no 32 although their file allows it.
- **Addendum (K5.1): index of 26 Oct 2026, checked complete.** `/v1/models` holds 248 models (pages of 100, 100, 48, then an empty one); 118 have cargo and a top view, 97 of them a cargo grid; none with cargo lacks a top view. Against 25 Oct no ship came or went and every cargo hold is identical; only hull dimensions changed for 18 ships. A ship "missing" from the app (the Javelin) is in the index with no cargo holds, like 20 others (Hull D/E, Kraken, Odin …): Fleetyards has no grid for it. `fetch.ps1` now stops instead of writing a short index (page limit reached, a repeated slug, a model without a slug) and names ships dropped for a missing top view; `ships.json` stays a plain array, its date is the file's timestamp. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** completeness is checked against the API's own paging, not against the website's ship list; a ship Fleetyards shows but the API leaves out would still go unnoticed. The date lives in a timestamp that a careless copy loses.
- **Addendum (D83):** Phase 8 is now the 3D ship view and Phase 9 the release; the expansion tasks named above moved on to Phase 10: T8.1 auto-update → T10.1, T8.2 winget → T10.2, T8.3 OCR → T10.3.

## D54 – Shape-aware fallback when a rack's crates don't fit its grid; a limit on the stacking search

- **Context:** The planner splits the crate line by SCU (D6); `CrateLayout.LayOut` checks the grid afterwards. D9 makes the SCU sum exact for the Hull A only. S7.0 run 4 (two 5×5×3 racks, 2 × 32 + 40 × 1 SCU) was rejected although each rack holds one 32 and the 1s fit the rest; T7.1 brings ships whose grids are no multiple of the crate shapes. The exact search for a layout in route order (D24 addendum, T5.4a) had no limit, and the editor plans on the UI thread.
- **Options:** (a) the SCU split first, a shape-aware fallback only when laying out fails; (b) a shape-aware split always, a grid check per segment of the DP; (c) nothing, only a better message; (d) a full search over layouts in route order (T5.4 spike, aborted on combinatorics).
- **Decision:** (a). The fallback is first-fit decreasing: every crate of the route, largest first, goes into the first rack whose free cells still take it, racks tried as `PlaceInto` tries them (D41: racks with cargo for the same destination, the first empty rack, then rack order). No search: every crate probes every rack once. The probe (`CrateLayout.RackSpace`) is the code that lays out, and `LayOut` lays every rack largest first, so a rack the probe filled always lays out. Placements are built in route order. Every rack with cargo gets the note "Racks not in route order – crates placed where they fit". `PlaceInto` uses the same fallback over the whole mission when stop by stop fails, crates on board fixed; `RetryUnplaced` and `PlaceProblem` follow it. If a crate finds no rack, the plan is refused: "Dest2 doesn't fit: no room for 2 × 32 SCU crates of Titanium – 22 SCU free, but no gap of that shape. Leave a stop out or take a ship with bigger racks." No fallback when the SCU split itself fails: only route neighbours share a rack (D6). The exact search stops after 30,000 steps (`CrateLayout.MaxOrderSearchSteps`): the rack keeps its first layout, crates on an earlier stop stay red, and the plan notes "Rack N: too many crates to rearrange – red crates lie on cargo that unloads earlier. Check the unload order by hand." Measured on one rack of 1,008 cells (12×12×7) with four stops, all seven crate sizes, 958 SCU: without a limit the search had done more than 50 million steps after 15 minutes and was aborted (a lower bound); with 30,000 steps the plan takes 877 ms. Derivation: the Hull A needs at most 2,859 steps (exhaustive check), one step costs about 22 µs on 1,008 cells; 30,000 is about 10 × the Hull A's need and keeps that rack under 1 s. Both notes are carried by `WithHints` through moves, ticks and the plan file; a new Plan starts without them. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** In the fallback the route no longer decides the racks: stop 1 need not be in rack 1, and stops that are no route neighbours share racks (D5/D6 hold for SCU plans only). First-fit decreasing is a heuristic: a load with a layout it misses is still refused. The fallback never runs on the Hull A, so its bugs show only on other ships. (b) would keep route order but miss loads where two stops must share both racks. The notes are the only warnings that can go stale (D10 promised none): a rack dragged back into route order keeps its note until the next Plan. 10 × is a thin margin over the Hull A's need, and on a slow PC the large rack may take 2 s; a time limit would give different plans on different machines.
- **Consequence:** Hull A plans from `Plan` stay byte-identical (golden test, S7.0 runs 1, 2 and 5 bit-identical in the CLI). `PlaceInto` on the Hull A now places missions it left unplaced before, e.g. racks with 12 and 4 SCU free and a mission of stops 4 + 4 and 8: stop by stop fails, first-fit decreasing lays 8 → rack 1, 4 → rack 1, 4 → rack 2. That is intended, not a break of byte-identity: a mission placed before is placed as before, since the fallback runs only after a failure. `LayOut`'s grid message is left for the schema 1 migration. Backlog: a split in route order that avoids stacking findings; a faster exact search, once a real plan shows the note; planning off the UI thread (`Task.Run`), once a freeze is reported.
- **Addendum (T7.1c):** the composition of the 958 SCU load behind "877 ms" was not recorded and the spike ship was deleted, so that number can't be reproduced. T7.1c rebuilt a load with the same key figures (one 12×12×7 rack, four stops, all seven sizes; composition in TASKS.md, T7.1c): 577 ms with the old 32 SCU shape, 636 ms with 8×2×2 (D57), median of three Release CLI runs, both with the note.
- **Addendum (T7.1d):** the fixed budget of 30,000 steps is replaced by one scaled by the rack's cells (D58).
- **Addendum (B2):** the SCU split is gone (D64): the search checks every rack's crates against its grid, so first-fit decreasing remains only for a search out of nodes without any assignment, and to name what doesn't fit. Its note "Racks not in route order – crates placed where they fit" is gone with the route; the search note reads "Rack N: too many crates to rearrange – a layout without crates on other cargo may exist." Notes of older plan files drop on reading.

## D55 – Ship outlines from traced Fleetyards renders

- **Context:** D53 kept the bow wedge and put an outline into the backlog, trigger: a licence-checked image source. Since then a pipeline outside the repo (`sc-ship-outlines`: fetch.ps1 → trace.py → bundle.py) traces the top-view render of every Fleetyards ship with cargo into a polygon in metres (x = bow+, y = port+, origin = centre of the render). 117 ships with cargo > 0 in the export; about 96 of them have a cargo grid and get a ship file (T7.1b), the rest keep an outline without a ship. 200 KB as `ship-outlines.json`, one assumption for all ("bow points left in the render", checked on the Hull A), contact sheets for a visual check. The renders are CIG artwork; `NOTICE.md` records that the silhouettes are not under the repo's MIT licence and are used as non-commercial fan content.
- **Options:** (a) keep the wedge (D53); (b) traced polygon per ship, drawn under the racks, wedge stays as fallback for ships without an outline; (c) bitmap images from the CIG fan kit; (d) the holo glTF models.
- **Decision:** (b), as T7.5 after T7.4 and before the tag. The outline is data, not a format change: `ship-outlines.json` ships next to `ships/` and is matched by ship id; a ship without an entry keeps the wedge. Rack positions are not derived from the outline (the arrangement stays an estimate: drafts, D53; T7.1b). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** Scope grows in a phase that already took T7.0. The licence is a fan-content grey area inside an MIT repo; `NOTICE.md` separates it but does not resolve it. Rendering 100-point polygons in the layered window was the D14 reservation and is unmeasured. The "bow left" assumption was checked on one ship of 117. A traced silhouette that is wrong (Reclaimer, 890 Jump) misleads more than a wedge that is honest. Rack origins are schematic (D14), the outline is to scale in metres (1 cell = 1.25 m): racks may sit off the hull, and how the polygon is aligned with `Ship.Bounds` is open until T7.5.
- **Consequence:** T7.5 starts with a number: orbit with the largest polygon (Apollo, 99 points) while Star Citizen runs, measured like D21. Stutter → T7.5 moves to Phase 8 without further discussion. `ship-outlines.json` and `NOTICE.md` enter the repo; `raw/` and the pipeline do not. README credit: Fleetyards (grids, renders), CIG (artwork), Erec (guide, cross-check). The "Bow" label lies on the wedge's floor (D52 addendum 2); where an outline replaces the wedge, the label needs a new place, decided in T7.5.
- **Addendum:** the outline is T7.4 since 23 Oct 2026, README/guide/screenshots follow as T7.5.
- **Addendum (T7.4):** **Gate.** The largest polygon of the file is the Hull D's (362 points), not the Apollo's (99); the RAFT's (203) is the largest with a ship file; 117 entries, 11,429 points. The gate ran with the Hull D's outline under the Hull A, on the real code instead of a throwaway spike (the spike was paused when the scope changed, D62): without the game the Hull A without an outline takes a median of 0.76 ms and a p95 of 1.24 ms per `OnRender` (Release, 32 renders/s while dragging); in the game Martin accepted the orbit with the outline. The gate script's log of that run holds no orbit, so the in-game milliseconds are not recorded here. **Data.** `outlines/ship-outlines.json` and `outlines/NOTICE.md` sit next to `ships/`, not in it (`ShipLoader` reads every `*.json` there as a ship), a byte-identical copy of the pipeline's output (SHA256 `197538962565…`); Core copies the folder next to every exe like `ships/`, `publish.ps1` checks both files in the ZIP. Matched by `fleetyardsSlug`, not by ship id: all 96 ships match. `ShipOutlineLoader` reads the file once at the start, entry by entry: unknown fields are skipped (a pipeline writes the file, not a person), required fields and types stay strict; a broken entry costs only itself, a missing or broken file costs all outlines, both with one notice line, never the start. **Rules:** scale, alignment, fit and drawing are D62. **Bow direction** checked for the Reclaimer (salvage claw under the bow in the port render) and the 890 Jump (pointed nose) against the pipeline's previews: correct, nothing struck. A wrongly traced entry is struck from the file, no new field; the test's `StruckSlugs` names it. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** The gate's thresholds (median + 1 ms, p95 16 ms) were not measured in the game; the verdict rests on Martin's orbit. The bow check catches an outline turned end for end, not one mirrored port for starboard, which a symmetric hull hides. Striking an entry makes the file differ from the pipeline's output.
- **Addendum (K5.2b): outlines from the index of 26 Oct 2026.** Fleetyards changed the length of 18 ships and added the Salvation; `trace.py` scales each outline to that length, so those 19 were traced again and bundled (118 entries, 11,490 points, SHA256 `C6ADEEC4C184…`, byte-identical copy). `trace.py` now runs with the pipeline's pinned numpy 2.5.3 and opencv 5.0.0.93: the 99 outlines with an unchanged length came out byte-identical, the 18 kept their point count and area / length² within 0.13 % except the C8 and C8X (fewer points: the simplification's 0.08 m floor binds on the shorter hull). The view shows no change for the 18 (the outline is scaled to the racks, D62); the Salvation shows its outline instead of the wedge. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.

## D56 – Ships as a folder next to the exe

- **Context:** D53 brings about 96 ship drafts (T7.1b, converted from Fleetyards). Since T1.x ship files were embedded resources in `CargoPlan.Core.dll`: a hauler can't correct a value, and a bug report can't tell which data the build carried. The loader is strict (`UnmappedMemberHandling.Disallow`), so fields the converter writes must be known before it runs.
- **Options:** (a) embedded (as before); (b) a folder `ships/` next to the exe; (c) hybrid: embedded as the default, a folder overrides.
- **Decision:** (b). The files live in `ships/` in the repo root, visible for "Ship correction" pull requests; Core copies them to `ships/` next to every exe and test assembly (`CopyToOutputDirectory="IfDifferent"`: a file restored by git keeps an older timestamp, `PreserveNewest` would skip it), and `dotnet publish` takes them into the release folder by itself (measured in T7.1a); `publish.ps1` checks that the ZIP holds `SC-CargoPlan/ships/hull_a.json`. `ShipLoader.LoadAll` returns a `ShipCatalog`: the ships that loaded, sorted by id (ordinal), and one line per broken file, which costs only its own ship. A missing folder, or a missing or broken `hull_a.json` (the fallback ship), is `ShipFolderException`: the app shows one message ("… unzip the release again") and exits with code 1, the CLI prints it and exits with 1. Format additions, all optional, schema stays 4: per rack `maxCrateSize` (SCU, the game's limit on top of the grid, Fleetyards `maxContainerSize`; a Carrack rack of 4×4×4 cells takes a 32 by shape, the game only 16), checked by `Rack.CanHold` and with it by every way a crate gets into a rack (layout and probe, moves, merge); per ship `manufacturer`, `fleetyardsSlug`, `source`, `notes`, carried in the `Ship` model without a reader yet (T7.2 groups by manufacturer, T7.5 matches outlines by slug). `hull_a.json` gets `"source": "in game"`, `"manufacturer": "MISC"`, `"fleetyardsSlug": "misc-hull-a"` and no `maxCrateSize` (its grid takes nothing above 16 SCU anyway). Licence: only derived values enter the repo, not Fleetyards' raw export. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Alternatives rejected:** (c) – two sources; in a bug report it is unclear which file was active. (a) – corrections only through a new release.
- **Counter-argument:** An editable folder needs robust validation at every start, and a hauler can break a file by hand with no reset but unzipping again. A deleted folder stops the app. The Hull A becomes a hard dependency of the start. Metadata in the model without a reader is code ahead of its use (A4).
- **Consequence:** `ship-format.md` describes the folder and the optional fields; the Every_shipped_ship_loads theory and `LoadAll_loads_every_shipped_file_without_problems` gate the folder for T7.1b. The catalog's problems are shown in the app with the ship selection (T7.2). `ship-outlines.json` (D55) can sit next to `ships/` the same way. Test ships (`spike_uneven`, broken files) stay in `tests/CargoPlan.Core.Tests/TestShips/`, copied next to the test assembly.
- **Addendum (T7.1b):** a rack's `position` becomes optional too, schema stays 4. Measured before: its only reader is the crate tooltip ("Shubin · Rack 2 · port lower · …"); planner, CLI and drawing don't use it. Ship drafts whose rack arrangement is generated (no usable Fleetyards offsets) would otherwise show a made-up "port lower"; they leave it out, and the tooltip skips the segment. Drafts with Fleetyards offsets keep a position derived from them. `Rack.Position` is `RackPosition?`, `RackDto` takes it as an optional parameter at the end (JSON binds by name). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** a required field caught a forgotten position in a hand-written ship file; now only the PR checklist does. Code that reads the position must handle null (today one place).
- **Addendum (T7.1c):** the Carrack example above (a 4×4×4 rack takes a 32 by shape, the game only 16) came from the wrong crate shape: with the 32 as 8×2×2 (D57) it doesn't fit 4×4×4, and Fleetyards' limit equals the largest crate the grid takes in 284 of 284 racks. `maxCrateSize` limits nothing in the shipped files today. It stays: the code is small and tested, and a rack may yet need a limit the grid doesn't give.
- **Addendum (K3.1, D70):** that rack came: the Clipper's door. `maxCrateSize` is the door limit since ship schema 5, from Fleetyards' `limits.max.capacity` where the door takes less than the grid; `maxContainerSize` is no longer read (283 redundant values dropped from `ships/`).

## D57 – The 32 SCU crate is 8×2×2

- **Context:** D9 set the 32 SCU crate to 4×4×2 cells without a source. The Star Citizen Wiki gives the 32 SCU container as 10 × 2.5 × 2.5 m, Fleetyards' `maxContainerSize.dimensions` (from the game data) as 8×2×2 cells. The series is consistent: 8, 16, 24, 32 SCU are 2, 4, 6, 8 cells long with a 2×2 cross-section. Checked against all 96 ship files of T7.1b: with 8×2×2, Fleetyards' per-rack limit equals the largest crate the grid takes in 284 of 284 racks; with 4×4×2 in 201, and 26 racks in 6 ships allowed a 32 their grid refused.
- **Decision:** `CrateSize.ThirtyTwo` is 8×2×2. No other production code changes: split, merge and the layout work on `Dimensions`. The Hull A golden plans stay byte-identical (no 32 fits a 4×2×2 plate either way). Test ships that relied on the old shape get new ones: `uneven_long` (2 racks 10×3×3, one 32 per rack by shape, two by SCU) replays S7.0 runs 3 and 4, the order search test uses an 8×2×4 rack, the max crate size tests an 8×2×2 rack with a limit of 16, the split tests an 8×3×2 rack. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** Never measured in the game: the Wiki and Fleetyards may both derive from the same game files, so they are not independent. If the in-game container is turned or the 32 has a second shape, all three tables are wrong together. Longer crates change the stacking search: a 10×3×3 rack lays run 4 within the budget, a 9×3×3 rack doesn't (T7.1c findings).
- **Consequence:** Hull B, C1, Freelancer, Shiv and Starlancer MAX/TAC now take 32s (26 racks, 0 left that allow a 32 but can't hold it). `maxCrateSize` limits nothing in the shipped data (D56 addendum). The default split stays 16 + 16 (D27 addendum). `CLAUDE.md`, `ship-format.md` and `plan-format.md` list 8×2×2.
- **Addendum (K3.1, D70):** "limits nothing" ended with the Clipper's door (2 SCU on a grid that takes 8); the redundant values are gone from `ships/`, so every `maxCrateSize` left is a door.
- **Addendum (K3.2, D71):** the shape is the unturned one; a 32 turned is 2×8×2. Racks now lie as Fleetyards gives them, so a 32 fits the Perseus's 6×8×2 rack only turned.

## D58 – Search budget scaled by the rack's cells

- **Context:** D54 limits the exact search for a layout in route order to 30,000 steps. T7.1c found a 9×3×3 rack (81 cells, 32 + 40 × 1) that needs about 1.57 million steps and solves in 509 ms; with 30,000 the planner drops the solution and shows the note. 57 racks in 26 shipped ships have more than 80 cells. A step costs more on a larger rack: about 5.7 µs on 1,008 cells, 0.3 µs on 81 (T7.1d, Release). The rule set before measuring: the largest budget that keeps the stress load (one 12×12×7 rack, 958 SCU, composition in TASKS.md T7.1c) at or under 1.5 s, since the editor plans on the UI thread.
- **Measurements (T7.1d):** Release CLI, three runs each, median; every output byte-identical across runs, exit 0. "note" = "too many crates to rearrange".

  | Budget | Stress load (1,008 cells) | 9×3×3 run 4 (81) | Hull A load case (16) | C2 small hold (216, 1 × 32 + 20 × 8 + 24 × 1) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 30,000 | 635 ms, note | 191 ms, note | 115 ms | 127 ms |
  | 100,000 | 1,028 ms, note | – | – | – |
  | 150,000 | 1,301 ms, note | note | – | – |
  | 200,000 | 1,602 ms, note | – | – | – |
  | 300,000 | 2,300 ms, note | 433 ms, note | 114 ms | 130 ms |
  | 1 million | 6,218 ms, note | 680 ms, note | 118 ms | 126 ms |
  | 2 million | 11,651 ms, note | 844 ms, solved | 114 ms | 125 ms |
  | 4 million | 23,329 ms, note | 840 ms, solved | 114 ms | 127 ms |
  | **scaled** | 150,000: 1,307 ms, note | 1,866,666: 863 ms, solved | 9,450,000: 110 ms | 700,000: 132 ms |

- **Options:** (a) keep 30,000; (b) the rule's fixed budget, 150,000: none of the four cases gains, the stress load takes 0.67 s longer; (c) a budget per rack, scaled by its cells.
- **Decision:** (c). `CrateLayout.MaxOrderSearchSteps(cells) = 150,000 × 1,008 / cells`: steps × cells stay constant, the stress rack keeps the rule's 150,000. Still a count of steps, not a time, so plans are the same on every machine. **Hull A:** 16 cells give 9,450,000 steps; the Hull A needs at most 2,859 (exhaustive check, D54) and its 8-stop load case starts no search at all, so its plans don't change (golden files unchanged since d93b8c6, exhaustive check green). The tests of plans that reach the limit become the category "Slow", out of the default run and in a CI step of their own; the 1 s wall-clock assertion goes (it failed CI at 1,099 and 1,297 ms since T7.0). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** Every plan that reaches the limit now costs about the same, about 1.3 s in Release on any rack, and 2–3 s on a slow PC, on the UI thread. The constant comes from one machine. Limit tests can't be cheap any more: a rack that reaches the limit spends the whole budget, so they leave the default run, and a case that reached the limit with 30,000 may now solve (the 8×2×4 PlaceInto and MoveStop cases did, they moved to an 8×4×4 rack).
- **Consequence:** The 9×3×3 case of T7.1c lays without the note. The backlog line "speed up `InOrder`" stays with its trigger. `dotnet test` stays fast; `Category=Slow` runs separately (CLAUDE.md, CI).
- **Addendum (B2):** the search looks for a layout without foreign stacking now (D64), and the constant is spent once per plan or placement over all racks and choices (`StackingBudget`), not once per rack. `MaxOrderSearchSteps` is `MaxStackingSearchSteps`. With the column bound the T7.1c stress load lays apart in 246 steps instead of running out; no load found so far reaches the limit, so the "Slow" tests take well under a second.

## D59 – A plan on another ship is planned anew

- **Context:** Ship selection (T7.2). A plan lies on one ship's grid: its rack ids and cell positions mean nothing on another. Until T7.2 a plan file for another ship was not loaded, the ContractTracker sync paused, the file stayed (no crash: `PlanStore` caught it). With 96 ships a hauler switches ships with a plan in hand, and a plan.json may come from another ship.
- **Options:** (a) plan the missions anew on the new ship; (b) throw the plan away; (c) refuse the switch while there is a plan; (d) keep one plan per ship.
- **Decision:** (a), in `LoadPlanner.Replan`, the only method that takes a plan of another ship. First `Plan` of every mission in the order the editor shows them (`SavedPlan.Stops`), as the editor's Plan would; on `PlanException` the empty ship and `RetryUnplaced`, which places mission by mission in mission order and leaves the rest unplaced (D41). Every stop ticked as a whole gets its tick back. Missions, ContractTracker ids, watermark and fill order stay; split crates keep their sizes, since split and merge book them into the missions (D27). A switch without missions happens at once; with missions the editor asks "Replan for <ship>? Crate positions change and manual moves are lost; split crates keep their sizes." plus "N stops already ticked – ticks stay." No "keep plan, switch anyway": a plan on the wrong grid is worthless. A schema 2 or 3 plan file for another ship loads without the grid checks and `OverlayViewModel.ShowPlan` replans it, notice "Plan was made for X, replanned for Y."; schema 1 for another ship is still refused (laying it out needs its grid). The ContractTracker sync goes on unchanged: the watermark belongs to the plan, not to the ship. Without `ship` in settings.json the start takes the ship of plan.json if it loads ("Ship set to <name> from your plan", not saved); only a choice in the editor writes `ship` (since T7.2b in the overlay header, see addendum). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** Two ways give two layouts for the same missions (in the fallback the route no longer decides the racks, D54). The dialog comes with every switch that has missions. Manual moves are lost. Ticked stops still need room on the new ship while planning, so a smaller ship falls back sooner; a stop with a delivered visit and new cargo (D41) comes back unticked. Planning runs on the UI thread: the 8-stop load case takes about 4 s in Release on the Freelancer (search limit, D58), the same as the editor's Plan there. (b) loses missions behind the watermark; (c) locks out the hauler who changes ships; (d) keeps two truths and plans nobody flies.
- **Consequence:** `EnsureBelongsTo` stays as a guard; no player action reaches it: every other way into the overlay's plan goes through Core with the overlay's ship. **A ship configuration is a catalog id.** Selection, header, switch and plan work only with `ShipCatalog` entries, never with file names; the catalog's problems carry the id (`ShipProblem`). Cargo modules (the Aurora's rear module, the Retaliator, the Caterpillar's `module_01–04`) will be further catalog entries made from one file, without a change to the plan schema or the selection; a module switch is then a ship switch. No `modules` field, no toggle in T7.2. Fleetyards' export knows no modules (Aurora Mk II: 2 × 1 SCU, the base fit).
- **Addendum (T7.2b): selection moved to the overlay header after hauler feedback: people look where the name is.** The selection in the editor wasn't found; the hauler looked for it on the ship name in the header. The ship name is now the closed box (name and chevron in the header's colour and halo); a click opens the list, grouped as before, on the window colour without its alpha (`WindowOpaqueBackgroundBrush`), since it stands over the game. Locked, the box is hidden and the name shows as text, like the header buttons. The box is not focusable: no keys, the wheel doesn't select in the closed box, so a selection changes only by a click in the open list; committed as in T7.2 (SelectionChanged, dialog after the list closed). The dialog belongs to the overlay; the view model stays WPF-free. The editor only shows "Ship: <name>" and keeps its rows on a ship change from outside: it tells the change by the ship id, since the overlay sets its ship before the replanned plan. One place to change the ship, not two. Spike over Star Citizen (borderless): the list shows above the game, the game keeps the focus after a choice, the wheel scrolls the list, the game taking the mouse closes it, and after the dialog a click into the game gives it the focus back, auto-lock works, nothing hangs. Without the game: the padlock closes the list 5/5, hiding the overlay 5/5, **the lock hotkey 0/5**, so locking closes it explicitly (afterwards 5/5). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument (T7.2b):** The ship name is no longer a handle to move the overlay while unlocked; the header right of it still is. The list is a window of its own above the game, not click-through; it closes on lock, but while unlocked it covers the ship view. The dialog takes the foreground from the game until the next click into it. Planning for another ship from the editor now needs the overlay's header; a choice in both places would have kept the old way for those who found it. An inline confirmation in the overlay (two buttons, no window) would avoid the dialog's foreground change, but needs state in the view model, room in the layout and a rule for locking while it asks; the spike gave no reason for it.
- **Addendum (T7.2c): a group "Recent" on top of the ship list.** With 96 ships a hauler who switches between two or three ships scrolls through the manufacturers each time. The list now starts with "Recent": up to 3 ships chosen last, latest first, with their full names ("MISC Hull A"), since no manufacturer heading stands above them. Without the current ship: it stands in the header, and the list opens on it. Each stays in its manufacturer group too, so the groups never change and Recent is only a short cut. The ship left behind counts as chosen, also when the start set it, so the first switch already fills Recent. Saved in settings.json as `recentShips`, the current ship and 3 more, written together with `ship` (an optional field, no schema bump, D31); a gap in the hand-edited list is dropped on loading, unknown ids stay like `ship` and are skipped in the list. `ShipChoices.Of` builds the list, `ShipChoices.Remember` the order; the view model builds the list anew on every switch. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument (T7.2c):** The list opens scrolled to the current ship, so Recent sits above the visible part until the hauler scrolls up or searches.
