A ship appears twice, under Recent and in its group, also among the search's hits. settings.json now holds history next to settings; a file of its own (`recent.json`) would separate them but needs its own saver and error path. The list is built anew on every switch, so the box loses its selection for a moment and the overlay sets it again.
- **Addendum (B4): `Replan` on the plan's own ship is the player's Re-plan (D66).** `LoadPlanner.Replan` dispatches: another ship goes through `ReplanForShip`, the body of this entry, unchanged (ticked stops still take room while planning, a stop ticked in part still comes back unticked); its own ship through `ReplanInPlace`, where ticked crates stay as they lay and take no room. No API change: the overlay's switch and `ShowPlan` call the same method as before. The ship change's question is no longer a system message box but the centred `ConfirmDialog` of D66, Yes preselected as before.
- **Addendum (K2.2, D75): what is delivered stays delivered, off the ship.** The open line above (ticked stops take room while planning, a stop ticked in part comes back unticked) is closed: every ticked delivery survives a ship change, racks that changed and another cap, and takes no room on the new ship (plan schema 6). See D75.
- **Addendum (K2.4, D74): a ship change empties the undo history.** Its snapshots are the old ship's layouts; going back would turn ship, settings and Recent back too and pass the question before the change. The notice says "Undo history cleared by ship change".
- **Addendum (K5.3): the module form.** A cargo module is a file of its own in `ships/modules/<id>.json` (module schema 1: `id`, `name`, `ships`, `racks`, optional `source`, `notes`; strict like a ship file). `ShipLoader.LoadAll` adds one catalog entry per ship it names: id `<ship>_<module>`, name "<ship name> · <module name>", the ship's racks, then the module's, `totalScu` their sum, the ship's manufacturer and Fleetyards slug (outline and hologram stay the ship's), checked by the same `ShipDto.ToShip` as a ship file. A broken module file, a ship it names that isn't loaded, an id that is a ship file's, or a module rack that doesn't fit the ship (overlap, grid, door) is one problem line and costs only those entries, never the ship or the start; no `modules/` folder, no modules. First module: the Aurora Mk II's TS module, counted in game by Martin on 26 Oct 2026 (a platform of 3 × 2 × 1 cells, crates up to 2 SCU, 8 SCU with the ship's own 2; placed schematically behind and below the ship's generated row). `ShipLoader.Load(id)` reads one file and knows no module ids; the app and the CLI use the catalog. Core copies `ships/modules/` next to every exe, `publish.ps1` checks it in the ZIP. No ship schema change, no plan or selection change. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** a second file format to keep, where a field in the ship file would have been one; D59 chose the catalog entry, so a switch of modules is a ship switch with its question. Fleetyards has no modules, so every module is hand data without a compare. The module rack's place is a guess on a ship whose own racks are a generated row.

## D60 – The overlay takes the keyboard for the ship search

- **Context:** T7.2c, Martin's wish after T7.2b: 96 ships are a long list. Typing a part of the name is the fastest way, but typing needs the keyboard focus, and since D11 (addendum T3.6) the overlay never takes the focus from the game: `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE`, nothing in the header focusable. Typing without the focus would need a global keyboard hook: ruled out (CLAUDE.md: no hooks; to EAC it is a keylogger).
- **Options:** (a) the overlay takes the foreground while the ship list is open; (b) a search field in the editor window, which takes the focus anyway; (c) no search, only the "Recent" group (D59, addendum T7.2c).
- **Decision:** (a). Open, the name line in the header is the search field ("Search ships…", as wide as the list, as high as one entry, outlined in the buttons' border colour, 2 px above the list; the list's border in the same colour). Opening the list calls `Activate()`: the click on the overlay gives this process the right to take the foreground, as the pencil's editor has it (D19). Every key filters by name and manufacturer code, ignoring case (`ShipChoices.Matches`); Esc empties the field, a second Esc closes the list; Enter chooses when exactly one ship is left (`ShipChoices.SingleMatch`, a ship under Recent and in its group counts once), then the dialog of D59 as for a click. Tab, the arrow and page keys stay in the field: they would move the focus out, and the box closes when it loses the focus (app run). Alt+F4 in the field is swallowed: it would close the overlay, which is the app (D34). Closing empties the search. The box and its items stay unfocusable; the field is the one exception. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Spike:** Throwaway build, 5 runs each. Without the game, by UI Automation with real clicks and keys against a stand-in window that logs its keys: (a) the field has the focus after the opening click, typing filters, 5/5; (b) after Esc or a choice one click into the stand-in gives it the foreground and its keys back, 5/5; (c) the lock hotkey with the focus in the field closes the list and locks, unlocking and opening again work, 5/5 (`RegisterHotKey` takes the keys before the field); (d) F and Tab land only in the field, the stand-in gets none, 5/5. Over Star Citizen (borderless): a–d green (Martin).
- **Counter-argument:** D11 held without exception since T3.6; now the overlay takes the foreground from the game while the list is open, and gives it back only with a click into the game: closing the list or locking leaves the overlay in front. Auto-lock can't fire meanwhile, since it needs the game in front (`AutoLockRule`). Handing the foreground back on closing (`SetForegroundWindow` on the window before) would save that click, but Windows may refuse it (foreground lock), so the overlay would behave one way or the other; not built. (b) would keep D11 whole, but haulers look for the ship on its name (D59, addendum T7.2b), not in the editor. (c) alone helps only with ships already chosen.
- **Consequence:** The overlay's focus rule reads: never the focus, except while the ship list is open. A new keyboard use in the overlay needs its own entry. Up/Down don't move through the list; the wheel and the search do.

## D61 – Long ships: side view, fit to the loaded racks, numbers above, screen-size rules for grid and bow label

- **Context:** T7.3, from the T7.2 screenshots: Hull C (16 racks, 144 cells long) and Caterpillar (14 racks, 83 cells) lay as a long diagonal; at 800 × 600 px with the eight-stop fixture a 1 SCU crate was 3.8 and 6.6 px, the free-cell grid covered the crates, the rack numbers sat on them, the bow label was a few pixels. The row is real (modules one behind the other), so the view changes, not `ships/` (generated rows, D53 addendum T7.1b). Acceptance is a visual check.
- **Decision:** A ship is **long** when `Ship.Bounds` is more than twice as long (x) as wide (y): Hull A no; C2, Starfarer, Caterpillar, Hull C yes. A geometric rule, so resizing never switches the mode. A long ship (1) starts at **yaw 70°**, default pitch (`IsoProjection.DefaultFor`); on the first scene and on a ship change, not on a tick or a new plan of the same ship; double-click resets to it. (2) **Fits to its loaded racks and the bow wedge** (`ProjectedScene.FitExtent`; delivered crates count, so a tick never refits; without cargo the whole ship); empty racks are drawn at 40 % opacity. (3) Shows each **rack number above its rack**, on the top middle of its outline, 4 px gap; the fit keeps room on top for it (`ViewTransform.Fit(…, top)`). Screen-size rules after D32, for every ship: (4) free cells are drawn only while a cell is at least `RackNumberSize.MinSize` (12) px, below only those in a stack layer holding a crate (`FreeCellGrid`); (5) the floor's room shrinks the "Bow" label only down to that size, below it the label leaves the floor as rack numbers below it overlap; a setting below 12 is kept. Hull A at the default fit is pixel-identical to before. Yaw 90 (length exactly level) was the plan; at 90 the bow faces are edge-on and crates behind each other can't be counted (Caterpillar module with crates in all four depth rows, checked at 90/70/60); 70 keeps the row flatter than 60. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** Two rules for the rack number by ship shape; a long ship with racks above each other would get the lower rack's number on the upper rack's crates (not in the data today, generated rows lie at z = 0). Fitting to the loaded racks makes the view jump when cargo enters an empty rack: on a crate move (editing, accepted like D20), and on a ContractTracker sync that plans new missions into empty racks – then the view jumps without anything the player did. Hull C can't reach 12 px per crate at 800 px when eight racks are loaded (71 cells in a row, at most 9.7 px even level and without the wedge), so for long rows 12 px is a goal, not a gate: all loaded racks in view with readable numbers, crates countable after at most two wheel notches. The "Bow" label lies flat and reads turned at yaw 70. The bow nose (a tenth of the length) is longer than a single loaded Caterpillar rack; capping it at the ship's width would give Hull C and Caterpillar about 10 % more scale. Seen zoomed out, an empty rack shows no grid at all.
- **Consequence:** Measured (800 × 600 px, 106 dpi, eight-stop fixture, 1 SCU edge x/z): C2 21.5/18.3 px (before 14.9), Starfarer 21.8/18.5 (14.3), Caterpillar 11.8/10.0 (6.6; 17.0/14.4 after two notches), Hull C 8.0/6.8 (3.8; 11.6/9.8). Backlog: cap the nose length, a spindle pattern for Hull B/C in the data (T7.1b follow-up), a tuned grid limit if the C2's full grid at 18 px bothers in the game.
- **Addendum (T7.4c-2):** With a hologram backdrop the fit shows the whole hologram on every ship (Martin, T7.4b; D63); the fit to the loaded racks holds for the outline and `none`.
- **Addendum (T7.4):** With an outline, the fit of a long ship keeps the bow zone (the racks' bow face to the outline's tip, at the racks' full height) where it kept the wedge; with `shipOutline: none` it keeps the loaded racks only (D62).
- **Addendum (T7.4d):** The empty racks' 0.4 also dims every crate outside the selected missions (D68).
- **Addendum (K1.1, D76):** The grid limit is 12 pixels per cell, the zoom's unit, as a value of its own (`FreeCellGrid.MinCellSize`); before it was the rack numbers' minimum font size, the same 12. The value stays and gets no setting (K1 brief); the backlog's "raise it if the C2's full grid bothers in the game" stays open as an observation, not a task.
- **Addendum (K5.2, D78):** the Hull C is no long ship any more. Laid as Fleetyards gives it, two crosses along the spindle, it is 17 cells long and 32 wide; the long rule still serves the C2, the Starfarer and the Caterpillar. The Hull C figures above describe the generated row.
- **Addendum (T8.2, D83):** On a long ship the number stands 4 px above the top middle of its rack's projected outline, and the fit keeps that room on top (`CameraFit.Distance` with `top` = font size + gap + halo); the picture stays centred on the target, so a little more room is left below. The 12 px of the free grid and the empty racks' 0.4 are measured and applied as before, the cell at the target's plane; a rack behind the target shows its grid while its own cells are a little under 12 px, one in front only a little above.

## D62 – The ship outline marks the bow; no "Bow" label with an outline; `shipOutline` setting

- **Context:** T7.4 draws the traced outline (D55). The plan had kept the "Bow" label (D52) at the outline's tip. While the gate was running, Martin set the outline as the standard view without the label ("the one with 'Bow' we don't need any more") and asked for a setting in the settings window at once. He also asked about a 3D outline like the in-game maintenance panel; the data is a top view plus three dimensions, so that is not part of this.
- **Options:** the choice in the settings window (a) Outline / Bow wedge, (b) Outline / Bow wedge / None, (c) Outline / None; for 3D later: a prism from the top view and `heightM`, a rough hull from top and side renders (pipeline work), the holo models (D55 option d).
- **Decision:** (c). settings.json gets `shipOutline`, `"outline"` (default) or `"none"`, top level and optional like `toolWindowTheme` (D36), no schema bump; an unknown name draws the outline with a notice (`AppearanceRules.NormalizeShipOutline`). The settings window has a section **Ship view** with **Ship outline**: Outline / None and a reset; a change applies at once (D30): `OverlayViewModel.OutlineMode` rebuilds the scene, plan and camera stay. **Outline:** the ship's outline, no wedge, no label; a ship without an entry shows the wedge with its label as before (none of the 96 today). **None:** racks and frame only. Every scene the overlay shows passes `OverlayViewModel.Marked`, which sets `ShipScene.Marker` (`BowMarker`: `Wedge`, `Outline` or `None`; scenes built elsewhere keep the wedge). **Scale** (`OutlinePlacement.Place`): a backdrop, not to scale; the outline in cells (m ÷ 1.25) is scaled evenly by k = 1.2 × max(racks' length ÷ outline length, racks' width ÷ outline width), so its box is a fifth larger than the racks' in the binding axis and a tenth of the racks' length stays free in front, as long as the wedge's nose; its origin lies on the middle of `Ship.Bounds`, on their floor (z = `Bounds.Min.Z`); no `outlineOffset` field. At true scale the Hull A's racks (4 × 5 cells) would sit in a hull of 24 × 10 cells. **Fit:** a compact ship fits its racks and the whole outline; a long ship fits its loaded racks and the bow zone (D61 addendum), the rest of the outline may be cut; `none` fits the racks. Martin's floor: the Hull A's crate edge at least 24 px in the default fit, else the long rule for compact ships too; measured 27.7 px, so the compact rule stands. **Drawing:** first, under frame, racks and grid from every side, so it needs no place in `DrawOrder`; filled with `MissionPalette.BowWedgeFill` (alpha 0x18, D52 addendum 2), a 1 px line in the rack numbers' colour at 60 % opacity, under it the crates' halo as a pen of line + 2 × halo size when the halo is on (D28); points go through `ViewTransform`, so the line keeps its screen width (D32); the hit test ignores it, the lock changes nothing. **Measured** (800 × 600 px, 106 dpi, the Hull A's eight-stop plan replanned per ship, 1 SCU edge along x, wedge → outline): Hull A 32.5 → 27.7 px, C2 20.4 → 20.4, Caterpillar 11.8 → 11.7, Reclaimer 14.0 → 14.2, 890 Jump 12.9 → 13.0. The wedge path is pixel-identical for all five (entry missing: 0 px in the window); a missing folder or a broken file shows the notice line and the wedge. Look accepted by Martin. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** Without the label the bow is read from the shape alone, which is weak on hulls that look alike at both ends. `none` takes every mark of the bow away and brings back what D52 solved: after orbiting, Rack 1 can't be told from Rack 4. The Hull A loses 15 % of its crate size for the whole outline. From below the outline is drawn behind the racks although it lies in front of them. A mode change builds a new scene, so the crate selection goes (it is bound to its scene, D25). The outline's line takes the rack numbers' colour: one setting for three things.
- **Consequence:** `ProjectedScene.BowWedge` is null unless the marker is the wedge; `ProjectedScene.Outline` carries the projected points. Backlog: a 3D outline (prism, rough hull from top and side renders, holo models), once the flat one turns out too little in the game. README credit (Fleetyards, CIG) and screenshots come with T7.5.
- **Addendum (K5.2, D78): the Hull A falls under the 24 px floor, accepted.** With Fleetyards' spindle gap the Hull A's racks are 7 cells wide instead of 5; the outline scales with them, and the crate edge in the default fit falls from about 27.7 to 23.4 px (fit scale × 0.844 at 780 × 420 and 780 × 480, × 0.755 in the default overlay's 400 × 302; the overlay's path from the scene with its outline marker to `ViewTransform.Fit`, measured by a throwaway test). Martin accepted it; the compact rule stays, no code changes. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Addendum (K5.2c): the outline is at least true size; with it a ship fits as with the wedge.** The fifth to spare shrank the hull of a ship with a small hold to about the rack's size (Aurora: a 14.8 × 6.6 cell hull drawn 3.6 × 1.6 around a 3 × 1 rack; Redeemer at 6 % of its size); 93 of 97 ships drew their hull smaller than it is. `OutlinePlacement.Place` now scales by max(the fifth to spare, true size): only the Hull A, Caterpillar, Syulen and Railen keep the fifth (their racks fill the hull; of the three generated rows only the Railen's, 53 cells, is longer than its hull of 40). The fit no longer takes the whole outline: with an outline a ship fits as with the wedge, its racks (a long ship: its loaded ones) and the wedge's nose, a tenth of their length; the hull beyond lies outside until one zooms out. **Measured**, crate edge along x in the default overlay's ship view (400 × 302, default camera, empty plan), before → after: Hull A 19.4 → 27.2 px, Cutlass Black 31.9 → 35.4, Freelancer 19.9 → 23.0, C2 12.1 → 13.2, Hull C 4.4 → 5.1, RAFT 20.7 → 22.2; Redeemer 90.5 → 84.7, Aurora MR 106.8 → 91.8, Salvation 80.5 → 76.3. The plan's bow zone up to the outline's tip gave Freelancer 17.4, C2 7.6, Hull C 4.2, RAFT 15.9 and broke Martin's stop rule (no hauler ship worse than today, none newly under the Hull A's value); the short zone passes it, C2 and Hull C stay under 19.4 as before (backlog). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** on opening, the bow tip of most ships lies outside the view; the bow reads from the outline's lines around the racks and the rack numbers until one zooms out. A generated row can stick out of a true-size hull (the Railen's does). The measurement is one machine's DIPs with an empty plan; a loaded long ship fits its loaded racks only.

## D63 – Ship holograms as an optional download (holo backdrop)

- **Context:** D62 draws a flat traced outline; the maintenance panel in the game shows a 3D hologram. Fleetyards serves a holo model (`holo.gltf`, Draco) for all 117 ships with cargo: CIG game models of 0.28–9.35 million triangles with the interior (tanks, pipes, cockpit) and, on the Hull C, D and E, the loaded cargo. T7.4b measured the drawing (a `Viewport3D` behind the ship view, 60 fps with the game at 20 k triangles) and got Fleetyards' agreement on 23 Oct 2026; its open point was the data: meshoptimizer stalled on three of five ships, the clustering after it spoiled their edges, crease angles alone gave no detail levels.
- **Options:** backdrop wedge / outline / holo flat / holo shaded / holo hidden line; source repo / app ZIP / a release asset downloaded on demand / the Fleetyards API; assets on every app release / a data release of their own / a repo of their own.
- **Decision:** Holos are used, as a choice next to the outline (shaded, flat, hidden line), drawn by a `Viewport3D` behind the ship view at true scale; it holds until CIG or Fleetyards says stop. **Data:** one file `<fleetyardsSlug>.holo` per ship in its own format SCH2 ([holo-format.md](holo-format.md): metres in ship axes, welded triangles wound outward, feature edges in three prefix levels), in a GitHub release of its own, tag `holo-v1`, a pre-release that is never "Latest" (the README links `releases/latest`), with `manifest.json` (SHA256 per file) and its own `NOTICE.md`; never from Fleetyards, never in the repo or the app ZIP. A ship missing from the manifest is withdrawn; a manifest that can't be fetched withdraws nothing. **Settings (T7.4c-2):** `shipOutline` gains `"holo-shaded"`, `"holo-flat"`, `"holo-hiddenline"` (default stays `"outline"`, an older app reads them as unknown: outline plus notice), and `downloadShipHolograms`, default `true`. **Pipeline** outside the repo (`sc-ship-outlines`, a private repo; versions in its `TOOLS.md`): Draco decode, transforms into ship axes (the bow axis by the top view's IoU against the D55 outline over both axes and signs), scaled to Fleetyards' length, stray specks out, a weld with a tolerance from the Draco step; then only the triangles seen from outside (100 directions); meshoptimizer 1.3.0 `simplify` with `Prune` and `Permissive`, no `simplifySloppy`; winding made consistent and outward; feature edges (creases ≥ 30°) linked into chains, levels by the chains' share of the score (30 / 60 / 100 %), at most 3,000 / 15,000 edges. The hidden-line contour runs only along edges under 30°, so the levels separate (with every crease as contour, all three levels looked alike). **Budget** a ladder 20 k / 30 k / 40 k triangles: the first step that passes every criterion set before the runs (IoU ≥ 0.95 and Hausdorff ≤ 0.5 % of the length in three views against a tight reference, error ≤ 0.5 %, holes, winding, slivers relative to the Hull A, edge F-score ≥ 0.8). **Shipped** (24 Oct 2026): all 117, 64.7 MB, 37 at 20 k, 21 at 30 k, 59 at 40 k; 69 pass every criterion, 48 don't (44 by the Hausdorff distance), Martin approved them on the contact sheets. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** In Marten's words (Fleetyards): "Grauzone, keine offizielle Freigabe, nicht im Fansite-Kit." Tolerance is an observation, not a right; a stop can come without warning. The overlay's first network access. The launch slips again, after D53 and D55. Dependence on GitHub's release hosting, and `holo-v1` stands at the top of the app repo's release list. `Permissive` is documented for attribute seams only; on these position-only meshes it lowers the triangle count at the same error (Ironclad Assault 47.8 k → 39.9 k), measured, not explained, so a meshoptimizer update may change the result. `Prune` takes thin barrels and antennas off the big ships (missing parts up to about 6 px of 512, Hausdorff up to 3.9 %); the Hull C, D and E show their cargo. 40 k is twice the budget measured with the game, and the Centaurus stays at 50.8 k. The metrics compare with a reference in the same axes, so a holo lying across passes them: five ships wider than long and the Cutter Scout were found only after the full run, by the outline drawn over the top view.
- **Consequence:** A stop means: delete the asset or its manifest entry; the app shows the outline, no new app release, no Nexus withdrawal. One sentence in README, user guide and Nexus description: "Ship holograms are an optional download; they derive from game models and may be withdrawn on request." T7.4c-2 builds reader, download, cache, `Viewport3D` and settings; its Gate 3 measures the largest shipped mesh (Centaurus, 50.8 k) with the game. Ships that share a model (five groups, e.g. the five Aurora Mk I) are byte-identical files; not merged (A4). Backlog Phase 8: meshoptimizer's `remesh` with `Shell` for the ships Prune thins out, cutting the cargo out of the Hull C, D and E.
- **Addendum (T7.4c-2): the app side.** **Settings:** `shipOutline` gains `"holo-shaded"`, `"holo-flat"` and `"holo-hiddenline"`; the settings window's list is now **Backdrop** (Outline, Hologram – shaded / flat / hidden line, None). `downloadShipHolograms` (default `true`) and `holoOpacity` (0.1–1, default 0.4, a slider in 5 % steps, on only for a hologram look; Martin's look gate: 0.5 was too strong) are top level and optional, no schema bump (D31), and so are `holoHullColor` (the hull in shaded and flat, default `#FF9CB0C0`) and `holoEdgeColor` (the edges in hidden line, default `#FFE6E6E6`), two colour fields under the slider without an alpha slider of their own (Martin: some want their hologram in another colour; two colours, his choice of three options); the hidden-line hull stays dark, and the colours are always opaque, since WPF 3D sorts no transparent triangles. **Network:** the overlay's first and only network access is `HoloSource`: plain HTTPS GETs of release assets under `github.com/MRiediger/sc-cargoplan/releases/download/holo-v1/`, not api.github.com (no rate limit, no token), User-Agent `SC-CargoPlan/<version>` without the commit hash, 5 s per request, any 2xx answer, then length and SHA256 against the manifest. The manifest: a cached copy younger than 24 hours is used without a request, otherwise one attempt per run at the first need (Martin). A manifest fetched now or within the 24 hours withdraws a ship it doesn't list (its cached file is deleted); an older one only stands in while the release can't be reached. A new request (ship change, look, switch) cancels the running one. **Switch off** = no access at all, not even the manifest; holograms already downloaded still show (Martin): a withdrawal stops the distribution, it doesn't reach files on a disk that no longer asks. **Kill switch for all:** publish a manifest with an empty ship list; deleting it would read as "can't be reached" (a 404) and keep every cache. **Cache:** `%LocalAppData%\SC-CargoPlan\holo\holo-v1\` (`<slug>.holo`, `manifest.json`). **Drawing:** a `Viewport3D` under the ship view in the same grid cell, collapsed without a hologram (the outline modes render byte-identical to T7.4), never hit; its orthographic camera is derived from `IsoProjection` and `ViewTransform` (tested to the pixel), the ship view publishes it before it redraws. True scale, the middle of the hologram's box on the middle of the racks in all three axes (z: cargo decks sit mid-height in most hulls; accepted in the look gate). The fit shows the whole hologram on every ship (D61 addendum). Shaded splits vertices along the feature edges with angle-weighted normals, flat per triangle, both with `BackMaterial`; hidden line is an opaque dark hull plus the edges as camera-facing strips 1 px wide at every zoom, rebuilt per camera step, level 1 / 2 / 3 from zoom 1 / 1.5 / 3, and the contour per camera along edges under 30°. Colours are constants in `MissionPalette`. **Ladder as shipped:** 59 × 40 k, 21 × 30 k, 37 × 20 k: 20 k is the exception, not the rule. Above 40 k lies only the Centaurus (50,782, 127 %), kept as an override in the pipeline (`overrides.json`; `run-all.ps1` lists every ship above 110 % of its step without one); the Ironclad Assault (40,025) lies within the 110 %. **Gate 3 with the game** (Martin's machine, Centaurus, orbit by timer 12°/s, 2 s warm-up, 30 s per run, UI-thread work per frame): hidden line at zoom 1 (level 1) +0.12 ms median against the outline, p95 3.31 ms; at zoom 3 (level 3) +4.10 ms, p95 11.07 ms, over the 1 ms set before but smooth in the game: green by Martin's judgement, the levels by zoom stay; shaded and flat 1.56 / 1.55 ms, p95 under 1.9 ms; 30–54 fps in every mode, the outline included, with the game on the same GPU; hidden line +41 MB private memory against the outline. The zoomed cost follows the edge count, not the triangles: almost every ship has 15,000 level-3 edges (strip build alone: Hull A 2.09 ms, Centaurus 1.80 ms), so the rule planned for red ("the budget ship gets level 1") would not have hit the cause. Download from the release: manifest and Hull A cold 1.2 s, Centaurus 413 ms, from the cache 6 ms; reading and preparing 5–42 ms off the UI thread. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** Holograms on a disk with downloads off or offline outlive a withdrawal; a running app learns of one at the earliest on the first start after its cached manifest turned 24 hours old. Hidden line zoomed in costs about 4 ms of UI thread per frame while turning, over the threshold: accepted by judgement on one machine, not by the number; the strips are rebuilt on every camera step. Middle on middle can lift the racks above the deck of a tall, flat hull. The whole hologram in the fit makes a long ship's crates smaller than the outline does (Caterpillar), and its generated rack row can reach out of the hull. The gate's ship, the Centaurus, looks ragged (Prune's slivers). One more setting in the Ship view section.
  - **Consequence:** README, user guide (Ship view, where data lives, known limits), settings-format.md and holo-format.md (reading side) describe it; CLAUDE.md names the network access. Any further network access needs an entry of its own. Backlog: strips of world width rebuilt only on a zoom step, if a slower machine stutters zoomed in.
- **Addendum (K5.2b): holo-v1 rebuilt in place for the index of 26 Oct 2026.** The pipeline scales a hologram to Fleetyards' length, read from `ship-outlines.json`; 18 ships changed length since holo-v1 (Hull A 30 → 23.42 m, so its hologram was 28 % too large; Avenger Titan and Renegade 20 → 24.6, too small; C8 and C8X 16 → 12.81; 13 more within 3 %), and the Salvation had none. Those 19 were rebuilt (`run-all.ps1 -Redo flatten`; the Salvation from its Draco file): every one kept its budget step, triangles within 0.2 %, the Salvation passes at 20 k. The release folder was rebuilt with the same tag `holo-v1` (`generatedAt` 2026-10-26, `pipelineCommit` b3da084): 18 files replaced, 1 added, 99 byte-identical, `verify` clean; Martin uploads them with `--clobber`, holograms before the manifest. No app change: the app asks for the manifest at most once a day and downloads every file whose SHA256 differs. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** a tag whose files change is no fixed data version any more; a bug report naming "holo-v1" needs the manifest's `generatedAt` too. A new tag `holo-v2` would have kept holo-v1 fixed but needs an app release to switch to it. A hauler sees the new holograms only after the cached manifest turned a day old.
- **Addendum (K5.3b): `holoOffset`, the hologram's shift from its middle on the racks.** The hologram's middle lies on the racks' middle in all three axes; moving the racks moves it along. On the Aurora Mk II (with and without its TS module) the racks sat a cell too high in the hull (Martin at the holo gate, 26 Oct 2026). An optional ship field `holoOffset: [x, y, z]` in whole cells, read by `ShipDto` (three values) into `Ship.HoloOffset`, kept by a module configuration and written back by the import's `-Force`, moves the hologram from there; the Aurora Mk II has `[0, 0, 1]`, source and date in its `notes`. Racks, plans and the outline don't move; the schema stays 5 (optional, as `position`, D56). After K5.2c the outline is at least true size; should a ship ever need an x/y offset, it holds for outline and hologram alike – then this field is extended to the outline, not a second one created. Today no ship needs that, so there is no code for it. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** hand values per ship where a better rule for all might do; an older app rejects the file (unknown fields are refused), but ship files always come with their own app.
- **Addendum (T7.5a2): the Ship view section's reset leaves `downloadShipHolograms` as it is.** It resets the view (backdrop, opacity, hull and edge color, zoom limit); the switch is consent to network access, and a click on **Reset to defaults** should not renew it unasked. The button's tooltip says so: "Reset the view settings to defaults. Download ship holograms stays as it is." Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** **Reset to defaults** no longer resets everything its section shows; someone who wants the default back ticks the box by hand.
- **Addendum (T8.1, D83): the hologram is part of the scene, no backdrop.** `HoloBackdrop` with its own orthographic camera left the overlay; `PreparedHolo` is the last child of the ship view's `Viewport3D`, under the one perspective camera. `holoOpacity` is on the material (brush opacity), no longer on a whole element; materials, the hidden-line look and the edge strips are as before, the strips' width one pixel in the target's plane (perspective makes nearer edges a little wider, further ones narrower; checked again with the joints in T8.2). The light is no headlight any more but fixed to the ship (D83). Martin's sight check (30 Oct 2026): "perfekt, kein Pumpen".
  - **Counter-argument:** Half-transparent triangles of the hull are not sorted; a concave hull can blend over itself in the order of its triangles. Not seen at the sight check; the fixed order of attempts if it shows: front faces only, an opacity floor, opacity 1 (T8.1, R5).

## D64 – No route order in the planner

- **Context:** Play test at the elevator (Covalex, Hull A): red "… which is unloaded first" findings about an order the player never chose. The planner assumed a route in three places: the crate line cut in stop order (D5/D6: stop 1 in rack 1, only route neighbours share), the stacking rule (D24 addendum, T5.4a: only on own or later stops), `LoadPlan.UnloadOrder` for legend and stacking (D41). ContractTracker contracts say "ANY ORDER"; the player decides the route at the terminal. The eight-stop golden plan held four destinations in rack 4 only because neighbours had to share.
- **Decision:** The planner assumes no order (Martin). Objective, lexicographic: (1) keep a destination together: fewest destinations in more than one rack, then fewest commodity lines in more than one rack; (2) one destination per rack: fewest destinations in the fullest rack, then fewest shared racks, then the sum of squares; (3) no crate on another destination's cargo (its support chain along the stack axis holds no other destination) while a layout avoids it, inside a rack and as the choice between assignments equal in (1) and (2): the first that lays without, else the fewest racks with; (4) fill order as before. `RackAssignment` searches it exactly: destinations by SCU descending, then name (ordinal), into an empty rack before an occupied one, alike racks once, every rack checked against its grid; this search order is the tie-break, so the same missions in any input order give the same racks and positions (test over permutations of every fixture). Budgets are counts: 1,000,000 nodes (a fixture needs at most 1,312), one D58 constant of layout steps per plan. **Column bound:** in a layout without foreign stacking every plate column belongs to one destination, so Σ max(⌈SCU / stack⌉, largest footprint) over the destinations new to a rack > its free columns proves "must stack" without a search (`MustStack`); the same bound prunes inside the search. **Hints, not problems:** `Validate` keeps only what can't lie like this (overlap, outside, nothing below); a crate on other cargo gives the white hint "Rack N: crate for X sits on cargo for Y", one per pair of the support chain, derived like "shared by" and "spans racks", so also after a drag. Legend and colours follow the order of entry (display only). Plan files keep schema 3: hints are derived anew on reading, positions and ticks stay, no re-plan. The capacity message names no stop. Golden files: crate_mix and hull_a_eight_stops regenerated on purpose, the other three byte-identical; in the eight-stop plan Seraphim moves from rack 4 to rack 3 (fullest rack 3 instead of 4 destinations); pairs on other cargo 4 before (all rack 4), 4 after (1 in rack 3, 3 in rack 4). Priority 2 before 3 is a decision: none of the four equal pairings avoids stacking. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** The player loses "you have to go to X first": deliberately, the app doesn't know the route. A "sits on" hint names who is on top, not which to unload first, so with a route in mind the player has to read it against it. Priority 2 before 3 can give more racks with foreign stacking (eight stops: two instead of one). Without "fewest racks" free capacity fragments sooner, which hurts captures placed later (B3's fourth step, B4's re-plan). `PlaceInto` stays history-dependent: captures in another order lay differently; invariance holds for `Plan`, and a re-plan restores it. The Hull A case of 23 Oct takes about 0.1 s longer to plan (weighing ties, layout searches). The exhaustive check of the search runs 8.5 minutes instead of about one. With the column bound no natural load runs out of steps, so `PlaceInto` and `MoveStop` have no test that reaches their note.
- **Consequence:** D5/D6, D24, D41, D54 and D58 get addenda. Measured (Release CLI, median of 3, incl. about 1.2 s `dotnet run` start): Freelancer eight stops 5,277 → 1,401 ms, Hull A Hurston case 1,279 → 1,395 ms. Backlog: an optional player order (legend sorted by drag) with stacking hints relative to it.
- **Addendum (B2, after Martin's acceptance): no hint lines in the overlay.** The lines below the legend ("shared by", "spans racks", "sits on cargo for", the search note) go from the overlay; the colours show which racks hold which destinations and what lies on what (Martin: "es interessiert niemanden was wie geshared wurde, das sieht man an den Farben"). Core keeps deriving them in `LoadPlan.Warnings`: the CLI prints them, plan.json stores them. `ShipScene.Hints`/`ReservedHints` and `LoadPlanner.HintsOnBoard` go (A4); red warnings, notices and the legend's markings stay. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** stacking in a dense rack is now only visible in the colours, where crates may hide each other; the D16 reserve that kept the view from jumping on a tick goes with the lines, as there is nothing left to reserve. Core computes text no hauler sees in the overlay.

## D65 – Moves are accepted whenever the cargo fits

- **Context:** Play test at the elevator (24 Oct 2026, Covalex, Hull A): "Move refused … has nothing below it" for a 4 SCU crate dropped onto a 2 next to a gap, with room in the rack. Since D24/D25 a move was checked at exactly the cells aimed at: `DropTarget` let only the column under the cursor fall, not the crate's footprint; a crate pulled from under another left that one floating; `MoveStop` refused a rack full around its cargo. Martin: if an assignment exists, the move is accepted, not refused; the app may rearrange other crates in the target rack and use other racks; a selection may be split over racks; refused only when the cargo fits nowhere.
- **Decision:** `MoveResolution` (Core) resolves every gesture in stages, each tried only if the one before finds no room: **(1) gravity**, the cells aimed at, fallen along the stack axis until the crate touches cargo or the plate, valid if every bottom cell rests; **(2) free spot**, the nearest free, supported place in the target rack (fewest grid steps, then nearest the destination's own cargo there, then fill rank; without cells aimed at, laid around the cargo there); **(3) relayout**, the target rack laid out anew with the cargo (`CrateLayout.Lay`, D58 budget, delivered cells free); **(3b) displace** (Martin's choice when asked, before going elsewhere), other destinations' crates leave the target rack, the destination with the fewest SCU there first, largest crates first, the shortest list after which the rack lays out and every crate taken out finds a place in another rack around the cargo there (D41 order, the drag's own rack a normal candidate); one level, nothing taken out displaces anything; **(4) elsewhere**, other racks in D41 order, the dragged crate's own rack last; **(5) refused**, "no room for 4 SCU crate" or "no room for 3 crates (10 SCU)", the overlay's "Move refused: " only then. Crates left floating where cargo was lifted settle along the stack axis (else the nearest free place in their rack, else that rack laid out anew). **A selection** goes as a block through 1–3b (its offsets kept in 1–2), then crate by crate (largest first, then fill rank of the cell aimed at, then plan order) through 1–4; it is **atomic**: if one crate finds no room, nothing moves and the text names only those crates. **A legend drag** runs 2, 3 and always 3b, and is **partial**: as many of the destination's crates as fit, largest first, the rest stays where it lies. The asymmetry is deliberate (E1): a selection is a discrete set meant as a whole, a legend drag means "as much of this destination as fits here". A crate is never split (split stays T3.15). A crate the player puts on other cargo is laid; the B2 hint says it. Notices: "Moved 2 crates for X to rack 1 to make room", "No room in rack 4 – placed in rack 1", "No room in rack 3 – 1 crate stays in rack 2". **One function for preview and drop:** `LoadPlanner.ResolveMove`/`ResolveStopMove` return a `MoveOutcome` (plan, stage, refusal, notices); with `upTo` below stage 3 it answers `Pending` instead of laying a rack out. **Preview rule:** stages 1–2 every frame; stages 3–4 every frame only if they stay under a frame (16 ms) in every measured case on Martin's machine, near 16 ms counts as above (E3); otherwise the preview shows `Pending` as the target rack's cyan edge with "Rack N will be rearranged" and the drop resolves fully. Until Martin's native run `CrateDrop.PreviewStage` resolves fully. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Measurements:** Release, median of 3 after a warm-up, `MoveResolutionTimingTests` (category Timing, by hand), dev VM; gestures found per stage by search, "–" where none exists. Preview = up to stage 2.

  | Case | Gravity | Free spot | Relayout | Displace | Elsewhere | Legend drag | Preview max |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Freelancer, eight stops | 0.19 ms | 0.24 ms | 0.62 ms (4 crates: 0.56) | 0.31 ms | 0.29 ms | refused 0.16 ms | 0.22 ms |
  | Hull A, Hurston 24 Oct | 0.03 ms | 0.03 ms | 0.05 ms (4 crates: 0.04) | 0.09 ms (4 crates: 0.10) | 0.23 ms | displace 0.19–0.96 ms | 0.03 ms |
  | Stress load + dock (12×12×7 and 8×2×4) | 2.65 ms | 2.72 ms | 39.6 ms (4 crates: 41.0) | – | 4 crates: 8.17 ms | displace 158 ms | 6.21 ms |

- **Counter-argument:** After a drop more moves than the dragged crate (deliberately, Martin): crates the player loaded jump in the plan, and the plan claims a layout the plate may not have. Dragging back is an undo only for stages 1–2 (backlog: undo for drags). Stages 3–4 rest on `CrateLayout.Lay`, exact for the Hull A (D9) and a heuristic on other ships, so a refusal there may exist where a layout does. Displacement picks the fewest SCU first, not the fewest crates. A crate pulled from under its own cargo can lose its place to the crate settling into it and is refused, although nothing moves then (Hurston case: a Green Glade 4 under its own 1, dragged onto a rack full of Green Glade). A legend drag with displacement on the stress rack costs 158 ms on the UI thread at the drop.
- **Consequence:** Eight Core and three App tests that expected a refusal now expect the resolved move; the play-test case (rebuilt: Green Glade 4 onto Faithful Dream's 2 in Rack 2, half over a gap) and the Hurston plan of 23 Oct (Everus Harbor's 4 pulled from under its 2) are regression tests. D24, D25 and D41 get addenda. Backlog: undo for drags and re-plan.
- **Addendum (B6, D69): the legend drag is atomic and still displaces.** It moves a line's or a mission's crates (no longer a stop's) and goes the selection's way without a cell: as a block through stages 2, 3 and 3b, so other destinations' cargo is still displaced from the target rack (E2), then crate by crate through 1–4, other racks in D41 order. Like a selection it moves as a whole or not at all (`LoadPlanner.ResolveMoveToRack`); the partial legend drag and the asymmetry of E1 go, and with them "No room in rack N – k crates stay in rack M": what the target rack doesn't take goes into another one ("No room in rack N – placed in rack M"). Crates already in the target rack stay where they lie. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** A line larger than the target rack no longer stops at "as much as fits here"; it spreads over other racks, which the player didn't point at. A line that fits nowhere moves nothing, where the partial drag moved what it could.
- **Addendum (K3.1, D70): a door that refuses a crate.** A target rack whose door is smaller than a dragged crate is no target for it: that crate goes to stage 4 at once, the block of a selection crate by crate, and the notice names the door ("Rack 1: door limit 2 SCU – placed in rack 2"); before, stages 1–3b tried and failed in that rack, displacement included. Moves still aren't refused for a door: the crate always fits back into its own rack. Displaced crates never go behind a door that refuses them (tested; `CrateLayout.Lay` checks every rack's door). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Addendum (K3.2, D71):** stages 1 and 2 keep the orientation the player chose (the wheel); from stage 3 on the planner decides orientation like position.
- **Addendum (K2.4, D74): undo.** "Dragging back is an undo only for stages 1–2" is answered: every move, a selection's and a legend drag's included, is a step of the overlay's undo; dragging back stays a move of its own.
- **Addendum (K6.4, D79): loaded crates stay put.** Stage 3 lays the target rack out around its loaded crates, which are fixed (`Board.Loaded`); stage 3b never takes a loaded crate out; so another destination's legend drag goes around them, to another rack where the target has no room left. Crates left floating by a lift settle as before, but a loaded one only drops straight down: where it lands with part of its bottom on cargo it stays, marked, and the settling passes it by. The player may drag loaded crates, alone, in a selection or by their legend line, and turn, split and merge them; they stay loaded.
  - **Counter-argument:** A drop into a rack whose room is held by loaded crates goes elsewhere although rearranging them would have made it fit: the player moves loaded cargo by hand or not at all.
- **Addendum (T8.4, D83): the preview's cost in the 3D view.** The cursor poll runs every frame (D45), Core only when the preview's key changes (`CrateDrop.KeyOf`). Measured with a drag over a half full Hull C (72 crates of 32 SCU, the only size its racks take; a full one has no target on top and measures nothing), 343 of 600 steps asking Core: p95 1.8 ms, max 12.1 ms (median of three runs). The 16 ms rule holds; `PreviewStage` stays `Refused`.

## D66 – Re-plan is an explicit action

- **Context:** Two contracts loaded, a third fits by SCU but not into the layout that grew (B4): with one destination per rack (D64) four destinations of 8 SCU each take half of every Hull A rack, and a 16 SCU crate finds no whole rack although 32 SCU are free. `PlaceInto` leaves the mission waiting (D41), a waiting mission has no crates to drag, and B3's stages move crates on board, not missions. Martin: the player decides to have everything sorted anew and accepts restacking; B3's stages stay conservative, the re-plan is the deliberate, destructive action.
- **Decision:** **Re-plan** lays out the cargo on board as a fresh `Plan` of the same missions would (`LoadPlanner.Replan` on the plan's own ship, `ReplanInPlace`): order-free, so the result doesn't depend on the order things came in (D64); without ticks the crates are exactly the editor's Plan. Missions, crate sizes (split, merge and typed lists, D23, D27), fill order of the plan (not the editor's draft), watermark, legend order and colours stay. **Ticked crates** stay where they lay, ticked, and take no room (D24); a stop ticked in part keeps its tick (D41). **Never loses cargo:** all missions, waiting ones included; else the cargo on board anew and the waiting ones placed after it (`RetryUnplaced`); else the layout stays. **Where:** a button "Re-plan" in the editor next to Import… and Sync from ContractTracker, and (Martin, after the first build) a header button ↻ (E72C Refresh) between merge and the pencil, hidden while locked like the others; both disabled while no unticked crate is on board, the header's tool tip then says "Re-plan: no cargo on board". The command lives in the overlay's view model, so the **hotkey `replan`** (no default, D17; recorded in the settings window like the others, D35) works without the editor; the editor runs it as its own change, so its draft stays a draft and no "changed outside" appears. **Asks first, every time** (Martin, after the first build): the command itself asks through `OverlayViewModel.ConfirmReplan`, so neither button nor hotkey gets past it; its default answer is no, so without a window nothing runs. The question names the cost ("Re-plan? All cargo on board is laid out anew: crates change racks and cells, you restack the ship, and manual moves are lost. Ticks and crate sizes stay."), **No** is preselected. **In the middle of its window** (Martin: the system's message box centres on the monitor, where nobody looks): an own `ConfirmDialog` (Fluent like the tool windows, D36; topmost; `CenterOwner`), owned by the app's active window, else the overlay, which never takes the focus (D11); used for the ship change too (D59, Yes preselected there as before). Measured by UI Automation: header, editor and hotkey 0 px off the owner's centre (the editor pushed against the monitor's edge included). Not chosen: moving the system message box with a thread-local CBT hook (CLAUDE.md: no hooks, and to EAC it looks like one) or with a timer and `SetWindowPos` (flicker). After a yes the notice line says what happened, "Plan rebuilt – 4 crates moved" (crates on board before whose rack or cell changed, `LoadPlanner.MovedCrates`, a multiset since crates have no identity) or "Plan unchanged" (nothing moved and nothing came in; then the plan isn't even set again, the selection stays), plus "– Everus Harbor still doesn't fit" for missions that still wait. **Synchronous on the UI thread**, by the numbers: `ReplanTimingTests` (Release, in process) give at most 3.2 ms median and 7 ms on the first run for every fixture, the Freelancer's eight stops included; only the stress rack (12×12×7, not a ship) takes 80 ms (first run 186 ms). So no task, no lock, no version counter; the backlog line "planning in `Task.Run`" is closed, since the editor's Plan, the sync and the ship switch run the same planner. The hotkey service checks `CanExecute` before `Execute` for every hotkey: the toolkit's commands don't check it themselves, a button's binding does. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Measurements:** before the change, whole processes (median of 5 after a warm-up, dev VM): `dotnet run -c Release` (B2, incl. build check and about 1.2 s start) Freelancer eight stops 1,401 ms, Hurston 1,395 ms; the Release apphost `CargoPlan.Cli.exe` directly 159 and 160 ms, `--version` alone 38 ms. The difference is the start of `dotnet run`, not planning.
- **Counter-argument:** Destructive without undo: moves and the grown layout are gone at one click, and dragging back doesn't bring them (deliberately; undo stays in the backlog with the drags'). The player has to restack the ship physically: that is the point, and the question is the guard against a misclick among the header buttons clicked often at the lift; it costs a click every time and, like the ship change, takes the foreground from the game until a click into it. Whether Windows lets the dialog come to the front when the hotkey fires with Star Citizen in front is untested. The own dialog is one more window to theme and doesn't look like the system's. `Replan` now has two behaviours in one method, own ship and another ship, and ticks survive only on the own ship. Synchronous holds for today's ships; a future ship with large racks and many small crates may freeze the overlay for a moment, and then the task comes back.
- **Consequence:** D24 and D59 get addenda (D59: the ship change asks in the same centred dialog). The fragmentation of D64's counter-argument has a remedy the player controls; `PlaceInto` stays history-dependent. Backlog: undo for drags and re-plan. Not part of this: the capacity display (B5).
- **Addendum (K2.4, D74): Re-plan can be undone.** The counter-argument "destructive without undo" goes: a re-plan is a step, undo brings the layout before back with the ticks set since. It still asks first, every time: the restacking at the lift is the cost, and an undo after the restacking costs it again.

## D67 – Capacity is shown in the header and per rack

- **Context:** Play test at the elevator (24 Oct 2026): neither the ship's nor a rack's maximum and free capacity was visible, only the legend's SCU per destination. Martin: both must show in the overlay, for the ship and per rack.
- **Decision:** **Header**, right of the ship selection (T7.2b): one compact format, `56/64 · 8 free`, and `56/64 · 8 free · 16 waiting` while missions wait for room (D41), the case for re-plan (D66); Martin replaced the brief's `56 / 64 SCU · 8 free` with it, no switch between formats. Max is every rack cell of the ship (`Ship.TotalScu`), loaded the crates on board, ticked ones don't count (D24, `LoadPlan.ScuOnBoard`, which `PlaceProblem` now uses as well), waiting the SCU of the waiting missions, not their number. It **stays visible while locked**: information, not a control, unlike gear and exit (D34); a test over the overlay's XAML checks that nothing on its way to the root carries an `IsLocked` trigger, since the project has no window tests. When room runs short **the ship name trims with an ellipsis, the capacity never**: the capacity is docked right inside a left-aligned panel whose last child is the name. Both name text blocks got `TextWrapping="NoWrap"`: the header's text style wraps and its 30 px hold two lines, so the gate showed "MISC" over "Hull A" instead of "MISC…". **Per rack:** a tool tip `Rack 3 · 12 / 16 SCU` over any part of the rack, free cells and empty racks included, and over a crate as a second line under the crate's own (Martin's choice); `RackLoad` (number, on board, cells) holds the text for scene and projected rack. **The rack number stays the number alone.** Everything is derived from the scene, which every plan change builds anew: move, tick, sync and re-plan need no path of their own. The legend is unchanged: the header is the one place for the ship. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Built and taken out again:** `3 · 12/16` behind the rack number, in the number's fixed size (D32), with a switch `crates.showRackLoad` in the Crates section, default on. Measured in the gate (real overlay offscreen, default look, zoom 1, 420 × 460 px): on the Hull A with the Hurston plan of 24 Oct the label `1 · 16/16` is 111.8 px wide against a rack 141.1 px wide on screen (0.79×), the number alone 15.5 px; on the ships with the smallest rack grid (1 cell, five tie) the Syulen's labels are as wide as their racks (80.8 against 81.3 px), the others 0.25–0.29×; no two labels overlap. Martin after the gate: rack numbers only, the load there is too much. Switch, setting and label went completely, not only to default off: a setting nobody turns on is code and documentation without use (A4), and settings.json keeps no key an older build would reject.
- **Measurements (header):** `55/64 · 9 free` 64 px, with waiting 129 px; the capacity stands in full from 310 px overlay width, with `· 16 waiting` from 370 px; at the minimum width of 240 px only about 10 px are left of the header buttons and the capacity is cut off under them.
- **Counter-argument:** The header gets wider: at the default 420 px with a waiting mission "MISC Hull A" shrinks to "MISC…", and below 310 px (370 px with waiting) the capacity no longer fits next to the six header buttons, at the minimum width of 240 px it is cut off; raising the minimum width or moving the capacity while locked would cost something else. The capacity moves 16 px left when locking, as the name loses its chevron. Without the number behind the rack a rack's load needs a hover, which a locked overlay doesn't answer (D15). "Waiting" counts SCU, so two waiting missions of 8 read like one of 16. Core gets a helper in a UI task.
- **Consequence:** `SceneHitTest.ToolTipAt` answers over a whole rack, not only over cargo. The user guide describes header and rack tool tip; settings.json is unchanged. Open for Martin: the look (taste gate) and what happens below 310 px.
- **Addendum (K6.4, D79):** the header counts loaded SCU, `56/64 · 8 free · 40 loaded`, `· 16 waiting` after it; nothing loaded, nothing shown. The rule stays (name trims first, the capacity never). Measured offscreen: at the default 420 px with ↶ shown and the Hurston plan, `28/64 · 36 free · 10 loaded` no longer fitted next to the header buttons: the ship name was gone and the capacity ran under the scissors. **Martin at the look gate:** the legend's mark instead of the word, `56/64 · 8 free · 40 ▣`, and the capacity's tool tip spells it out ("56 of 64 SCU on board · 8 free · 40 loaded · 16 waiting", `ShipScene.CapacityToolTip`); then it stands in full before the scissors in the same case, the ship name giving way as the rule says.

## D68 – Pickup on the item, crates know their item (plan schema 4)

- **Context:** T7.4d, from the game: crates come up the elevator per mission and per pickup, the overlay colours per destination (D29). Two missions to the same destination, or one mission with three pickups ("7 Stims to Everus Harbor, collect from A, B, C"), couldn't be told apart. Nothing held a pickup: not the editor (its Source column names the ContractTracker capture), not the mission file, not the plan. A crate knew its mission (D42), not its item; crates of one kind took the items of their missions in a queue. ContractTracker keeps one pickup text per contract row (`Contracts.PickupLocation`, several places joined with " / "), and neither it nor the contract text names amounts per pickup: "Collect Waste from …" four times, one "0/9 SCU".
- **Options:** the pickup on the mission or on the item; one place per item (three pickups as three rows with guessed amounts) or a list without amounts; the crate's item as an index or an id; ContractTracker's pickup text read or captures left without one.
- **Decision:** An optional **`pickup` on the item**, a list of places without amounts: `MissionItem.Pickup`, `pickup` in mission file and plan file, the editor's column **Pickup** typed as "A / B" next to the ContractTracker column Source, which stays. One normalization for every way in (`Pickups`: split at "/", trimmed, blanks and repeats in any case left out, first spelling and order kept). **Every crate names its item** (`Crate.Item`, the index in `Mission.Items`, stops and items in list order): `SavedPlan` checks the crates per item, `SavedPlan.WithItems` gives items where nobody did (planner, `PlaceInto`, schema 3, and a re-plan with ticked crates, whose missions were planned without them), `RenameStop` moves the indexes when it merges two stops of a mission. **Plan schema 4** (`crates[].item` required); schema 3 is migrated on reading, each mission's crates of one kind taking its items in order, and the overlay writes 4 at once (D18). Mission files stay schema 2, `pickup` optional (D31). **Split keeps the item, merge needs one item**, also without pickup: before, an item holding all the crates was enough, and the merged crate could take another item's crate. **ContractTracker:** `Contracts.PickupLocation` joins the fingerprint and goes to the items of its row's commodity through the same normalization; the OCR text stays as read and is no part of the duplicate rule (D38: one contract gave three pickup texts). **View**, no planner change: one selected crate shows "pickup · commodity · destination" top left over the ship view (over it, not above it: a line that came and went would refit the ship under the cursor); a selection frames every crate of its missions in white under the magenta selection and dims every other crate to 0.4 like empty racks (D61); the legend gets a header "From A, B" over the lines of each pickup list, a destination once per list, the tick per stop (D16), and "⚠ duplicate?" only on the line of the mission it concerns; a plan without pickups shows no header. Hull A goldens regenerated for the format only: apart from `schemaVersion` and `item` byte-identical. The overlay of the eight-stop plan without selection, rendered offscreen, is byte-identical to the build before. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** An older app refuses a schema 4 plan and a mission file with `pickup` (strict readers, D13). Which of two equal crates is "the one from Lorville" the app decides, not the game: the player has to load as planned. Typed rows of one destination are one mission (D42 addendum), so the frame shows rows from Lorville and Everus Harbor typed in the editor together; the info line still tells them apart, and captures are missions of their own. Pickups from ContractTracker carry OCR noise into info line and legend, every spelling a header of its own, and a ContractTracker update that renames the column stops the whole import (fingerprint). A pickup typed after loading needs Plan and drops the moves (D19). A legend drag still moves the whole destination, not one pickup's crates; a destination under two headers has two lines and one tick. The legend's header wraps for long lists.
- **Consequence:** D19, D27, D33, D38, D42 and D61 get addenda. Backlog: amounts per pickup (trigger: ContractTracker or the game names them), hiding the crates of other missions, editing a pickup without a re-plan.
- **Addendum (T7.4d, after the first look):** The info line reads "from Lorville · Quartz · to Shubin" ("Quartz · to Shubin" without pickup). With more than one mission on board every legend header with a pickup gets a check box in the swatch column, 12 px like a swatch. It is a real selection (D25): ticking adds that pickup's crates still on board, unticking takes them out, the rest of the selection stays, and the box shows ticked while all of them are selected, so a right click on free space or any plan change unticks it with the selection. Its state comes from the selection through a converter, the binding TwoWay with nothing written back, because a click's own value would end a one-way binding. The editor's toolbar wraps in a narrow window. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** A right drag after ticking moves every crate of that pickup. One converter class more. The box has no keyboard access, like everything on the overlay (D20).
- **Addendum (B6, D69):** The legend groups by mission, not by pickup list: one header per mission ("From A, B" from all its items' pickups, "From ?" without), lines per mission and destination with "To"; the header without text for items without pickup and the rule "a destination once per list" go. The check box moves from the pickup header to every line and every mission header, always, in grey. The info line keeps its lower case and reads "from ? · Quartz · to Shubin" without pickup. ContractTracker's pickup text is no longer shown as read: it is cut to the place's name (D38 addendum). Two tick units with two purposes: a click on a crate in the ship view ticks the whole stop, a click on a legend line ticks that mission's crates for that destination. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** see D69.
- **Addendum (K3.2, D71):** plan schema 5 adds `crates[].rotated`; schema 4 stays readable, every crate unturned.

## D69 – Legend groups by mission; check box selects, line click ticks

- **Context:** B6, from Martin's screenshot with his plan of 25 Oct: the legend of T7.4d (D68) grouped by pickup list, so missions read before T7.4d stood under a header without text, ContractTracker's pickup field showed as read ("From Everus Harbor, Everus Harbor. - Freight elevator at Green Glade Station at Hurston's L1 Lagrange point", contract 38: ContractTracker itself stored the next list line), lines had no "To", the check box sat at the header, and a stop was one line under each header with one tick. Crates come up the elevator per mission; the player loads and unloads by mission and checks them off by mission.
- **Decision:** **One header per mission** once any mission on board has a pickup: "From A, B" from every item's pickup, in first order and spelling, or **"From ?"**; never the contract's text; without any pickup no headers, as before. Missions stand in the order their destinations first appear along the route (D64), on a tie in the plan's mission order; a mission's destinations in route order. **A line per mission and destination**, "To Riker · 12 SCU · 4 SCU?", coloured by destination (D29, the same colour under every header), the markings those of that mission (D68); lines stand indented under their header. **Two interactions per line, two looks:** a grey check box in front of it (Martin after the look gate; first proposal the selection's magenta) selects the line's crates on board, the same selection as a right click (D25), so the same drag and the same stages move it (D65); ticked = all selected, a bar = part, empty = none, so a right click in the view shows in the boxes; a click on a box with a bar selects all, on a ticked box takes them out; several boxes add up; the box at a header selects the whole mission. A click on the line itself ticks it, grey and struck through as before; ticked lines have no box. **Two tick units with two purposes:** the ship view's click on a crate ticks the whole stop, every mission (the station is the unit of unloading at the elevator); the legend line ticks one mission's crates for one destination (`LoadPlanner.SetDelivered` with a mission), the fine control. **Legend drag:** a line's right drag moves exactly its crates, a header's the whole mission (`LoadPlanner.ResolveMoveToRack`), atomic like a selection (D65 addendum). **Pickups are names:** ContractTracker's pickup field is cut at a list line's dash and at the DROP OFF/PICK UP LOCATIONS headings, then normalised like a destination (`ContractText.NormalizePickup`, `PickupNames`); plans saved before are cleaned once on loading (ContractTracker missions only, typed pickups are the player's), notice "Pickups cleaned: Everus Harbor". **A pickup can be a destination:** contract 4 of the screenshot, "Everus Harbor · 8 SCU", is Waste, delivered to Everus Harbor where the Stims of contract 37/38 are collected (8 SCU, max crate 1 SCU, size read from the text, the pattern of every Waste fixture); its raw text is not checkable, ContractTracker deleted contracts 1–4. The rule "a pickup never shows as a destination" from the brief would have hidden that cargo and was dropped. The header line is unchanged. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** The legend grows with many missions: a header per mission and a line per mission and destination, two missions to Riker are two Riker lines; position and visibility of D33 stay as the remedy. Without any pickup two ContractTracker missions to one destination stand as two equal-looking lines without a header. Two tick units mean a click in the view and a click on a line do different things to the same crates. The cut of the pickup text is a rule on observed patterns: a place whose name holds " - " or " at " loses its end, and spelling noise in the name stays. Waste contracts' pickups (the stations, "Collect Waste from …") are not read from the text: ContractTracker's field names one, torn. The grey box (#B0B0B0) is close to the grey of a ticked line's text (#8C8C8C); a ticked line has no box, so the two never stand side by side.
- **Consequence:** D16, D25, D38, D65 and D68 get addenda. Backlog: Waste pickups from the "Collect X from Y." lines of the raw text.
- **Addendum (K4.1): the check boxes' colours are settings.** `appearance.legend.checkBoxColor` (the frame) and `appearance.legend.checkMarkColor` (the tick for all selected and the bar for part of them), optional, no schema bump (D31), both default to the grey above (`#FFB0B0B0`), so the overlay looks as before. Two keys, not four (colour and opacity each): transparency is a colour's alpha (D31), and the settings window shows it as the alpha slider in percent next to the picker (T4.2b, T4.2c). They sit in `appearance.legend`, not in the top-level `legend` (D33: layout only), so the Legend section's reset takes them back to grey. Alpha 0 is allowed, unlike a clickable background: the box keeps its own hit-test background (`HitTestBackgroundBrush`) and still takes the click. The template binds `LegendCheckBoxBrush` and `LegendCheckMarkBrush` by DynamicResource; `MissionPalette.CheckBoxBrush` is gone. The brief's "chevron" is the tick inside the box (Martin); the legend has no expand and collapse. A selected box has no colour of its own: ticked and bar are the mark's colour, and the crates' selection magenta (`MissionPalette.Selection`) is another source that stays. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval. **Counter-argument:** a box and mark both at alpha 0 leave an invisible control that still selects on a click, and nothing in the window warns; the per-section reset is the way back. The bar takes the mark's colour, so a player can't tell "part" from "all" by colour, only by shape, as before.
- **Addendum (K6.1): the line names mix and commodity, the header the ContractTracker id.** At the elevator a crate is matched by commodity, size and destination, and the line said only "To Riker · 12 SCU". A line now reads **"To Green Glade Station · 5 SCU (4+1) · Pressurized Ice"**: the mix of the crates the plan holds for the line (after split and merge as they are, delivered crates included), largest first, a repeated size as count×size ("2×4+1", Martin over writing each crate out), a single crate without brackets; several commodities (Martin: a mix per commodity) as "12 SCU (8+2) Titanium, (2) Gold", in item order, each with its brackets. Markings stay at the end. A waiting mission (D41) has no crates on board; its line takes the mix its items would take under the plan's cap ("Hurston · 9 SCU (2×4+1) · Gold · ⚠ …", E1). **The header** reads "From Lorville · #47": `ContractSource.ContractIds`, ContractTracker's `Contracts.Id`, the key the sync already carries; the game shows no id (none in any raw text), so the number is matched against ContractTracker's window, not the elevator (Martin). One capture with two commodities has consecutive ids, "#47–48"; others apart, "#47, 49"; a typed mission has none. A mission from ContractTracker now gets a header without a pickup too ("From ? · #47"), else its id would never show. **Shortening:** the legend stays as wide as its lines (D33); when the overlay is narrower, `LegendRowPanel` (App) gives way by rank: the commodities first (ellipsis, in proportion when there are several), then the destination or pickup; quantity, mix, id and markings never. Rows are DockPanels: a horizontal StackPanel measures its children at infinite width, so nothing could shorten. The tool tip of line and header holds the whole text (unlocked only, D11). The line is a list of `LegendSegment`s (text, rank, reserve), and the D16 reserve sits on the markings segment alone; K6.4's "loaded" mark is one more fixed segment, no change to the panel (E3). Measured offscreen (real overlay, Martin's Hurston plan, default look): at 420 px every line whole; at 270 px only the commodities shorten; at 240 px they are gone and the destinations end in an ellipsis. Hit tests there (E2): a click on the line's text lands on the line button and ticks the line; on the line's box, outside the button, it selects the line's crates and ticks nothing; on the header's box it adds the mission's crates to the selection. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Addendum to it (K6.3):** the id comes from `MissionText.Source`, the formatter the editor and the notice for deleted contracts already used ("ct 37–38" until then); K6.1 had built a second copy (`LegendText.ContractIds`, found against A1 in K6.3 and removed). One spelling everywhere, "#", and runs everywhere: "#36–37, 40" where the editor wrote "ct 36, 37, 40".
  - **Counter-argument:** Lines get longer, so the legend gets wider and covers more of the hangar; at the default 420 px the Hurston plan fits, a longer station name with two commodities doesn't. A commodity shortened to nothing leaves no trace on the line but the tool tip, which a locked overlay doesn't answer. The id is ContractTracker's, so a hauler without it open at the elevator has nothing to match it against; "#1" next to "#37–38" says nothing about the game's contracts. One more panel class in the App (A4: a Grid has no priority, a DockPanel shortens only its last child, two commodities need more).

## D70 – A rack's door limits its crates; derived mixes are capped to what the ship takes

- **Context:** Fleetyards' operator (23 Oct 2026): some holds have a door smaller than their grid; the Clipper's grid (3×2×2) takes an 8, its door only crates up to 2 SCU. The field is `cargoHolds[].limits.max.capacity`, with `limits.min.capacity` beside it (Martin, Fleetyards API, 25 Oct 2026); the local index of 22/23 Oct had neither and gave the Clipper's `maxContainerSize` as 8, which Fleetyards has since set to the door's 2. D57's "284 of 284" was measured on that stale index. `maxCrateSize` existed since D56 as "the game's limit on top of the grid", taken from Fleetyards' `maxContainerSize`, which equals the largest crate the grid takes in 284 of 284 racks (D57), so it limited nothing. A mix the planner derives from a contract (D23) followed the contract's max alone: on a Clipper with its door an 8 SCU contract gave one 8, and the plan was refused. A crate dragged onto a rack that refuses it went through stages 1–3b in vain (D65).
- **Options:** the door as a field of its own next to `maxCrateSize` (two fields, one effect) or `maxCrateSize` itself; a derived mix capped by the door alone or by door and grid (the largest crate any rack takes); a move onto a door that refuses the crate refused, or sent on as in D65; a flag per item for "derived", or the existing `Crates == null`.
- **Decision:** `maxCrateSize` **is** the door limit (Martin). Ship schema **5** marks the meaning: a marker for the change of meaning, not a compatibility boundary. The ships ship with the app, so an older reader never sees a newer file, and D56's "optional fields without a bump" still holds for additions; schema 4 reads the same. The 283 values that only repeated the grid leave `ships/`, and a test checks that every shipped value is below what its grid takes. `import-fleetyards.ps1` reads the door from `limits.max.capacity` (index of 25 Oct 2026) and `maxContainerSize` not at all; the walkway order comes from the effective maximum. Values above 32 are clamped to 32, and only a door below what the grid takes is written (P1 in the import). **A 1 on a grid that takes more stays unverified and is not written** (Martin): it is the one value that can also be a default, and the errors aren't symmetric, a missing limit costs a hauler one crate he sets smaller in the editor, a wrong one locks the rack (Ironclad 20×6×6 "1 SCU only"). The compare names such racks without counting them as a deviation; 32 racks in 16 ships wait for Fleetyards' answer (TASKS.md). `-Create` writes a door, the compare reports one the file lacks or holds otherwise, `-Force` adds a missing one and never overwrites one. From the index 14 ships carry a door: Clipper (2), 300i, 315p, Apollo Medivac and Triage, Cutlass Black, Idris P, Reclaimer, Shiv (2), Nomad (8), 400i, Idris M, Starfarer, Starfarer Gemini (16). **`CrateCap`** (Core) is the largest crate any rack of the ship takes (`Rack.CanHold`) and why: `Door` if some rack's grid would take more, `LargestRack` if the grid is the limit (Martin: door and grid), `None` at 32; the one place for "door limit 2 SCU", "largest rack takes 16 SCU" and the notice. A derived mix (`MissionItem.Crates == null`, so no new flag) is split up to the cap; a set mix stays, and if no rack takes one of its crates, `Plan` refuses with the cap's text and `PlaceProblem` says "doesn't fit (no rack accepts 4 SCU crates, door limit 2 SCU)". The cap follows the ship: a switch derives anew (D59), split and merge book against the capped mix. `SavedPlan.CrateCap` is mandatory (no default) and not stored in the file: the reader takes the ship's, and a file split under another cap gets the largest cap its crates fit (`CapReason.Saved`), which `Replan` and `ShowPlan` treat like another ship's plan ("Plan was made for other racks, replanned for DRAK Clipper."). The overlay's notice "crates capped to 2 SCU (door limit)" is derived from the plan (`SavedPlan.CratesCapped`), so it needs no path of its own; the editor's Crates column shows the capped mix and follows a ship change. A move onto a rack whose door refuses the crate keeps D65 (Martin): stage 4 at once, "Rack 1: door limit 2 SCU – placed in rack 2"; never refused for the door alone, since the crate always fits back into its own rack. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Measurements:** the four fixtures (Hull A eight stops, example missions, crate mix, Hurston 24 Oct) on all 96 ships, every plan file hashed. Data cleanup on the old code: 381 of 384 byte-identical, the 3 others the Clipper, refused before and after, now naming the door. Cap: every case planned before plans byte-identically; 149 refused cases stay refused with another reason (mostly capacity, as the crates are capped first); 2 now plan (600i Executive and Explorer with the crate mix: their one-cell-wide racks take 2s). Doors from the new index: 370 of 384 byte-identical, the 14 others all on ships with a new door, 4 of them now refused (the example missions are a schema 1 file, a set mix of 4s, which a door of 2 refuses). Hull A goldens unchanged, exhaustive check green. Timing in TASKS.md (K3.1).
- **Counter-argument:** A field changes its meaning, and 95 ship files change for it. The grid cap is not checked in the game: if the kiosk offers no smaller crates for a contract with a larger max, the plan shows crates the player can't get (gate below). A player who points at a door rack sees the crate land elsewhere; the notice says why, the drop doesn't stop. The doors are Fleetyards' data, checked in the game for none of the 14 ships, and a wrong one is caught by no test; the rule for a 1 is a guess about Fleetyards' defaults, so a real door of 1 SCU is missed until the answer comes. The cap isn't in the plan file, so a file can't tell under which racks it was made; the reader takes the largest cap that fits.
- **Consequence:** D56, D57 and D65 get addenda. Every way to a mix names a cap (`CrateListUnder`, `ToStops(cap)`, `MissionRows.ToStops(rows, cap)`); tests without a ship pass `CrateCap.None` (`Uncapped`). Gates in the game (Martin): the Clipper with a ContractTracker contract above 2 SCU crates (notice and legend), and at the terminal whether the kiosk offers 2 SCU crates for a contract with max 8; if it doesn't, the cap comes from the door only and the grid part goes.
