- **Addendum (K3.3): crate size limits per rack, min and max.** Fleetyards gives a lower bound too, `limits.min.capacity`; above 1 only on the Hull C (32, all 16 racks: its spindle takes 32s only, as known from the game), the MPUV Tractor (16) and the CSV-SM (4); one hold says 0, read as none. `minCrateSize` is optional per rack (ship schema stays 5), imported above 1, clamped above 32, an error above the hold's maximum; a minimum the rack can't hold doesn't load. `Rack.CanHold` checks `min <= size <= max` and the grid. **`CrateCap` is the set of sizes some rack takes** (Martin's two corrections): with minimums it need not be one range (racks of 32s next to racks of 2s take no 8), so `Accepts` decides; `Min` is the smallest size some rack takes (Martin), `Max` as before, both only for the texts; the set is a bit mask, so record equality covers the minimum and a plan read under another cap is planned anew (K2). **No rounding up:** the derived mix is greedy over accepted sizes up to the contract's max; if a rest is left that no accepted size covers (Hull C: 16 at max 16; 40 at max 32 is 32 + 8), there is no mix, and the mission waits with "doesn't fit (ship takes 32 SCU crates only)", a set mix below the minimum the same (K1). Moves treat a minimum like a door (D65): the rack is skipped, "Rack 1: takes 16 SCU crates only – placed in rack 2"; a split below the minimum is refused. A plan file with a crate on board the ship's racks don't take reads under another cap and is planned anew. Measured: the four fixtures on all 96 ships, 372 of 384 byte-identical, the 12 others on these three ships (the Hull C now refuses all four fixtures, none being 32s only). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** On the Hull C every contract with crates below 32 waits, though in the game a hauler may load smaller crates elsewhere on the ship (its arms are generated rows, D53); the rule follows Fleetyards' data for every rack. Greedy over accepted sizes can miss a mix a non-greedy split would find (accepted 16 and 24 only, 32 SCU: 24 + 8 fails, 16 + 16 fits); no shipped ship has such a set. Whether the kiosk gives smaller crates than the contract's max is still the open gate of D70.
- **Addendum (K5): the gates.** The Clipper's door passed in the game (Martin, 26 Oct 2026). The kiosk gate is dropped: the freight elevator hands out the crates it wants, the player chooses no crate sizes (Martin), so the cap on derived mixes stays as it is. The Hull C's overlay gate stays open: ContractTracker never brought Martin a contract with 16 SCU crates; `MinCrateSizeOverlayTests` covers the case. Torlek's answer on `limits.max = 1` is still open (36 racks in 18 ships after K5).

## D71 – Crates turn 90° about the stack axis; the racks lie as Fleetyards gives them

- **Context:** In the game a crate turns 90° in the grid about the stack axis, it doesn't tip. Only an oblong footprint lies another way turned: 2 (2×1×1), 16, 24, 32 (4/6/8 × 2 × 2); 1, 4 and 8 are square. D9 had one shape per size, "never turned", so T7.1b turned the racks instead: `length` the longer horizontal side, 56 racks in 21 ships (the "turned" note in their files). That only works for a rack with one size of crate along one side: the Freelancer's 9×2×3 rack leaves a 1×2 column per layer that only a turned 2 fills, the Taurus's 3×2×1 rack takes three 2s only with one turned. The brief named the Hull C's outer block as the proof that a 32 fits only turned; in Fleetyards it is x 5 m × y 10 m × z 7.5 m, so length 8 (y) × width 4 × stack 6, the orientation the file already had, and a 32 lies along it unturned. "4×8×6" in TASKS.md was x × y × z read as length × width × stack.
- **Options:** keep turning racks at the import, or let crates turn in Core; in the planner turn always (both ways per crate), unturned first and turned only where that fails, a second pass only after a failed plan, or only 2s; in the overlay the wheel during a drag, a polled key (R: reload, held holster on foot in Star Citizen's defaults), the middle button polled like the right one (D45; in a ship seat Star Citizen's missile operator mode), a context menu; a plan file with a crate outside today's rack (the turned racks went back) refused as broken, or planned anew.
- **Decision:** **Crates turn, racks don't.** `Crate.Rotated`: length and width swap in the grid, the stack stays. The grid's plane across the stack is fixed by `stackAxis` (D26), so "turned" is defined there, not horizontally: on the Hull A's upright plates (±y) a turned 2 stands along z. A square crate that says turned is refused (constructor and `with`). The import writes Fleetyards' own orientation (`length` = y, `width` = x), the 56 racks went back, and a ship with offsets on every rack and no overlap now gets Fleetyards positions with `-Create`. `Rack.FitsGrid`, the minimum check and with them `CanHold` and `CrateCap` take either orientation. **The planner lays a crate unturned where it fits and turned only where it doesn't** (`RackSpace.Add`); the stacking search runs unturned as before and only if it ran out of layouts, not of steps, once more with turned crates, from the same budget (D58 unchanged). The layout of a given rack content that works unturned is byte-identical; the rack assignment can still change where a content fits now that didn't (Taurus crate mix: Riker 6 SCU of 2s alone in its 3×2×1 rack instead of sharing rack 1). Moves carry the orientation (`CrateMove.Turn`, relative, default keep), gravity and free spot use it, a relayout decides orientation like position (D65); turned in place is a move. Split turns the parts where the footprint needs it (a turned 32 gives turned 16s); merge takes the block in either orientation and the new crate lies as the block does, the parts' orientations don't matter. **Plan schema 5:** `crates[].rotated`, written only when true; schema 4 reads unturned, `rotated` before 5 is broken, an app from before D71 refuses 5 (measured: the core of `0892454` says "schemaVersion 5 is not supported"). **A crate outside today's rack** reads as `CapReason.RacksChanged`, whatever the schema (Martin, K1: every ship data update can do this after the release), and the overlay plans it anew through the existing path ("Plan was made for other racks, replanned for MISC Freelancer."); ticks as on another ship (D59). **Gesture: the wheel** while one crate is right-dragged from the view, one notch 90°, finer wheels add up, instead of zooming; the preview resolves at once and shows the turned ghost, release is the preview; a turn counts as moving, so released on its own cell the crate turns there; a square crate swallows the wheel. A selection keeps how its crates lie (B3), a legend drag leaves it to the planner; both zoom as before. **Spike K2** before building it (Martin, Star Citizen in front, the probe not in the foreground): during a right-button capture `WM_MOUSEWHEEL` arrived 38 and 37 times, exactly as often as raw input (`RIDEV_INPUTSINK`) saw the wheel, unlike mouse moves (D45). So no raw input, no new P/Invoke, no `RegisterHotKey`, no settings line. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Measurements:** Release, `ReplanTimingTests`: assignment nodes and final layout steps unchanged for every Hull A fixture and the stress load (Hurston 1,312 nodes, 10 steps; stress 25 / 246); Freelancer eight stops refused after the rollback without turning ("no gap of that shape"), planned again with it (67 nodes, 2.5 ms); Hull A eight stops 3.7 → about 6.7 ms median (a second first fit on every failing fit probe), the stress load 80–120 ms either way (noise). Four fixtures on all 96 ships: 176 of 384 planned before the rollback, 168 after it unturned, 176 now, the same cases; 42 hashes differ from before, all on 16 ships. Hull A goldens: only `schemaVersion`. The exhaustive check is green (8 min 34 s).
- **Counter-argument:** The orientation is the planner's and the player's guess of the game: that a crate turns only about the stack axis is Martin's observation, not checked on the Hull A's upright plates, where turned means upright (gate). Unturned first can miss a stacking-free layout with turned crates where an unturned one with stacking exists, and the second search pass can cost up to the budget again on a failure. A file whose racks changed is planned anew silently but for the notice, its moves are gone, and a really broken position looks the same. Star Citizen sees the wheel too (in a seat: speed limiter; on foot no default in starcitizen.tools' table, not checked in the game), and it depends on Windows' "scroll inactive windows" (on by default). Zooming during a single-crate drag is gone.
- **Consequence:** D9, D15, D25, D57, D65 and D68 get addenda; `docs/plan-format.md` (schema 5), `docs/ship-format.md` (Fleetyards orientation), `docs/user-guide.md` (the wheel). Gates (Martin, publish build): Hull A drag a 2, turn, release, restart (the orientation stays); in the game the wheel turns during the drag; with the tractor beam a 2 upright on a Hull A plate, and if the game refuses that, `CanTurn` allows only racks whose stack axis is upright, notice "can't turn crates on this rack"; Freelancer contract of 2s plans into racks 2/3 turned; Perseus 32s contract three turned 32s; a selection with a turned 2 keeps its orientations; an old Freelancer plan.json reads with "Plan was made for other racks".
- **Addendum (acceptance, 25 Oct 2026):** every gate passed (Martin, publish build and game). The tractor beam sets a turned 2 upright on a Hull A plate, so turning about the stack axis holds on upright plates too, and `CanTurn` stays the same for every rack.

## D72 – Mission files carry the fill order (mission schema 3)

- **Context:** Since D24 the fill order is stored in the plan, chosen in the editor since T4.5. A coordinator who shares a route as a mission file couldn't pass it on: the user guide said "exported mission files don't carry it", and T4.5 left it in the backlog as a Core format change. Mission files were schema 2 (D23, `pickup` optional since D68).
- **Options:** the field optional or required in schema 3; an import that sets the editor's fill order or only offers it; the spelling of the plan file or another; one reader returning stops and fill order, or a second read method beside the old one.
- **Decision:** **Mission schema 3** = schema 2 plus an optional **`fillOrder`** at file level, `"bowFirst"` or `"sternFirst"`, the plan file's words (`FillOrderJson`, moved out of `PlanFileDto`, so both formats share one spelling and one message). Schema 1 and 2 still read; `fillOrder` in schema 2 is refused ("fillOrder is new in schema 3 (the file says 2)"), an unknown value with the plan file's message; schema 3 without the field is valid. **Export always writes it**, the draft's (the editor's Fill order box, since export writes the rows as they are), right after `schemaVersion`. **Import sets it** when the file names one, as an unplanned change like any edit (it takes effect with Plan, T4.5); without the field the editor's fill order stays, which is T4.5's rule. `MissionFileReader.Read` returns a `MissionFile` (stops, fill order or null); `MissionFileWriter.Write` takes the fill order. Mission files embedded in plan schema 1 and 2 stay schema 1 or 2: they were written before schema 3, and a plan has one fill order, its own. The CLI's `plan` uses the file's fill order, else bow first. Plan schema stays 5, ship schema 5; goldens unchanged. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Measurements:** the reader before K2.1 on a schema 3 file: "schemaVersion 3 is not supported (expected 1 or 2)". A schema 3 file read and written again is byte-identical (Core, line endings aside, and the editor's import then export). Tests Core 1,358 → 1,372, App 1,145 → 1,151.
- **Counter-argument:** An older app refuses every exported file now, also one whose rows it could read (strict readers, D13); a hauler on an old build has to update or delete the line. An import that sets the fill order overrides the player's choice silently but for the box and "unplanned changes", while a file shared by a coordinator is exactly the case where it should. Every caller of the reader takes `.Stops` now. The CLI's text output names racks, not cells, so the fill order it now honours doesn't show there.
- **Consequence:** D24 gets an addendum; `docs/mission-format.md` (schema 3, schema 2 still read), `docs/user-guide.md` (import and export carry the fill order).

## D73 – Discard plan empties the plan and the sync's baseline

- **Context:** Only the editor's Plan replaced a plan (D19); after a run the old plan stayed, and with ContractTracker the watermark (D39 addendum) kept every contract below it out. The backlog asked for "Discard plan" for an empty overlay after the route.
- **Options:** (a) empty the plan and set the watermark back to 0, so the next sync takes every contract in again; (b) empty the plan and keep the watermark, so only new captures come; (c) empty it and remember the discarded contracts' ids, so those stay out. For the empty state: `Saved = null` (as on the first start) or a plan without missions.
- **Decision:** **(a)** (Martin): nothing remembers what was discarded; the player deletes finished contracts in ContractTracker, as before the first sync. `LoadPlanner.Discard` gives **a plan without missions, crates and ticks, watermark 0, the old plan's fill order**, for the overlay's ship; not `null`, because without a plan the sync starts with bow first and so does the editor, and the fill order is the player's way of loading (T4.5); `plan.json` is saved empty, so it survives a restart. **`HasPlan` means there are missions**: an empty plan shows the empty state of the first start ("No plan. Open the editor…"), also one emptied by deleting its last mission or a new database's. The command lives in the overlay's view model (`DiscardPlanCommand`, on while there are missions), with the **hotkey `discardPlan`** (no default, D17; optional in settings schema 2) and a **button "Discard plan"** in the editor next to Re-plan, run as the editor's own change (clean rows become one blank row, a draft stays). **No question, a notice** (D66's pattern for the result): "Plan discarded – the next sync brings every ContractTracker contract back." A running auto-sync takes everything in at its next signal; that is meant and in the guide. No ship change. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** One click without a question throws away missions, moves and ticks; until undo (K2.4) there is no way back but planning again. Everything still in ContractTracker comes back, finished contracts included, if the player didn't delete them there; with auto-sync on that happens at once, so a discard while ContractTracker still holds the run looks like nothing happened. `plan.json` stays as an empty file, unlike the first start. `HasPlan` changes its meaning.
- **Consequence:** D19 and D44 get addenda; `docs/user-guide.md` (Discard plan, hotkey, ContractTracker), `docs/settings-format.md` (`hotkeys.discardPlan`). Tests Core 1,388 → 1,391, App 1,155 → 1,165.

## D74 – Undo and redo: snapshots, ticks and syncs carry over

- **Context:** Since D65 a drop moves more than the dragged crate, and a re-plan (D66) or a discard (D73) changes the whole plan; dragging back undid a move only in stages 1–2, nothing undid the rest (backlog of D65 and D66). Martin's brief: a snapshot stack with redo, not saved.
- **Options:** snapshots of the whole plan or diffs; what a step is; for ticks (route state) and syncs (ContractTracker's state), which aren't steps: a sync clears both stacks (the brief's proposal), a sync is a step, or the stacks stay and an undo carries what happened since; for a ship change: clear, or a step that turns the ship back; the resync after an undo reading ContractTracker or the plan's own captures.
- **Decision:** **Snapshots** of the whole `SavedPlan` (a value; the records share what didn't change), `PlanHistory` in the App, `MaxSteps` 100 per stack (measured below). **A step** is every player action on the plan: a move, a selection or a legend drag, a turn, split, merge, Re-plan, the editor's Plan, deleting a mission (editor or a waiting mission's line) and Discard plan; `OverlayViewModel.Step` records the plan before and after, a new step drops the redo. **No step:** ticks and syncs; a cancelled drag never changes the plan. **The stacks stay through a sync** (Martin, against clearing: the sync in the middle of a drag series is the contract the hauler just accepted at the terminal, and then the undo must not vanish): an undo restores the snapshot and then syncs incrementally, `ContractSync.Resync`: the captures the current plan holds that neither the snapshot nor the plan the step left knew came in by a sync since and are placed again by `Apply` (D41), missions gone from ContractTracker leave as in a sync; the database isn't read (the plan knows them, and an undo with auto-sync off never takes in a contract the player hasn't synced). **Ticks carry over** per delivery (K2.2), but only those changed between the plan the step left and now (`LoadPlanner.CarryTicks`), so undoing the editor's Plan, which clears every tick (D19), brings them back; a delivery is known by its contract, as a capture placed again gets another mission id; a tick back without room stays ticked and the notice says why. Redo is the same with the plan before the undo. **A ship change clears both stacks** (Martin), with the notice "Undo history cleared by ship change". **Header** ↶ ↷ left of the gear (E7A7, E7A6), each only while its stack has a step, hidden while locked; **hotkeys `undo` and `redo`**, no default (D17), optional in settings schema 2. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Measurements:** Release, App `UndoTimingTests` (category Timing, by hand). A whole plan (written and read back, nothing shared): Hull A eight stops 19 crates 8.6 KB, Martin's Hurston plan of 25 Oct 23 crates 10.5 KB, C2 eight stops 8.2 KB; a move adds 1.7–2.5 KB to the plan before (once 9.9 KB on Hurston, a move that laid a rack out). So 100 steps are at most about 1 MB per stack, typically some 200 KB. Showing a plan (validation and scene) 0.4–0.7 ms. The first undo after a move, a sync and a tick (it places the contract again and carries the tick): 0.85–1.2 ms, redo 0.4–0.55 ms; far under the 16 ms Martin set, so the fallback (a sync clears the history) isn't needed.
- **Counter-argument:** A contract deleted in ContractTracker after the last read stays until the next real sync, since the resync reads nothing. An undo can place a re-added contract elsewhere than it lay (D41's rule, the snapshot's layout), and a tick back can be refused in a snapshot where the cells are taken. A step whose snapshot dropped a mission the player then ticked in another plan carries nothing to it. Undo after restacking at the lift means restacking again; the history isn't saved, so a crash or restart loses it. The header grows by two buttons while there is history, and the ship name trims sooner (D67). `MaxSteps` drops the oldest step silently.
- **Consequence:** D59, D65 and D66 get addenda; `docs/user-guide.md` (Undo and redo, Re-plan and Discard can be undone, hotkeys), `docs/settings-format.md` (`hotkeys.undo`, `hotkeys.redo`), CLAUDE.md (the App's Timing command). Tests Core 1,391 → 1,401, App 1,165 → 1,182.
- **Addendum (K6.4, D79): a change of state is a step, restore takes the snapshot's state.** Loading, ticking and ticking back, by a legend line or a crate, are steps now: an undo after a misclick at the elevator takes exactly that click back. Restoring takes every delivery's state from the snapshot, ticks included; `LoadPlanner.CarryTicks` and its tests go. Syncs stay no step and carry over (`ContractSync.Resync`); a delivery a sync took away since the snapshot is not brought back by it (today's behaviour). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** A tick set after a move is undone before the move; to undo only the move, the player undoes both and ticks again. The undo stack fills faster at the elevator (100 steps).
- **Addendum (D81):** the resync after an undo marks a contract gone again, with the setting the overlay has. A new ContractTracker database clears both stacks like a ship change ("Undo history cleared by new ContractTracker database"): the snapshots hold the old ids undetached.

## D75 – Delivered stays delivered: ticks per delivery survive a new plan, off the ship (plan schema 6)

Written as a D59 addendum in K2.2 and made an entry of its own at the acceptance (Martin), after D73 and D74, so the numbers stay as they are cited.

- **Context:** D59 left a line open: on another ship only stops ticked as a whole came back ticked, a stop ticked in part (D41) or one mission's line (D69) came back unticked, and ticked cargo took room while planning; since K3.2 the same held for racks that changed (D71) and for a plan read under another cap (D70), which all go through `ReplanForShip`. On the plan's own ship ticks already survived (B4, D66). The tick was always stored per crate (D24), and `SetDelivered` ticks whole deliveries, a stop being all of its deliveries; D41's case (a stop ticked, then a sync brings new cargo for it) was solved on the own ship by that: the new cargo is a new mission.
- **Options:** the unit: the delivery (a mission's crates for one destination, the legend's line); per crate (fails: under the new ship's cap a derived mix is split anew, a ticked 8 becomes four 2s on the Clipper, so crates can't be matched one to one); a stop's tick with the time of the visit (plan schema 6 with a migration for what missions already tell apart). Where delivered cargo lies on the new ship: (A) laid as if loaded alone, dark in free cells, or (B) nowhere, never drawn. B's marker: plan schema 6 with a field, or a position behind the grid. Ticking back such cargo: laid in, or refused.
- **Decision:** **The unit is the delivery.** A delivery whose crates are all ticked leaves its mission before planning, so it takes no room, and only the cargo on board is planned as the editor's Plan would; without ticks the path is the one before. D41's case needs nothing of its own. **Variant B** (Martin): delivered cargo was never on the new ship; its crates come back delivered and **off the ship**, `Crate.OffShip`, in no rack (`Crate.NoRack`, the empty rack id), with the crates its items take under the new cap. Code that works rack by rack (layout, drawing, `Validate`, the text) passes them by on its own; the legend still shows the line struck through, and `Placements` keeps them, so the palette's order stays. **Plan schema 6** (Martin, over a position behind the grid): `crates[].offShip`, written only as `true`, with `rackId` `""` and position `[0, 0, 0]`; refused in schema 5, without `delivered` or with a rack; the reader's rack and grid checks and `Validate` pass such a crate by, so a set crate the new ship can't hold (a 16 on the Clipper) stays ticked instead of reading as racks that changed at every start. **Ticked back** (Martin, over refusing), an off-ship crate is laid into free cells by `PlaceInto`'s rule (D41; `LayStop`, the per-stop part of `StopByStop`, now shared), nothing else moves, each crate keeps its place in the list (legend and colours keep their order), and without room the tick back is refused ("Riker can't go back on board: no room for 16 SCU crate"). The question before a change counts ticked deliveries ("2 deliveries already ticked – ticks stay."), a stop with two ticked lines as two (B6 counted stops). Hull A goldens: `schemaVersion` only. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Measurements:** Release, median of 3, `ReplanTimingTests.Ship_change_with_and_without_ticks` (eight stops): Hull A → Freelancer 1.1 → 1.2 ms without ticks, 1.3 → 1.0 ms with two stops ticked; back 3.3 → 3.1 and 3.4 → 0.9 ms (less cargo to plan). Search counts of every fixture unchanged. Tests Core 1,372 → 1,388, App 1,151 → 1,155.
- **Counter-argument:** An app from before K2.2 refuses every plan file written now, also one without an off-ship crate. A mission that waits on the new ship (its cargo on board doesn't fit) has no crates, so a delivery of it loses its tick: the elevator hands out whole missions, and a waiting mission's tick would need the tick stored on the mission (not done). A delivery ticked in part (only a file edited by hand, or `RenameStop` joining a ticked and an unticked stop of one mission) counts as on board. Delivered cargo on the new ship is visible only in the legend, so the view no longer shows where it lay; a crate ticked back lands where `PlaceInto` puts it, not where it was, and may be refused on a full ship although it fit on the old one. `LoadPlan.Placements` holds placements of rack `""`.
- **Consequence:** D24, D41 and D59 get addenda; `docs/plan-format.md` (schema 6, `crates[].offShip`), `docs/user-guide.md` (ticks stay on a ship change). K2.4's undo (D74) carries ticks per delivery the same way.

## D76 – The view is framed in pixels per cell from a ship-fixed anchor

- **Context:** K1.1: on large ships (C2, Hull C) the wheel didn't get close enough to hit and read single crates. The zoom was a factor of 0.5 to 4 on top of the fit (D15), so a ship whose fit is small was capped small: the Hull C fits to 8.0 px per cell at 800 × 600 (D61), so 32 at most, and with a hologram the fit shows the whole hull, smaller still; the Hull A in the default overlay fits at about 30 (D32), so 120. The pan was in pixels around the screen's corner.
- **Options:** a larger factor (still tied to the fit, the Hull A would get absurdly close); an absolute maximum in pixels per cell; a fixed absolute minimum too (the brief's 4 px) or the old relative one; DIPs or physical pixels; the setting in the Crates or the Ship view section; the zoom kept as a factor and converted only to save it, or the view itself held absolutely; the pan from the fit's centre or from a point fixed to the ship.
- **Decision:** **Pixels per cell** is `ViewTransform.Scale`, in **DIPs** (Martin), the unit the grid limit (D61) and the rack numbers (D32) already used: one cell in the default isometric view, up to √1.5 × that across the view at other angles. **Zooming in stops at `shipView.maxZoomPxPerCell`** (default 160, 40–240, clamped with a notice, no schema bump), whatever the fit, so a Hull C gets as close as a Hull A; **zooming out stops at half the fit as before** (Martin, K1.1). The fit is always in range: above the maximum it is the upper limit. A notch is still a factor of 1.2, so it feels the same at 10 and at 100 px per cell. The view is a camera and an optional **framing** (`Framing`: pixels per cell, and the pan of the view's middle from the **anchor**, in projected units, cells in the default view); no framing is the fit, which follows window, camera and cargo as before. The anchor is the projected centre of `Ship.Bounds` (`ProjectedScene.Anchor`), **never the fit of the cargo** (Martin at the approval): a sync that loads a rack further out moves the fit extent, and a framing hung on it would move the view while the hauler looks. Zoom limits may still depend on the cargo through the fit: a limit, not a stored value. The setting is applied by the transform, so a lower maximum shrinks a framed view around its middle at once (D30), and the next navigation writes the clamped value. The grid limit is 12 px per cell of its own, no longer borrowed from the rack numbers' font size; same value. The slider sits in the settings window's **Ship view** section with its reset, not under Crates: the setting is the view's, not the look's. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** A framed view keeps its size when the window is resized; before, it grew with the window. Two limits in two terms, the lower relative, the upper absolute. At oblique angles a cell edge is up to √1.5 × the number in the setting. DIPs aren't screen pixels on a scaled display: at 150 % 160 are 240 pixels. `shipView` is an object while `shipOutline` and the hologram's keys lie flat.
- **Consequence:** D15 and D61 get addenda; `docs/settings-format.md` (`shipView.maxZoomPxPerCell`), `docs/user-guide.md` (how far the wheel zooms). The hologram's edge levels (D63) still read the zoom on top of the fit.
- **Camera per ship (K1.2):** the view as the player left it is saved in `settings.json`, **`shipView.cameras`, per catalog id** (`ShipCamera`: `yaw`, `pitch`, and the framing as `pxPerCell`, `panX`, `panY`, all three or none), because the Hull A and the Hull C want different angles and a ship change starts that ship's view anyway (D61). Only turned, no framing is written: the next start fits at that camera, whatever size the overlay has then. A framing keeps its pixels per cell and the same point in the middle, so after a restart with another overlay size the view hangs on the same rack and a smaller overlay shows less around it. Kept only for the recent ships and the current one (`Remember` keeps 4 ids, T7.2c), so the file never collects every ship tried. The view writes its camera on every turn, zoom and pan (two-way like the selection, `ShipViewControl.Camera`); the session saves after 500 ms of rest (T3.4), an exit flushes. The reset (double-click, K1.3's button) removes the ship's entry. A camera is no step (K2.4). **Stored camera components are ship-fixed:** yaw and pitch in ship axes, the pan from the projected centre of `Ship.Bounds`. When ship data changes the racks' origins (K5: `offset`/`rotation` from Fleetyards), `Ship.Bounds` moves and saved cameras move with it; that is a data update, not a bug. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** settings.json now changes whenever the player turns the ship (debounced, but more often than before; the backup stays once per run, D47). A camera saved on a ship that later leaves the recent list is gone. The pan is in projected units of the saved camera, so it reads as cells only in the default view.
- **Addendum (K5.2d): zooming out reaches the whole scene.** Since K5.2c the outline is at least true size and the fit shows the racks and a short bow zone; half the fit didn't reach the whole hull (Constellation Mk IV Phoenix), while the Hull C's did. The lower limit is now the smaller of half the fit and the scale at which the whole scene fits (`ProjectedScene.Extent`: racks, whole outline, hologram or wedge; same margin and room on top), carried by `ViewBasis.WholeScale`; `ZoomLimits` takes the basis. Where the scene is hardly larger than the fit nothing changes. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Counter-argument:** zooming out keeps the point under the cursor, so at the limit the whole outline fits the window only once the view is moved to it; no automatic centring (A4). Around a tiny hold (Redeemer) the racks are a few pixels at the limit, which is the point of it.
- **Addendum (K5.5): orbiting holds the scale.** The fit followed the projected extent at every angle, so with a hologram the picture grew and shrank while turning (fit scale over a full turn in the default overlay: Hull A 10 %, C2 25 %, Aurora Mk II 32 %, Hull C 39 %). `ViewNavigation.OrbitBy` now takes the basis and turns the fit into the framing at the first step (`Framing.OfFit`), as `PanBy` already did; a framing already set stays. In every mode. The test `Orbiting_the_fit_keeps_fitting` is replaced by `Orbiting_the_fit_fixes_its_scale`. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval (Martin, 26 Oct 2026).
  - **Counter-argument:** after a turn the view no longer follows the window's size or new cargo until the reset, and the framing is saved per ship (K1.2), so the next start shows it too; tilting can push a tall ship out of the top or bottom of the view.
- **Addendum (holo pump bugfix, 30 Oct 2026): the zoom limits are fixed to the ship.** The pump was back after start and reset, without zooming. No commit since K5.5 refit while turning. The cause was a path K5.5 didn't cover: `ViewNavigation.On` clamps the framing to `ZoomLimits` whenever it draws, and their lower limit was half the fit at the current angle (K1.1, K5.2d). Where that fit is more than twice the framing, the clamp raised the scale. Looking from the bow (yaw 0, pitch 0) in a 420 × 420 view: Hull A +24 %, Hull C +11 %. At the zoom-out limit it pumped at every angle: Hull A 148 %, Hull C 122 %. On a long ship with the wedge the lower limit also followed the loaded racks (D61), so at the limit a plan step that emptied racks changed the scale. The limits now come from `ViewBasis.ReferenceScale` and `WholeScale`, the ship at its default camera (`IsoProjection.DefaultFor(Ship.Bounds)`, what the reset shows). Its extent is `ProjectedScene.ShipExtent`: the fit extent with every rack, loaded or not, so the cargo never moves it. `ShipViewControl` projects it once per ship and marker; a plan step, a tick or a sync keep it, another ship, an arriving hologram or another marker renew it. The fit itself is unchanged (no framing = the fit, D76), and a fit is never clamped. Guards (`OrbitScaleTests`, red before the fix except the second): orbiting from the fit in 15° steps over every yaw and pitch keeps scale and framing; a hologram arriving after the framing keeps the scale; orbiting at the zoom-out limit keeps the scale; a plan step at the limit on a long ship keeps it. K5.5's test turned from the fit only and would have stayed green. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval (Martin: limits fixed to the ship, the third and fourth guard).
  - **Counter-argument:** the limits no longer follow the angle, so at a steep angle zooming out may stop before or past where half that angle's fit would be. A camera saved without a framing (settings from before K5.5) at an angle whose fit lies outside the limits jumps to the limit at its first turn. A long ship opens on its loaded racks (D61), and that fit can lie above `maxZoomPxPerCell` while the reference, all racks, lies below. In the default overlay the largest is the Syulen's with one rack loaded, 147.9 px per cell. In a larger overlay (about 455 px wide and up for the Syulen) the first turn from that fit drops once to the maximum and stays there; before, it pumped there.
- **Addendum (D83):** with the perspective camera the view is a distance, not a framing: `shipView.maxZoomPxPerCell` keeps its key and value but means the size of a cell in the plane through the camera's target, square to the view, and sets the least distance; the framing in pixels per cell and the pan in projected units go (panning moves the target), and saved cameras hold `distance` and `target` instead.

## D77 – Look files and presets: the look is a subset of settings.json

- **Context:** Backlog A4 (theme presets and export): haulers want to swap looks, and the T7.5 screenshots need a defined look Martin can load. Since K4.1 and K4.2 the look also holds the check box colours and the stop palette. Undo (K2.4) is for plan steps; settings apply on change and reset per section (D30).
- **Options:** what a look is (the `appearance` section only, or everything the eye sees); the file's shape (a format of its own, or settings.json's keys); what a missing key means on import (default or unchanged); presets embedded or as files next to the exe like `ships/` (D56); how the window tells a preset from a changed look (a stored name, or comparing values).
- **Decision:** **A look** (`Look`) is `appearance` (all five sections, K4.1 and K4.2 included), `legend` (visible, position), `toolWindowTheme`, `shipOutline`, `holoOpacity`, `holoHullColor`, `holoEdgeColor` and `shipView.maxZoomPxPerCell`: what the overlay and its tool windows look like. **Not:** `hotkeys`, `contractTracker`, `overlay` (bounds), `shipView.cameras`, `ship`, `recentShips` (the machine's or the run's state) and `downloadShipHolograms`, a network switch (D63) a shared look must not turn on. **The file** `*.cargoplan-look.json`, `schemaVersion: 1`, has exactly these keys with settings.json's names and shape, so settings-format.md explains both; `shipView` there holds only `maxZoomPxPerCell`, and `cameras`, `hotkeys` or any other key is unknown and refuses the file (strict like D13). The schema is checked before the keys (`JsonDocument`), with Core's wording ("schemaVersion 2 is not supported (expected 1)."), so a newer file is named by its version. **Missing means unchanged** per look key and per appearance section; inside a section the file has, a missing value takes its default as in settings.json (D31), so a section stays consistent in itself. Values go through the same `AppearanceRules` as loading (`Normalize(Look)`), corrections become notice lines. Export writes every key. The DTOs share `SettingsJsonContext`, so both files keep the same strict options. **Presets** Default, High contrast and Minimal are embedded look files (`Looks/*.cargoplan-look.json`, `LogicalName looks/<file>`), written by the exporter: they are the app's, not the community's, and a player shares a look by Import, so nothing next to the exe and `publish.ps1` unchanged. Default is the defaults (a test holds file, code and export together); High contrast and Minimal ship as drafts until Martin replaces them with exports tuned in the game. **The window** gets a row on top, outside the scroll: "Preset" (the presets, and "Custom" shown only as the selection), **Export…** and **Import…** with the system dialogs (allowed in a tool window, D36). The box follows every change by **value equality** of `Look`: only scalars and records, never a collection (the reason `customStopColors` went to the backlog, K4.2, and the max zoom stands alone instead of `ShipViewSettings` with its cameras). A preset or an import applies at once (D30) as one batch: the appearance once, each setting of its own only if it changed. The notice has a source of its own (`NoticeSource.Look`), because a settings save rewrites the Settings line: "Preset applied: High contrast.", "Look imported: <file>" plus one line per correction, "Look exported: <file>", "Look not imported: <why>"; the next change in the window and closing it clear it. **No undo:** settings are no plan step (K2.4); the reset per section is the way back, and `settings.json.bak` (D47) keeps the file as it was before the run's first save. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** A look with `toolWindowTheme` switches the tool windows of someone who only wanted the overlay's colours, and one with `shipOutline` a hologram look that downloads nothing on a machine with downloads off (it shows the outline, the documented fallback). Missing-is-unchanged and missing-is-default meet in one file: a hand-written file with `"crates": { "fillAlpha": 100 }` keeps the window as it was but resets the rack numbers. Presets inside the DLL can't be swapped without a build; an incremental build may keep a stale copy after a restore with an old time stamp (a test compares the embedded bytes with the repository's). The preset box shows "Custom" after a slider moves one step and back only if the value comes back exactly. A hauler who imports over a tuned look has only the per-section resets and the once-per-run `.bak`.
- **Consequence:** settings-format.md gets a "Look files" section, the user guide presets, export and import. K5's handover names the look keys and the preset names for T7.5.

## D78 – Fleetyards offsets place the racks

- **Context:** Fleetyards gives an `offset` for every hold of 9 ships (C8 Pisces, C8X, Carrack, Caterpillar, Hull A, B and C, Starlancer MAX and TAC) and a `rotation` of 90 or 270 for 19 holds (the Hull C's upright arms, the Carrack, the Caterpillar's nose). Since K3.2 `tools/import-fleetyards.ps1` used the offsets on `-Create` only and ignored the rotation: the Hull C overlapped and became a generated row of 16 racks, 144 cells long. Existing files kept their origins as hand work, nothing compared them, and `-Force` refused a changed rack set (Fortune, Moth). The Hull A's file was schematic (D14), one cell between the plates.
- **Decision:** The mapping, in cells (Fleetyards metres ÷ 1.25), checked against the Hull A and the Starlancer MAX:

  | Quantity | Fleetyards | Ship (D14) | Checked by |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | reference | `offset` = the hold's min corner | `origin` = min corner | Hull A levels z 0 / 3 exact; a centre reading overlaps (K3.2) |
  | across | x+ = starboard | y = −(x + width) | hold names `left` at the smaller x (Hull B, Starlancer); the Pisces' say the opposite; no layout shows it |
  | along | y+ = bow | x = y | the Starlancer MAX over its hologram (Martin, 26 Oct 2026): belly grids forward, bay aft. The Hull B's names (`front` at y 0) say the opposite and are no evidence |
  | up | z+ = up | z = z | Hull A levels |
  | rotation | 90 / 270 about y | x and z extents swap, the offset is the min corner after; the grid stays | only so the 16 Hull C holds make two symmetric crosses without overlap |
  | stack axis | not given | the file's where its frame (`Rack.FrameOf`) lays the grid in the box, else the first that does (+z, +y, +x, then minus; same sign first) | – |

  The Hull A through the table: the file's sides, levels, rack order and grids, but three cells between the plates; the file takes Fleetyards' gap (Martin: no exception), and the compare, which never writes `hull_a`, finds it "same" including the arrangement, the mapping's standing check. The compare checks the boxes of every ship Fleetyards lays out up to a shift of the whole ship (holo and camera hang on the middle of the bounds, D63, D76); a generated row's origins stay unchecked hand work. `-Force` lays such a ship as Fleetyards does: origin, position and a stack axis that fills the box. A changed rack set keeps each rack whose id and grid still match, with all its hand work, adds the new racks behind the last kept one that takes more than 2 SCU (walkways last), drops the vanished, writes the file anew if nothing maps, and says so in `notes`. Any other rotation stops the import; a rack on the ship's middle gets no `position`. The Hull C keeps its grids (8×8×6, 8×4×6; planning byte-identical), so its level arms stack along z and its upright arms along y (Martin). The Carrack and the Caterpillar's nose stay generated: their offsets are module-local and overlap. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** Port and starboard rest on hold names that disagree between ships, and a mirror goes unnoticed on every symmetric layout. Whether the Hull C stacks across its arms or along them (outwards, like the Hull A) is unknown; its stack axes keep the grid, nobody saw them in the game. The Hull A loses its compact look: its crate edge in the default outline fit falls from about 27.7 to 23.4 px, under D62's floor (accepted, D62 addendum). A saved camera of a changed ship sits elsewhere after the update (D76), a data update, not a bug. Fleetyards' grid is the game's logical one; the hologram shows the model with frames and gaps: the Hull C's cargo blocks there are larger than its grid in every direction (an arm 10 m across instead of 7.5, 8.75 → 29 m out from the spindle instead of 5 → 20, blocks about 12.5 m along it instead of 10; the hologram is scaled to Fleetyards' length of 125 m, D63). The racks sit inside the hologram's blocks, not on them; Martin keeps Fleetyards' geometry (26 Oct 2026).
- **Consequence:** K5.2's data: Hull A starboard plates at y −4..−2; the Hull C as two crosses, no longer a long ship (D61 addendum); Fortune 16 SCU and Moth 224 SCU with their new racks; the Salvation new, without an outline yet (bow wedge, D62). The equality run (4 fixtures × every ship) stayed byte-identical wherever only origins moved. Backlog: ship-wide offsets for module holds (Carrack, Caterpillar), the Hull C's stacking direction, one grid or two per Hull C arm, outlines and holograms from the new hull dimensions (K5.1).

## D79 – A delivery is planned, loaded or delivered (plan schema 7)

- **Context:** Martin's final test at the freight elevator (26 Oct 2026): accept mission 1, sync, load its crates, accept mission 2, press **Plan** in the editor: mission 1 is laid out anew although it is in the ship. Right by D19 (Plan replaces the plan), but nothing protected cargo already on board. Martin loads every mission, then hands them over mission by mission: first in, last out, so the first loaded lies at the bottom.
- **Options:** the state per crate or per delivery; where loaded is set (legend line, crate, both); what planning does with loaded cargo (obstacles in the exact assignment of D64, or the rest laid in around it by D41's rule); what happens when support goes (crates float red as before, drop straight down, or are laid anew); a mission loaded in part whose rest finds no room (the delivery waits, or the Plan is refused); undo carrying ticks (D74) or restoring the snapshot's state.
- **Decision:** A delivery (mission × destination, K2.2) goes **planned → loaded → delivered** (Martin). Loaded means on board in the game and fixed; the tick stays "delivered". Stored per crate, `Crate.Loaded`, never with `Delivered` (the constructor refuses it; `AsLoaded`, `AsDelivered`, `AsPlanned` set both); **plan schema 7** writes `crates[].loaded` only where true, schema 6 reads with nothing loaded, `loaded` in schema 6, with `delivered` or off the ship is an error (the `CheckRotated` pattern), an older app refuses 7. **The planner never moves loaded crates:** the editor's Plan and Re-plan keep them (`PlanKeeping`, D19 addendum) and lay the rest in around them by D41's rule, the rest of missions loaded in part first; sync places new missions around them; stage 3 of a move lays a rack out around them and 3b never displaces them, so another destination's legend drag goes around them (D65 addendum). **The player may move them** (drag, turn, split, merge); they stay loaded. **Settling:** whenever cargo leaves (a tick, a contract handed in that ContractTracker no longer lists, a deleted mission), every crate on board that rests on nothing drops straight down its column along the stack axis until it touches cargo or the plate, lowest first so a stack of three settles whole, loaded and planned alike (Martin), never sideways; a crate that touches cargo with part of its bottom stays and is marked red. The notice line says "2 loaded crates settled · 1 planned crate moved down". **The legend line** cycles planned → loaded → delivered → planned; loaded is set there only, per delivery, since loading happens per mission; **a click on a crate** still delivers the whole stop (B6), since unloading happens per station. Back to planned the crates go into their cells while those are free, else they are laid in (K2.2); a delivery without crates on board can't be loaded ("not placed yet", E3). **A sync** removes a mission gone from ContractTracker when nothing of it is planned on board (D41 addendum). **A ship change** or racks that changed clear loaded, with the notice "2 crates were marked loaded – cleared for MISC Freelancer"; delivered stays (K2.2); going back doesn't bring loaded back. **A mission loaded in part** whose rest finds no room refuses the Plan with the reason ("Shubin: 24 SCU not loaded yet find no room around the loaded cargo"), since a mission waits whole and its loaded crates can't wait (E1: the waiting unit per delivery was weighed, about 20 places wired to the mission plus a format field, and left to the backlog). **Re-plan** keeps the plan when laying in around the loaded cargo would leave more SCU waiting ("Re-plan found no better layout", E2). **Undo:** every change of state is a step and restoring takes the snapshot's state (D74 addendum). **Look** (Martin's look gate): loaded crates get a bright white edge (`MissionPalette.LoadedEdge`, 1.5 px), a loaded legend line ends in a fixed segment " ▣" (K6.1, E3), the header counts "· 40 ▣" with "40 loaded" in its tool tip (D67 addendum, Martin at the look gate), delivered stays grey. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Measurements:** Release, before and after on 8d17ed2: the equality run (4 fixtures × every ship) 392 of 392 hashes identical; search counts identical, gave up 0; re-plan medians within noise (Hull A eight stops 6.3 → 7.4 ms, stress load 76.6 → 87.0 ms, single runs on a shared VM); new case "every fixture with its first stop loaded": 0.1–0.8 ms per fixture, 36.8 ms on the stress load, nothing waits, the old layout never kept, gave up 0. Goldens: the `schemaVersion` line only. Slow green. Tests Core 1,433 → 1,460, App 1,384 → 1,396.
- **Counter-argument:** Loaded is set by hand at the elevator, one legend line per delivery; a mark forgotten leaves cargo the planner may move, and the app can't see the hold. Laid in by D41's rule instead of the exact assignment, loaded plans fragment sooner (E2 guard; backlog `RackAssignment` with obstacles). A mission loaded in part without room blocks the whole Plan until room is made (backlog: the delivery as waiting unit, trigger: a refusal at the elevator, likely after K6.5's destinations added by hand). A tick now moves planned crates; a half-supported crate stays red. A ship change loses loaded for good. In the header the mark needs knowing; the word is in the tool tip, which a locked overlay doesn't show.
- **Consequence:** D19, D24, D41, D65, D67 and D74 get addenda; `docs/plan-format.md` (schema 7, `loaded`), `docs/user-guide.md` (Loading at the cargo lift, legend cycle, settling, editor Plan, undo). Backlog: the delivery as waiting unit (E1), `RackAssignment` with obstacles (E2).
- **Addendum (D81):** "A sync removes a mission gone from ContractTracker when nothing of it is planned on board" is superseded: it stays marked gone, and loaded cargo never leaves by a sync, also with remove. Plan schema 8 follows 7.

## D80 – The legend's stop lines scroll within a share of the overlay

- **Context:** Martin's screenshot (26 Oct 2026): two missions from Everus Harbor and a waiting one fill more than the legend area. The legend block is docked in the overlay's DockPanel and gets what is left after the header; what doesn't fit is cut off at the bottom, here the "doesn't fit" line that asks for an action.
- **Options:** the limit as a fixed constant, as a fixed least height of the ship view, or as a setting; what scrolls (the stop lines only, or the whole legend with warnings); scrolling while locked (unlock, or hotkeys that page the legend).
- **Decision:** A setting, `legend.maxHeightShare`: the stop lines take what they need, at most this share of the overlay's height (0.2–0.8, default 0.5), and scroll beyond it (Martin). Top level in `legend` like the position, optional, no schema bump (D31), clamped with a problem line like `holoOpacity`, carried by look files; the settings window shows it in percent under Legend layout. **Only the stop lines scroll** (`Scene.Legend`): missions that don't fit (D41), violations and the palette notice stand below and never scroll out of sight. The ScrollViewer has no horizontal range (the lines keep shortening, K6.1) and takes no focus (a focus would activate the overlay, and a focused ScrollViewer handles the left click, so DragMove would stop on the legend). The ship list's slim bar became `SlimScrollBarStyle`, coloured through Foreground: the header's colour in the list, the legend's in the legend. **Locked, nothing scrolls:** a click-through window gets no wheel; the player unlocks or shows the cursor (Martin). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** One more setting where a constant would have done, with model, check, slider and docs to keep. A share of the whole overlay, not of what is left below header and notices, so a long notice still squeezes the ship view. The lines scroll by pixel, so a line can stand cut at the edge. Locked, a legend longer than its share can't be read to the end without unlocking.
- **Consequence:** `docs/settings-format.md` (Legend layout, corrections, schema row), `docs/user-guide.md` (Legend layout, padlock); the bundled looks list the key.

## D81 – A contract gone from ContractTracker stays in the plan, marked (plan schema 8)

- **Context:** Since D41 and D79 a sync took a mission out when ContractTracker listed none of its ids and nothing of it was planned on board: all loaded (its crates gone, what lay on them settled), all delivered, or waiting, which was lost for good since the watermark had passed it. ContractTracker loses contracts for reasons that aren't the game's (an OCR miss, a restart, a row deleted by hand, a contract abandoned with cargo still aboard): another tool caused data loss in the plan. Planned cargo on board kept its mission, with a notice derived on every change. No guard for all contracts going at once existed (D39 dropped D38's reset detection), and a new database made every capture gone. Reviewer's brief, a blocker for v0.1.0.
- **Options:** keep by default or remove as before; the mark on the mission or on its capture; what remove takes (as before; the brief's planned and waiting; Martin's planned, waiting and delivered, never loaded); at every sync or only when the contract goes; back by the same id or by the duplicate rule (a content key, ruled out in D38 and D42); the old ids after a new database (a flag or a generation counter).
- **Decision:** **Keep by default** (`contractTracker.onContractGone`, `"keep"` or `"remove"`, settings schema 2, an unknown name keeps with a problem line): crates, positions, states and ticks stay, no planner move. The mark is the capture's, **`ContractSource.Gone`** (`missions[].contract.gone`, written only as `true`, **plan schema 8**), so the editor's Plan, renames and ship changes carry it without a field of their own; a typed mission is never gone. **Remove in two stages** (correction K1 at the approval): when the contract goes, its planned and delivered deliveries leave and its loaded ones stay, marked; a waiting mission leaves whole. Later syncs take a gone mission only whole and only once nothing of it is planned or loaded, so planned cargo never leaves after the moment it went: the guard below and a switch from keep to remove keep it. **All at once:** every listed contract gone in one read (at least two) or a new database marks and removes nothing, whatever the setting ("All contracts gone from ContractTracker at once – kept in plan"). **Back** by one of its ids in a read: the mark goes ("Contract #47 back in ContractTracker"); what remove took stays out; a recapture (F4) stays a mission of its own with "⚠ duplicate?" (D44 addendum). **Detached** (correction K2): a new database marks every gone capture detached (`contract.detached`, only with `gone`), its ids shown and never compared again: not present, not back, not a known id of the read, a delivery key of its own in `PlanKeeping`, so a new contract #1 is never the old #1. A waiting mission that is gone isn't placed (`RetryUnplaced`); the editor's Plan places it like any. **Seen:** the sync's notice says once what it did (`SyncResult`, `GoneReport`): "Contract #47 gone from ContractTracker – kept in plan", "… – removed 2 planned, 1 delivered, kept 1 loaded", "… – removed, it was waiting", rows added by hand named as in K6.5; the notice "Deleted in ContractTracker, crates still on board" goes. The legend header ends in a fixed segment " · gone" after the id, a waiting line says "gone", the editor's Mission column "#47 · gone" (sorted by id as before, K6.3). No colour, no grey. **The settings window** picks it in the ContractTracker section ("Keep in plan", "Remove from plan"), applied on change (D30); the section gets a reset for auto-sync and gone contracts, the path keeps **Detect automatically**. **Quiet reads:** a read is no news while every listed capture is present and no gone one is back (`ContractSync.Quiet`), so a waiting mission still waits for the button (D43) and a remove after a tick waits for the next sync with news. **Undo:** a sync stays no step (K2.4), the resync marks again; **a new database clears the undo history** (its snapshots hold the old ids undetached; added while building, ratified at the acceptance). Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Measurements:** Release, median of 5, `SyncTimingTests` on the Hurston plan of 25 Oct (Hull A, four captures), before (f3f9996) → after: a watcher read without news 0.01 → 0.00 ms, the button without news 0.05 → 0.05, two contracts gone 0.07 → 1.22 (now marked: a new scene), two gone and all delivered 0.55 → 0.68; new: remove 0.84, remove all delivered 0.54, a watcher read with two gone before 0.00, two back 0.73. Far under 16 ms (K2.4). Goldens: the `schemaVersion` line only. Tests Core 1,467 → 1,492, App 1,435 → 1,458, Import 141.
- **Counter-argument:** With keep, finished contracts stay until the player deletes them or discards the plan, so the legend grows over a run; K6.5's gate ("hand the contract in, the mission goes") needs remove now. With remove, a gone mission ticked later leaves at the next sync with news or the button, not at once. Exactly the last two contracts handed in within one read count as all at once and stay. A plan with one contract has no guard. A contract back keeps nothing remove took. A new database ends the undo history. Two detached captures with the same old id from two databases share a delivery key. An older app refuses schema 8.
- **Consequence:** D41, D43, D44, D74 and D79 get addenda; `docs/plan-format.md` (schema 8, `gone`, `detached`), `docs/settings-format.md` (`contractTracker.onContractGone`, correction, schema row), README (known issues), wiki (ContractTracker integration, Legend and delivery states, Plan and editor).

## D82 – A failed sync costs neither the plan nor the overlay; five in a row pause it

- **Context:** Up to now the ContractTracker sync caught one failure, a file that is no database (`SqliteException`, T5.4). Anything else left the timer's `async void` handler, or the button's and hotkey's `AsyncRelayCommand`, and reached the dispatcher, where nothing handled it: the overlay vanished mid-flight without a word. There was no log, so a player could report nothing but "it closed". A failure that comes back with every file event could also loop.
- **Options:** the catch only in the timer handler (the brief), in one method that the timer and the command share (chosen, Martin), or none and only a crash dialog; the stack trace in a log file in `%LocalAppData%`, or `Debug.WriteLine` only; the plan kept by a rollback in the catch, or by a `Saved` setter that changes nothing until the scene is built (chosen); a pause that blocks reading, or reuse of the view model's pause for an unreadable plan.json (it blocks applying and ends with the next plan, so another trigger and another end).
- **Decision:** **One catch, one boundary:** `ContractTrackerSync.RunSyncGuardedAsync`, called by the timer's tick (whose whole body, the file state included, runs inside it) and by the button and hotkey. A failure leaves the plan as it was, writes the full exception to **`cargoplan.log`** (`%LocalAppData%\SC-CargoPlan`, moved to `cargoplan.log.1` at 1 MB) and shows "ContractTracker sync failed: Type: message – details in cargoplan.log", once per type and message until a sync succeeds. **Five failures in a row pause the sync** ("Sync paused – check ContractTracker file"): a tick reads again only when the files differ from the state before the failing read, and the button or hotkey always reads; only a success lifts the pause, and every success sets the count to 0. A sync only queued behind a running read counts as neither. The **`Saved` setter** builds the scene first and changes the plan after, so a failure while building leaves plan and scene alone (correction K1 at the approval). **Unhandled exceptions** elsewhere are logged and shown in a dialog (`DispatcherUnhandledException`, `Handled` stays false, the overlay ends as before); those of other threads are only logged (`AppDomain.UnhandledException`). `AppLog` never throws: a log that can't be written goes to `Debug.WriteLine`. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Counter-argument:** A catch of `Exception` also catches real bugs; they now end in a notice line and a log the player has to find, so fewer of them reach an issue than a crash would bring. Five is set, not measured. A failure that changes nothing in the files stays paused until ContractTracker writes or the player syncs by hand. The crash dialog has no owner and may open behind the topmost overlay or the game, as the ships folder message does (D56).
- **Consequence:** D44 gets an addendum; the bug report form asks for `cargoplan.log`.

## D83 – The ship view is one perspective 3D scene

- **Context:** After the holo pump bugfix (D76 addendum) the hologram still breathed while turning, the crates didn't, the GRIN UTV worst. Spike S8.0 (TASKS.md, 30 Oct 2026) compared today's view with a `Viewport3D` in the same transparent window, UTV and Hull A, six steps: orthographic it pumps with a headlight or world-fixed light, with or without a floor; perspective it is calm from the first step on, also with a headlight and no floor, also with an opaque hull. Only the projection matters. Since T7.4c the view has two renderers for one picture: the crates in a 2D painter (`SceneProjector`, `DrawOrder`, `IsoProjection`), the hologram as a `Viewport3D` backdrop behind them, two cameras kept equal by code.
- **Options:** (a) the whole ship view in one `Viewport3D`: crates, hologram, markers and helper geometry in one scene under one camera; (b) the 2D painter with a perspective projection, the hologram still a backdrop with a perspective camera of its own.
- **Decision:** **(a)** (Martin, 30 Oct 2026). One scene, one renderer, one camera: nothing to keep two drawers equal. A depth buffer instead of the painter's algorithm, whose sort by crate centre tips over in perspective (D15's counter-argument grows). The hologram becomes part of the scene, not scenery: crates inside the hull are hidden correctly, racks under the hull lie under the ship. Light, selection edge and depth exist as primitives. **The pieces:** `CameraMath`, a pure class without WPF, from yaw, pitch, distance, target, field of view and viewport size, with `Project(Point3D) → Point` and `Unproject(Point) → Ray`, is **the only projection**: the scene's `PerspectiveCamera` is derived from it, never the other way round, and overlay anchors, hit test, the landing-point ray (T3.14a) and the click points of the gesture tests use it alone; one conformity test holds it against WPF's own mapping (`GeneralTransform3DTo2D`, rack corners and crate centres, several cameras, 0.5 DIP). **Camera:** field of view 40° (a constant, no setting), up (0, 0, 1), pitch ±85°, orbit about the centre of `Ship.Bounds`, pan moves the target in the camera's plane, zoom changes the distance; ship axes (x bow, y port, z up, right-handed) go into WPF 3D unmirrored. **Light:** world-fixed in ship axes, a main light from above and ahead, ambient, and a weak light from below so racks under the hull don't stand flat grey when looked at from below; strengths set on the real element. **Crates:** one `GeometryModel3D` each, inset for the joints (T3.11b), stop colours with hatching (D29), delivered grey, the loaded mark (D79); selection and problem edges as edge material or thin frame boxes, chosen with a picture on the real element. **The free grid** and "all cells" one mesh per rack, never one model per cell. **Hologram:** `PreparedHolo` as a `ModelVisual3D` in the same scene, the last child (WPF doesn't sort transparent models); the outline (D62) a flat surface in the hologram's floor plane (its bottom with `holoOffset`, not z = 0), the wedge (D52) a 3D wedge; fallback hologram → outline → wedge as before. **Text** (rack numbers in screen size, D32; the bow label; tool tips) lies in a 2D overlay above the `Viewport3D`, placed anew on every camera change; a rack number hides when the ray from the camera to its anchor first hits another rack or the hologram's box. **Hit test:** a ray against boxes on the rack and crate data, not `VisualTreeHelper.HitTest`: K1.4's limit and the drop rules (D65) are data, not visuals. **Drag:** D45's polling stays; the preview is a ghost box at the target cell, the same Core check. **No second 2D path:** no `--legacy-2d`; screenshots and behaviour are compared against a publish build of `main`. The old painter stays unused in the repository until T8.6 removes it. Core and the plan, ship, mission and look formats stay as they are. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
- **Measurements (S8.0, Hull C, transparent window, without the game):** frame interval over `CompositionTarget.Rendering`, orbit 12°/s, 30 s: empty baseline 59.0 fps, today's 2D view with hologram 58.6, 144 crates with hologram in perspective 57.2, 4,608 cells as one mesh with hologram 57.9, 4,608 cells as one model each 30.7. Hit test p95 at most 0.64 ms. Work: 14 functions of the 2D view, 1 large (drag preview), 7 medium, 6 small.
- **Formats:** the camera per ship (K1.2, `shipView.cameras`) holds `yaw`, `pitch`, `distance` (cells) and `target` (ship axes), all ship-fixed whatever the cargo, instead of `pxPerCell`, `panX`, `panY`; an entry with `pxPerCell` is dropped on loading (the ship starts at its default camera, no notice); new keys, no schema bump, `settings.json.bak` (D47) keeps the old file. `shipView.maxZoomPxPerCell` stays as key and value and becomes the least distance, the cell measured in the plane through the target square to the view: `d_min = cellDip · viewportHeight / (2 · maxZoomPxPerCell · tan(FOV/2))`; half the fit becomes twice the fit distance. Look files (schema 1), plans (schema 8), ships (schema 5) and missions (schema 3) are unchanged.
- **Counter-argument:** WPF 3D renders in software under `AllowsTransparency` (D2); S8.0 measured in such a window, but never with the game running (T8.7). Transparent models aren't depth-sorted: the hologram goes last and crates are opaque, yet half-transparent crates (`crates.alpha`) over the hologram may show artefacts. Text is clean only in the 2D overlay, so the rack numbers are placed and tested for cover on every camera move and may flicker. Hit test and drop rules move from projected polygons to rays, the place with the highest risk of regressions; `CameraMath` as the only projection, the conformity test and the existing tests as the oracle carry it. Pixels per cell now hold only in the target's plane, so the zoom key no longer says exactly what it measures; saved cameras are lost once. A rebuild this size before `v0.1.0` delays the release and the media of T7.5b have to be taken again. Without a second path in the build there is no way back but the previous release.
- **Consequence:** Phase 8 (T8.0–T8.7, TASKS.md) comes before Phase 9 (the release, with T7.5d as T9.2) and Phase 10 (the former Phase 8 after the launch). D14, D15, D53 and D76 get addenda now; D52, D62 and D63 with T8.1 or T8.5, D32 and D61 with T8.2 or T8.5. `docs/settings-format.md`, the settings tool tips, the README and the wiki are reviewed for the changed terms in T9.1, together with new media. Lesson: a new kind of element on screen means deciding the render architecture again, not fitting the element into the one there is; two renderers for one picture are a stopgap, not a design (TASKS.md lessons, CLAUDE.md A9).
- **Addendum (T8.1): what building the camera showed.**
  - **The field of view is horizontal** in WPF (`PerspectiveCamera.FieldOfView`), so the formula under Formats is wrong: a cell in the target's plane is `W / (2 · d · tan 20°)` DIPs wide, with the view's **width** `W`, and the least distance is `d_min = W / (2 · maxZoomPxPerCell · tan 20°)`. The conformity test turns red with a focal length from the height. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **The limits came with T8.1, not T8.5:** zooming without them goes through the ship, and the holo pump guards are guards of the limits. In: the preference or the reference fit, whichever is nearer; out: twice the reference fit or the whole scene; the reference is every rack at the default orientation (`CameraBasis`, `DistanceLimits`). T8.5 keeps the settings window's text and the presets.
  - **The view hits nothing from T8.1 to T8.3:** the hit test on projected polygons is orthographic, the picture perspective, and a click on a wrong guess would tick or move cargo in a real plan. No test covered the view's own hit test against its picture before; the ray test of T8.3 brings that.
  - **`CameraMath.ApplyTo` sets the `PerspectiveCamera`**, not the control: the conformity test checks the very path the view takes.
  - **Downgrade:** a settings.json with `distance` or `target` is refused whole by every build before Phase 8 (strict options): it starts with defaults and a notice, `settings.json.bak` keeps the file. Accepted, only rc testers are concerned; named in the known issues (T9.1). The old keys `pxPerCell`, `panX`, `panY` stay readable and drop their entry until the next schema bump (backlog).
  - **Frame time (M1, `--measure-frames`, without the game):** Hull C with hologram 53.3, 54.6 and 55.0 fps against 58.8 and 59.5 for the 2D view (M0); the empty window 51–53 in both builds, median 16.67 ms throughout. The gate "≥ 55" is not met as written; Martin accepted it with the measurement in the game in T8.7, since the window's pacing, not the scene, is suspected (not shown).
- **Addendum (T8.2): what the crates showed.**
  - **Two kinds of edges, as in 2D** (decided with pictures against `main`, Martin, 30 Oct 2026): violated crates (red over a dark under-stroke), violated racks, the selected missions' frame and the selection edge with its contour are drawn **on top** in a 2D overlay (a `DrawingVisual` over the `Viewport3D`, projected through `CameraMath`) with the 2D view's pens, so a violation never hides behind cargo and the K4.4 pixel settings keep their meaning. Crate edges, the loaded edge (K6.4), rack edges, the dashed frame round `Ship.Bounds` and the free grid are strips in the scene, one pen wide at the target's plane, and hide behind what lies in front. Neither "edge material" (it colours faces, it draws no edge) nor frame boxes (a width in cells, hidden behind cargo) would have kept that. Against it: a red edge at the back shows through the crates in front, as in 2D. Dropping the frame would be a decision of its own (T8.5).
  - **Rack numbers:** on a compact ship in the middle of the largest visible face that no other rack covers, counting only faces with at least a quarter of the largest one's area on screen, so a sliver seen edge-on takes none; hidden when every such face is covered, or behind the camera. Taken literally ("the anchor's ray first hits another rack"), the rule hid half the Hull A's numbers in its default view, where a lower rack's top and port side are almost equal. **The hologram hides no number:** its box holds every rack, so the ray always hits it. Precision in the planning phase, ratified by approval.
  - **Hatch:** the legend's tile drawing (`StopBrushes.HatchTile`) on the crate faces, the faces carrying texture coordinates in cells, u right and v down as seen from outside, so no hatch shows mirrored. A tile with `ViewportUnits.Absolute` is measured in texture coordinates (a render test holds this, the documentation doesn't say). The tile is 6 DIPs at the target's plane rounded to a power of two cells, so the material changes on a zoom step only, never while turning. It costs about 0.4 ms per frame (C2, 16 stops: 48.0 against 48.4 ms, `StopHatchTimingTests`).
  - **Order:** the crates are half-transparent by default (`crates.fillAlpha` 0xD0) and WPF sorts no transparent triangles, so they are kept from the back to the front by their centres' depth and re-added only when the order changes; the hologram stays last. Known limit: hologram parts behind a half-transparent crate are lost, the crate wrote the depth first; at 0xD0 hardly visible.
  - The hologram's edges were 0.82 px wide since T8.1 (`HoloLines.HalfWidthCells` takes the orthographic stretch); now 1 px at the target's plane. Cargo off the ship is not in the scene at all (D75), so there is nothing to draw.
  - **Frame time** (K1 regime: ms, three runs per target, the median of the runs; "late" = share of frames over 1.5 medians, since a 60 Hz p95 only jumps between 16.9 and 33.1 ms): `hull_c_states` (145 crates, 24 stops, every state, one selected mission) p95 31.97 → 16.95 ms, late 5.2 → 4.2 %; `hull_c` 33.07 → 16.68 ms; the empty window 33.07 ms both times. The gate (p95 ≤ empty + 2 ms) is met; that the full scene comes out faster than before is the window's pacing, not a gain.
- **Addendum (T8.3): what the hit test showed.**
  - **The camera of the picture:** the view keeps the `CameraMath` it last drew with and hits with it alone, so the hit test can't drift from the picture; the first test that clicks the control against its own picture takes its click points from WPF's mapping of that camera, not from `CameraMath`. The rule is D21's, unchanged (D21 addendum).
  - **A hit is geometry, not what shows:** a half-transparent, dimmed or delivered crate covers what lies behind it, whatever `crates.fillAlpha`; the hologram, the outline and the wedge are no scene data and neither answer nor cover, as they cover no rack number (T8.2). A box behind the camera doesn't count; one around it (a large rack, zoomed in and panned) counts from the camera on, which `RayBox.Ahead` gives from the same slab test.
  - **Frame time and hit time (M2, K1 regime):** hit p95 0.002 ms on Hull C (polygon path 0.007 ms), gate ≤ 1 ms met. `hull_c_states` p95 33.07 ms against 16.95 in T8.2 (late 5.9 against 4.2 %): the stop "no worse than before by 2 ms" was missed, as p95 tipped in two of three runs, though T8.3 touches the drawing path by one field. Martin accepted after turning a full Hull C with every state himself, without stutter, at about 110 % display scaling; that it is spread is supposed, not shown.
- **Addendum (T8.4): what the drag showed.**
  - **Ghosts, target rack and refusal on top, in the 2D overlay** (R1, decided at the picture like T8.2's edges): cyan ghosts where Core puts each crate that changes place, red ones at the cells aimed at for a refusal, the target rack's cyan edge, whole cells, filled and edged with the K4.2 pens over the dark under-stroke. A drop target behind cargo stays visible, as in 2D. Against it: a ghost behind crates lies in front of them on screen, without depth.
  - **Fading in the scene:** the crates held and those the outcome moves keep 0.35 of their alpha, fill and edges, a dimmed one further (`CrateMaterials.FadedShare`, R2); only materials and edge strips change, and only for another set. The rules of what shows live in one pure place (`DragPreview`).
  - **Frame time while dragging (M3, K1 regime):** the measuring window drags a crate over a half full Hull C with the real cursor (`SetCursorPos`), so the poll drives it as in the game; p95 33.90 ms before and after (M3a/M3b), late 13.7 → 12.6 %, the empty window 33.07 ms; gate p95 ≤ empty + 2 ms met.
